## 2026-09-06: fix pacman "unknown key" install warnings for the Arch package

1. **Root cause (`tools/arch_package.py`):** the hand-rolled `.PKGINFO` emitted
   `makepkgopt = strip/docs/purge` (3 lines) and `install = .INSTALL` — exactly matching
   the 4 warnings every `pacman -U` printed. `makepkgopt` is a legacy key removed from
   .PKGINFO in pacman 5.0 (2016, superseded by .BUILDINFO); pacman's install-time parser
   (`be_package.c parse_descfile`) never had an `install` key — the scriptlet is
   discovered from the package's `.INSTALL` tar member by name, not by metadata.
2. **Fix:** dropped all four lines; added `PKGINFO_KNOWN_KEYS` (the exact set pacman's
   parser accepts: pkgname, pkgbase, pkgver, pkgdesc, url, builddate, packager, size,
   arch, license, replaces, group, provides, conflict, backup, depend, optdepend,
   makedepend, checkdepend, xdata) and `validate_pkginfo_keys()` which fails the build
   on any key outside it (also rejects a key line without `=`).
3. **Gates:** `verify_arch_package()` now runs the allowlist on every package build;
   `arch_package.run_self_tests()` (validator fixtures incl. the `makepkgopt`/`install`
   regression keys) runs from `security_gates.run_build_script_regression_tests()`, which
   also source-guards that the literals `makepkgopt = ` / `install = .INSTALL` never
   reappear in `tools/arch_package.py`.
4. Deliberately **no** `xdata = pkgtype=pkg` added: it is valid per the PKGINFO v2 spec
   but older pacman (< 6.1) warns about it too; emitting only long-universal keys keeps
   installs warning-free on every pacman generation.
5. Verified: fresh package's `.PKGINFO` contains only allowlisted keys; `--test` and full
   `python build.py` green.

## 2026-09-06: fix MSVDD offset writes rejected by RM driver (wrong domain entry)

1. **Root cause (`source/gpu_backend_xbar.h`):** MSVDD voltage offsets (`xbar_msvdd_offset_uv`,
   ±25 mV etc.) never applied — the driver rejected SET `0xD14B69CF` with status 0xFFFFFFFF.
   The schema placed the µV field in the XBAR-frequency entry (entry 1, `+0x11c` → buffer
   offset 0x544), but that offset belongs to the SYS domain's second voltage rail, which
   Blackwell does not support. The driver's validation requires µV at buffer offset **0x23c =
   entry 0 (0x124) + 0x118**, range-checked to [-500000, 500000] µV; XBAR frequency stays at
   entry 1 `+0x114` (0x53c).
2. **Fix:** `XBAR_MSVDD_OFFSET_FIELD` 0x11c → 0x118; new `XBAR_PINNED_MSVDD_ENTRY_INDEX = 0`
   and `XbarClkDomainsSchema::msvddEntryIndex` (schema tracks frequency and voltage entries
   independently); `xbar_layout_for_buffer()` computes `msvddFieldOffset` from
   `msvddEntryIndex`; `XbarControlSnapshot`/`XbarBufferLayout` carry `msvddDomainIndex`.
3. **Diagnostics:** probe and write logs now print resolved `freqEntry`/`freqOff` and
   `msvddEntry`/`msvddOff` so future offset-field mismatches are visible in
   `greencurve_debug.txt` without re-deriving addresses.
4. **Live hardware verification (RTX 5070, elevated diag using the audited
   `xbar_write`):** GET layout resolves `msvddEntry=0 msvddOff=0x23C`; SET
   +25000 µV accepted with exact readback, confirmed by a fresh GET, then
   restored to the prior state (net-zero). Non-elevated SET returns
   **-137 `NVAPI_INVALID_USER_PRIVILEGE`** (privilege gate before RM dispatch;
   GET works unprivileged) — the old wrong offset from an elevated caller got
   the RM-level generic `0xFFFFFFFF` instead, so the status change is itself
   evidence the field is now in the validated set.
5. **Tests/gates:** regression tests place MSVDD at entry 0 (assertions 4505/4521, new 4525
   for `msvddDomainIndex`, schema-pinned check 4517); `tools/xbar_gates.py` pins
   `XBAR_PINNED_MSVDD_ENTRY_INDEX = 0`, `msvddEntryIndex`, and the explicit decode of
   the -137 privilege status (`F-XBAR-MSVDD-ENTRY`).
   `python build.py --test`, full `python build.py` (6 targets), `--self-test FULL` green.
5. **Stale-risk note:** this pin is Blackwell V2-schema-specific (version word 0x000261A4);
   future generations must pin their own frequency/voltage entry indices from real evidence,
   never copy these numbers.

## 2026-09-06: fix XBAR dialog "Measured now" row truncation under multi-sensor reporting

1. **Root Cause & Geometry Analysis (`source/xbar_dialog.cpp`):**
   - The 4-domain measured row (`Measured now: XBAR %s | SYS %s | VIDEO %s | MSVDD %s`) contains 73 characters when all clocks and MSVDD voltage are actively reporting.
   - At standard 125% DPI scale (9pt / 12px Segoe UI base), the string requires 549 physical pixels.
   - With previous `clientW = dp(460)` (535 px available content width between 16 dp margins), the 14 px overflow truncated ` 0.90V` right after `MSVDD `.
2. **Layout & Sync Fix:**
   - Expanded `clientW` from `dp(460)` to `dp(540)` (508 dp / 635 px available content width at 125% DPI), providing 86 px of safe margin across all supported DPI scales (100% to 200%).
   - Pinned `clientW = dp(540)` in `tools/xbar_gates.py` (`F-XBAR-DIALOG`).
   - Unified `xbar_dialog_sync_controls()` to directly call `xbar_dialog_update_live_values()`, eliminating initial 1-second format divergence and dead code.
3. **Regression Tests:**
   - Added test units 4907-4909 in `tests/regression_main.cpp` verifying logical content width >= 500 dp, maximum measured row length = 73 chars, and >= 70 physical pixels of headroom across 100%, 125%, 150%, and 200% DPI scales.

## 2026-09-06: real active XBAR voltage telemetry and display on Windows and Linux (protocol v25)

1. **Active XBAR MSVDD Voltage Measurement (`source/gpu_backend_xbar.h`):**
   - Implemented private NvAPI query interface ID `0x465F9BCFu` (`NVAPI_GPU_CLIENT_VOLT_RAILS_GET_STATUS`) using request structure `NvApiVoltRailsStatus` (size 76 bytes, version word `0x0001004C`).
   - Reads physical measured MSVDD rail voltage from offset 40 (`value_uV`) in microvolts dynamically across GPU load and P-states.
   - Added `xbar_measure_voltage(voltFunc, gpuHandle, &measuredUv)` helper returning boolean status and zeroing on error or invalid payload.

2. **Protocol v25 & Wire Layout (`source/service_protocol.h`, `source/main_state_sync.cpp`, `source/linux_daemon_snapshot_runtime.cpp`):**
   - Added `unsigned int xbarMeasuredVoltageUv;` to `ServiceSnapshot` directly following `xbarMeasuredClockKhz`.
   - Tail padding on `ServiceSnapshot` (due to 8-byte aligned `gc_u64` fields) absorbed the 4-byte scalar, keeping `sizeof(ServiceSnapshot) == 4248` and `sizeof(ServiceResponse) == 7112`.
   - Bumped `SERVICE_PROTOCOL_VERSION` from 24 to 25 to prevent older peers from misaligning downstream fields (`sysClkSupported`, `videoClk...`, `health`).
   - Synced in `populate_service_snapshot_locked` / `adopt_service_snapshot_locked` and Linux daemon snapshot runtime.

3. **Linux TUI Advanced Tab (`source/linux_tui_layout_fan_profiles.cpp`):**
   - In `tui_draw_advanced_tab()` (`TUI_FIELD_XBAR_MSVDD`):
     - Formats active voltage alongside applied offset as `"readback %+d mV • now %.2fV"` (e.g. `readback +0 mV • now 0.81V`) when measured voltage is available, and falls back to `"readback %+d mV • now ---"` when readback is valid but voltage is unmeasured.

4. **Windows GUI Advanced Dialog (`source/xbar_dialog.cpp`):**
   - Added `hMsvddNowLabel` to `XbarDialogState` for the MSVDD row, formatted dynamically as `"%.2fV (%+d mV)"` (e.g. `0.81V (+0 mV)`).
   - Updated `hMeasuredLabel` to include MSVDD: `"Measured now: XBAR %s | SYS %s | VIDEO %s | MSVDD %s"`.
   - Adjusted `clientW` from `dp(420)` to `dp(460)` to cleanly display all four measured metrics without caption clipping.

5. **Regression Tests and Source Gates (`tests/regression_main.cpp`, `tools/xbar_gates.py`, `build.py`, `tools/linux_gates.py`):**
   - Added test unit (codes 4890-4906) asserting `NvApiVoltRailsStatus` size 76 / offset 40, `xbar_measure_voltage` success/error/null paths, and `x.xxV` formatting.
   - Pinned `NVAPI_GPU_CLIENT_VOLT_RAILS_GET_STATUS` and `hMsvddNowLabel` in `tools/xbar_gates.py` (`F-XBAR-VOLT`, `F-XBAR-LIVE`).
   - Verified clean compilation and tests on both Windows and Linux targets.

## 2026-09-05: Arch Linux package enhancements: auto group enrollment, XDG icon hierarchy, daemon lifecycle hooks

1. **Automatic User Group Enrollment (`packaging/arch/greencurve.install`):**
   - Added `_setup_group_enrollment()` in `greencurve.install`: detects the non-root calling user via `$SUDO_USER`, `$GREENCURVE_USER`, or `logname` (UID >= 1000).
   - Automatically executes `usermod -aG greencurve "$candidate"` and alerts the user about next login / `newgrp`.
   - Safely falls back to displaying manual command instructions if no interactive non-root user is resolved.

2. **XDG Desktop Application Icon Hierarchy (`tools/icon_render.py`, `tools/arch_package.py`, `packaging/arch/`):**
   - Added `Icon=greencurve` to `packaging/arch/greencurve.desktop` and `tools/greencurve-setup.sh`.
   - Added deterministic PNG rendering and hicolor directory exporter in `tools/icon_render.py` (`write_png()`, `export_hicolor_icons()`).
   - Packaged full hicolor application icon tree (`16x16`, `32x32`, `48x48`, `64x64`, `128x128`, `256x256`) and universal fallback pixmap (`usr/share/pixmaps/greencurve.png`) directly into `greencurve-*.pkg.tar.zst`.
   - Rendered and tracked 256x256 icon `packaging/arch/greencurve.png` with binary attribute in `.gitattributes` and exception in `.gitignore`.
   - Updated `PKGBUILD` and `PKGBUILD.bin` to source and install the icon assets.

3. **Systemd Lifecycle & Sleep/Resume Hooks (`packaging/arch/greencurve.install`):**
   - `pre_remove`: cleanly stops and disables `greencurve.service` and `greencurve-resume.service` before package file removal, avoiding orphaned background daemon processes.
   - `post_remove`: issues `systemctl daemon-reload`.
   - `post_upgrade`: automatically executes `systemctl try-restart greencurve.service` if the daemon was active before upgrading.
   - `post_install`: explicitly informs the user to enable both `greencurve.service` and `greencurve-resume.service` (for sleep/resume VF curve restoration), and highlights the installed desktop entry.

## 2026-09-05: Linux PATH command symlink, XDG user config policy, and Arch Linux PKGBUILD packaging

1. **Linux PATH command symlink (`tools/greencurve-setup.sh`, `source/linux_service_install.cpp`):**
   - After using the Linux install script (`tools/greencurve-setup.sh install`) or `--service-install`, a symlink `/usr/local/bin/greencurve -> /usr/local/libexec/greencurve/greencurve` is created.
   - Running `greencurve` from any terminal path or directory now immediately launches the TUI client.
   - `greencurve-setup.sh uninstall` cleanly unlinks `/usr/local/bin/greencurve`.
   - `greencurve-setup.sh status` reports the status and destination of `/usr/local/bin/greencurve`.
   - Setup script and installer prefer `/usr/local/bin/greencurve` for desktop launcher `Exec` entries rather than transient extraction directory paths.

2. **Non-Root User Configuration Policy (`source/linux_config_path_policy.h`, `source/linux_port.cpp`):**
   - Previously, default Linux config path was resolved relative to `/proc/self/exe`. For system-installed binaries (`/usr/local/libexec/...` or `/usr/bin/...`), that directory is root-owned (`0755`), preventing unprivileged users from saving profiles, custom settings, or writing debug logs.
   - Introduced pure policy `linux_resolve_default_config_path()` in `source/linux_config_path_policy.h`.
   - If `config.ini` exists beside the binary in a non-system directory (i.e. not in `/usr`, `/bin`, `/sbin`, `/opt`), portable mode is preserved beside the binary.
   - Otherwise, for system installs or non-system directories without a local `config.ini`, configuration defaults to `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/greencurve/config.ini` or `$HOME/.config/greencurve/config.ini`.
   - `linux_port.cpp` creates `~/.config/greencurve/` with secure `0700` permissions on initial resolution.

3. **Arch Linux Packaging & Automated Package Build (`tools/arch_package.py`, `packaging/arch/`):**
   - Added `tools/arch_package.py` and integrated into `build.py` Linux release packaging.
   - Running `python build.py` by default on either Windows or Linux produces pacman-installable Arch Linux packages (`greencurve-<version>-1-<arch>.pkg.tar.zst`) for all built Linux architectures.
   - Assembles full Arch package filesystem layout, permissions (0755 dirs/binary, 0644 config/units), `.PKGINFO`, `.BUILDINFO`, `.INSTALL`, and gzip-compressed `.MTREE`.
   - Fully compatible with direct `sudo pacman -U` installation.
   - Added full Arch Linux packaging files under `packaging/arch/`:
     - `PKGBUILD`: Builds `greencurve` from source using `python build.py --target linux --arch "$_gc_arch" --no-package` and packages `/usr/bin/greencurve`.
     - `PKGBUILD.bin`: Prebuilt binary package using official release tarballs with exact sha256 checksums.
     - `greencurve.service`: Systemd service unit running `/usr/bin/greencurve --daemon` with sandboxing matching internal installer.
     - `greencurve-resume.service`: Sleep/suspend resume unit running `/usr/bin/greencurve --resume-restore`.
     - `greencurve.sysusers`: Declarative group registration for `systemd-sysusers`: `g greencurve -`.
     - `greencurve.desktop`: Desktop launcher with `Terminal=true` and `Exec=greencurve --tui --from-desktop`.
     - `greencurve.install`: Post-install message advising `usermod -aG greencurve $USER` and `systemctl enable --now greencurve.service`.
     - `README.md`: Instructions for building with `makepkg -si`.
   - Configured `.gitattributes` to pin `packaging/** text eol=lf`.

4. **Build System & Toolchain Flexibility (`build.py`):**
   - Updated `build.py` to allow overriding `ZIG_DIR` and `ZIG_EXE` via environment variables (`os.environ.get(...)`), enabling Arch Linux or custom build environments to provide system/custom Zig without hash mismatches against pinned versions.
   - Kept exact line count of `build.py` at 5340 lines (`BUILD_SCRIPT_SIZE_RATCHET`).

5. **Regression Test Coverage & Source Gates (`tests/regression_main.cpp`, `tools/linux_gates.py`):**
   - Added unit test suite for `linux_config_path_policy.h` covering system directory classification, portable mode, XDG/HOME resolution, trailing slashes, and buffer overflows (exit codes 4860-4882).
   - Added source gates in `tools/linux_gates.py` verifying setup script command paths, installer symlink constants, policy delegation, Arch Linux packaging files, and LF line endings.

## 2026-09-05: updater trust, pipe failure paths, and atomic service write pinning hardened (commit 9a60dc4)

Audit follow-up addressing cryptographic trust, concurrency lifetime hazards, and filesystem pinning:

1. **Overlapped Pipe I/O Lifetime Safety (`main_service_connection.cpp`, `main_service_pipe_listener.cpp`, `service_pipe_prefix_read.h`, `windows_pipe_regression.cpp`):**
   `CancelIoEx()` requests asynchronous cancellation but does not wait for driver completion. Because `OVERLAPPED` and event structures were stack-allocated, exiting on timeout/wait failure allowed the stack frame to be popped while driver IRP completion routines still held references to the stack addresses, causing kernel wild writes into invalidated stack memory upon deferred completion. Fixed by joining every cancellation with `GetOverlappedResult(pipe, &ov, &cancelled, TRUE)` before stack storage leaves scope. Stored `waitError = GetLastError()` before `CancelIoEx` to avoid clobbering wait error codes. Updated test fixture with `fixture_cancel_and_join()`.
2. **Fail-Closed WinHTTP Security Policy (`main_service_update_fetch.cpp`):**
   Unchecked return values from `WinHttpSetTimeouts()`, `WinHttpSetOption(WINHTTP_OPTION_SECURE_PROTOCOLS)`, and `WinHttpSetOption(WINHTTP_OPTION_DISABLE_FEATURE)` allowed the privileged `LocalSystem` updater to silently continue under insecure TLS versions or chase unvalidated redirects if policy calls failed. Fixed by failing closed (logging error, closing handles, returning false). Added `WINHTTP_DISABLE_COOKIES` to ensure the updater remains strictly stateless.
3. **Atomic Service File Writer Directory & Temp Pinning (`main_secure_write.cpp`):**
   `write_text_file_atomic_service_scoped()` opened `parentHandle` and temp file `h` with `FILE_SHARE_DELETE`, allowing concurrent processes to rename or delete the parent directory or staging temp file during the atomic write/verify transaction. Removed `FILE_SHARE_DELETE` (`FILE_SHARE_READ | FILE_SHARE_WRITE`), locking the directory and temporary file against rename or deletion TOCTOU races while open.
4. **Canonical Base64 Signature Decoding (`main_service_update_verify.cpp`):**
   Replaced greedy line-ending loop with strict single-ending parsing (permitting exactly one `\r\n` or `\n`, rejecting lone `\r` and multiple line endings via alphabet validation). Enforced zero unused pad bits for `=` (`quad[2] & 0x03 == 0`) and `==` (`quad[1] & 0x0F == 0`) per RFC 4648 §3.5, eliminating signature malleability.
5. **Signing Tool Low-S Policy Parity (`tools/update_signing.py`):**
   Updated `update_signing.py:verify` to enforce `1 <= s <= N // 2` (matching the client verifier's `GC_UPDATE_P256_LOW_S_CEILING` policy) and added a regression test for high-S rejection in `run_self_tests()`.

## 2026-09-05: comprehensive security code review audit — PII log redaction, fail-closed service atomic writes, and reparse-point traversal defenses hardened

Full-repository security audit across the entire codebase ("turn every stone"). Triaged and hardened all security boundaries, verified toolchains, static analyzers, PE headers (ASLR/DEP/CFG/CET), memory safety, IPC permissions, update verification, and logging. Implemented four key security improvements:

1. **Finding 1 (Medium, fixed) — Unredacted User SID in Service Data Paths Debug Log (`source/main_data_paths.cpp`):**
   `resolve_service_user_data_paths()` logged raw Windows account SIDs in plain text (`oldSid=%s newSid=%s`) when the active session identity changed, violating the log privacy/redaction policy that all other service lifecycle logs adhere to. Fixed by tokenizing both `g_serviceUserPathsSid` and `userSid` with `gc_log_identifier_token()` into non-reversible, stable correlation tokens (`[id #xxxxxxxx]`).
2. **Finding 2 (Medium, fixed) — Insecure Un-Scoped Relative Fallback for Service Error Log and Atomic Writes (`source/main_diagnostics.cpp`, `source/main_secure_write.cpp`):**
   `error_log_path()` previously returned `"greencurve_error.log"` (relative path) when `g_errorLogPath` was empty (e.g. before user logon in `greencurve-service.exe`). In `write_text_file_atomic()`, service writes without resolved user paths previously bypassed `write_text_file_atomic_service()` and fell through to an un-scoped write, creating temporary files in the service CWD (System32). Fixed by introducing `service_early_error_log_path()` and `effective_error_log_path()` backed by the admin-only LocalAppData machine data dir (`resolve_service_machine_data_dir()`), adding `GC_SERVICE_WRITE_MACHINE_DATA` scope in `app_shared.h`, and enforcing fail-closed scoping in `write_text_file_atomic()` for any un-scoped service write.
3. **Finding 3 (Low/Medium, fixed) — TOCTOU Window and Reparse Point Traversal in Service Atomic Writes (`source/main_secure_write.cpp`, `source/main_service_request_policy.cpp`):**
   `write_text_file_atomic_service_scoped()` previously closed its parent directory handle immediately after querying its path, leaving an unlink/swap race window before `gc_MoveFileExUtf8()`, and omitted checking `FILE_ATTRIBUTE_REPARSE_POINT` on `parentHandle`. Fixed by keeping `parentHandle` open and pinned via `ScopedHandle` across the entire write, flush, and move transaction, checking `FILE_ATTRIBUTE_REPARSE_POINT` on the parent directory handle, validating ancestor reparse points with `verify_parent_no_reparse_point()`, and adding `GC_SERVICE_WRITE_MACHINE_DATA` support to `service_verify_written_file_path_scoped()`.
4. **Finding 4 (Low, fixed) — Reparse Point Traversal on Target Directory in Directory Creation (`source/main_data_paths.cpp`):**
   `ensure_directory_recursive_windows()` checked `FILE_ATTRIBUTE_REPARSE_POINT` for intermediate path segments, but when the final target directory already existed (`ERROR_ALREADY_EXISTS`), it omitted validating the terminal directory's attributes. Fixed by checking `(attrs & FILE_ATTRIBUTE_DIRECTORY) != 0` and `(attrs & FILE_ATTRIBUTE_REPARSE_POINT) != 0`, refusing to traverse or write through symlinks/junctions.
5. **Regression Test Coverage (`tests/regression_main.cpp`):**
   Added regression test units (4850-4855) verifying `GcServiceWriteScope` definitions and ensuring SID string inputs to `gc_log_identifier_token()` produce deterministic, collision-free `[id #...]` tokens with no raw SID or RID substrings leaked.

## 2026-09-05: comprehensive codebase review audit — equality semantics, memory comparisons, CLI side effects, and static analysis hardened

Full-repository review audit across the entire codebase following the hardened audit rubric. Fixed 1 High bug, 3 Medium issues, and 35 baselined static-analysis warnings across all subsystems without degrading performance, breaking APIs, or dropping unsupported GPU support:

1. **F-01-001 (High, fixed) — Equality check omitted 8 advanced clock fields (`source/desired_settings_ui_policy.h`):**
   `desired_settings_equal()` compared basic VF offsets and fan states but completely omitted XBAR offset (`hasXbarOffsetKhz`, `xbarOffsetKhz`), MSVDD voltage offset (`hasXbarMsvddOffsetUv`, `xbarMsvddOffsetUv`), SYS clock offset (`hasSysClkOffsetKhz`, `sysClkOffsetKhz`), and VIDEO clock offset (`hasVideoClkOffsetKhz`, `videoClkOffsetKhz`). This caused dirty-tracking in the Linux TUI, config profile sync, and startup sync to falsely report equality when advanced clock overrides differed. Regression test assertions added to `tests/regression_main.cpp` (4810-4818).
2. **F-03-001 (Medium, fixed) — Suspicious `memcmp` over padded structures (`config_profile_sync_cache.cpp`, `main_service_lifecycle_apply.cpp`, `main_service_persist.cpp`):**
   `memcmp` was used to compare `DesiredSettings`, `GpuAdapterInfo`, and `ConfiguredGpuSelection`. Structures with alignment padding contain uninitialized/indeterminate bytes, which causes false inequality. Replaced with `desired_settings_equal()`, `gpu_adapter_info_equal()`, and `configured_gpu_selection_equal()` in `source/gpu_selection_policy.h`. Regression test assertions added (4820-4827).
3. **F-03-002 (Medium, fixed) — Tainted/unbounded `malloc` in Linux supplementary groups (`source/linux_daemon_transport.cpp`):**
   `getgroups()` result was passed directly to `malloc` without upper bound. Introduced stack fast-path (`stackGroups[64]`) avoiding heap allocation for typical group counts, plus explicit upper bound (`count <= 1024`).
4. **F-02-001 (Medium, fixed) — Unhandled exception escape from `main()` (`source/linux_main.cpp`):**
   Wrapped `linux_main_impl()` in a top-level `try ... catch (const std::exception&)` / `catch (...)` barrier in `main()`, preventing unhandled C++ exception escapes. Compacted report generation blocks to keep source size at 799 lines (within the 800-line ratchet limit).
5. **F-07-001 (Low, fixed) — Side effects in CLI parsing conditions (`source/linux_cli_options.cpp`):**
   Replaced 13 instances of `!argument_requires_value(argc, i) || !parse_int_strict(argv[++i], ...)` by checking argument presence before incrementing `i++`.
6. **F-07-002 (Low, fixed) — Unchecked `sscanf` in PCI BDF parsing (`main_runtime_nvml.cpp`, `linux_port_profiles.cpp`, `linux_backend_discovery.cpp`):**
   Introduced `parse_pci_bdf_string()` with strict bounds and delimiter verification to replace `sscanf("%x:%x:%x.%x")`. Added regression tests (4830-4841).
7. **F-07-003 (Low, fixed) — Unchecked `sscanf` in SGR 1006 mouse parser (`source/linux_tui_render.cpp`):**
   Replaced `sscanf` with strict `strtol` parsing and validation.
8. **F-07-004 (Low, fixed) — Missing switch default cases, dead stores, redundant checks, duplicate branches:**
   - Added `default: break;` in `auto_profile_dialog.cpp`, `fan_curve_dialog.cpp`, `main_runtime_ui.cpp`, and `main_shell.cpp`.
   - Removed dead stores in `gpu_backend_apply.cpp`, `config_profiles_ui.cpp`, `main_runtime_nvml.cpp`, and `main_service_controlled_restart.cpp`.
   - Added deleted copy constructors and copy assignment in `ServiceClientIdentity` (`source/main_service_pipe.cpp`) to protect RAII token handle.
   - Combined redundant conditional branches in `main_data_paths.cpp`, `main_fan_runtime.cpp`, and `main_service_logon_coordinator.cpp`.
   - Cast widening conversions in file size checks and thread stack sizes (`main_secure_write.cpp`, `main_service_fan_worker.cpp`, `main_startup_task_definition.cpp`).
   - Cleaned up 35 baselined `clang-tidy` warnings across 29 translation units.

## 2026-08-29: security audit (full repo) — one finding fixed: update-state envelope was stamped for unauthorized pipe callers

Full-repository security audit against the condensed cross-language template
(manual review of every trust boundary: pipe transport/identity/ACL, secure
write, updater fetch/verify/install chain, manifest cache, installer
payload/apply, Linux daemon transport/socket/install, systemd unit, config
parsing, hotkeys, admin helpers, log redaction; plus build, regression suite,
fuzz targets, tidy ratchet, CET/PE gates, supply-chain digest pinning, and an
embedded-strings scan of built binaries). Everything else held; the audit
found exactly one gap worth fixing:

**F-SEC-4 (Low, fixed):** `service_execute_checked_request()` in
`main_service_pipe.cpp` stamped `service_update_populate_response()`
UNCONDITIONALLY, so a caller that failed the active-session/PID/medium-
integrity gates still received the machine's update posture (available
version, staged/verified flags, install phase) — contradicting the invariant
printed right above it. Fix: the stamp moved inside the
`stateEnvelopeAuthorized` gate beside `populate_service_state_response()`,
with a debug log when the envelope is withheld. GUI/CLI callers that pass the
gates are unaffected; only refused/low-integrity callers lose the envelope.
Pinned by `check_update_envelope_is_authorized_only()` in
`tools/update_gates.py` (single-occurrence + positional check; verified to
reject the pre-fix shape) and the updated
`if (stateEnvelopeAuthorized) {` shape gate in `build.py`. Wiki:
windows-architecture.md (F-SEC-3 bullet), updates.md (protocol-v19 section),
testing.md (updater source gates).

Notable strengths confirmed during the audit (no action): updater's
verify-before-parse + low-S enforcement + TOCTOU-closed hash-then-launch
chain; hand-validated allowlisted redirects with no Content-Length trust;
pipe impersonation RAII + admission table; machine-config/staging DACLs;
archive/asset name refusals; Linux socket proof + unit sandboxing with
documented omissions; digest-pinned toolchain downloads with cached-binary
re-verification.

## 2026-08-29: fixed the two CI failures (and a latent LSP corruption) left by the 2026-08-28 toolchain commit

CI run 33260128824 (push of the toolchain commit; the two intermediate commits
never got individual runs) failed exactly two jobs:

1. **release-packaging** — MinGW link of the x64 uninstaller stub failed with
   `undefined symbol: gc_invoke_fatal_dump_hook`. Root cause: the MSVC-ABI
   split moved the fatal-dump hook into the toolchain-neutral
   `process_hardening.cpp` but `INSTALLER_SOURCE_NAMES` still listed only
   `ssp_glue.cpp`/`cfg_glue.cpp`. Fix: link `process_hardening.cpp` into the
   stubs (matching that file's own "linked into every binary, including the
   setup program" contract) + a `check_all` gate on the list. Falsified
   locally: with the fix stashed, the x64 MinGW packaging run reproduces the
   exact CI error.
2. **windows / UBSan step** — named-pipe fixture case 1: `client exit=907,
   server exit=0`. Root cause: a latent fixture race (the fixture and its
   headers were untouched by the commit; compiled code was identical to the
   last green run — the commit only perturbed scheduling). The server
   disconnected immediately after writing the response, and
   `DisconnectNamedPipe` discards unread buffered data, so a client
   descheduled between its completed write and its `ReadFile` got
   `ERROR_BROKEN_PIPE`. Fix: event-driven response-consumed ordering
   (bounded server wait, client signals on both terminal paths) plus
   client-side read/pong verification, which the fixture previously lacked
   (a completed-but-failed overlapped read passed silently). Diagnostics now
   print concrete `GetLastError` codes. Validation caught a second facet
   immediately: the stalled case's pending read ENDS via broken-pipe by
   design, so strict pong verification is gated on `!ctx->stall`.
3. **Bonus (found while validating, pre-existing on the toolchain commit):**
   `_zig_arm64_windows_command`/`_mingw_x64_windows_command` used
   `*"-DGREEN_CURVE_SERVICE_BINARY=1"`, exploding the define into
   single-character args. That corrupted the generated LSP database so
   clang-tidy failed silently on main.cpp (execution failure, exit 1, no
   diagnostic — would have broken CI's "Static analysis ratchet" next) and
   would have broken the legacy `--jobs 1` llvm-mingw x64 service build
   (verified that branch builds now). Self-test in
   `run_build_script_regression_tests` rejects exploded args / misplaced
   service defines; the fixture ordering is pinned in
   `check_ipc_transport_and_probe_gates`.

All six windows-job CI steps now pass locally (`--test`, `--test --asan`,
`--check --target all`, `--tidy` with zero execution failures, `--fuzz
--fuzz-runs 5000`, `--target windows`), plus the full build, the llvm-mingw
windows matrix with packaging, and the serial service build. Commit
de137ec.

---

## 2026-08-28: MSVC-ABI (clang-cl) toolchain adopted as the default for Windows targets on native Windows

Implemented after the assessment below: `python build.py` (no args) on a
native Windows host now builds windows-x64 AND windows-arm64 with
clang-cl + lld-link (/GS, real OS CFG via /guard:cf, /cetcompat shadow stacks
on x64, CFG metadata + PAC/BTI on arm64, native PDBs for both arches).
`--toolchain {auto,clang-cl,llvm-mingw}` selects explicitly; auto falls back
loudly to llvm-mingw when no verified MSVC-ABI installation exists, and the
llvm-mingw path reproduced the legacy artifacts byte-for-byte when forced.
Linux targets and Linux-host Windows cross-builds stay on llvm-mingw/Zig.

Implementation shape: `tools/msvc_toolchain.py` (discovery incl. a fail-closed
dual-arch compile+link probe, cl-style hardened flag builders — clang-cl
rejects GNU-style flags under -WX and has no `-Oz`, so `-O1` is the size
floor — and scheduler-integrated object/link helpers with self-tests);
`tools/pe_verify.py` (PE hardening gates + RSDS sanitization moved out of
build.py for the size ratchet, extended: x64+clang-cl requires the CET_COMPAT
debug entry, arm64+clang-cl requires nonempty GFIDS);
`source/process_hardening.cpp` split out of `cfg_glue.cpp` (fatal-dump hook +
`initialize_process_mitigations()` are toolchain-neutral; the MinGW CFG shim
stays MinGW-only). `WINDOWS_SERVICE_LINK_LIBS` now names `-luser32` — the
MinGW driver appends it implicitly and lld-link does not. Installer stubs
gain the same toolchain; `CLSID_TaskScheduler`/`IID_ITaskService` are
DEFINE_GUID-instantiated under `_MSC_VER` because the SDK's taskschd.h only
declares them (MinGW's libuuid carries them). `verify_windows_private_symbols`
dispatches on the PDB file magic, not the arch. Sources fixed for the MSVC
ABI along the way: `GcThemeFonts.small`→`small_text`, two `small` locals in
installer_ui.cpp (rpcndr.h `#define small char`).

Verified: full six-target `python build.py` + packaging + both setup
executables; `--toolchain llvm-mingw --check` byte-identical fallback;
`--check-cet`, bounded fuzzing, `--tidy` (no new findings), full regression
suite, x64 `--self-test` FULL under CFG+CETCOMPAT. Open questions: arm64
MSVC-ABI binaries have never run on real ARM64 hardware; CI's windows runner
may lack standalone LLVM (auto falls back loudly, so CI stays green but may
stop exercising the new default; add the LLVM/ClangCL component if coverage
is wanted). Sizes: windows-x64 GUI 897,536 B vs MinGW 834,048 B; setup stub
noticeably larger than the MinGW one (static MSVC CRT).

## 2026-08-28: clang-cl / MSVC-ABI hardening assessment — verdict recorded; real OOB found and fixed in `--self-test`

User question: would a clang-cl (MSVC-ABI) toolchain give better CFG and
security/performance flags than the pinned llvm-mingw + Zig setup? Full
empirical assessment run on the dev machine (VS 2026 18.9 + MSVC 14.44/14.51
toolsets + SDK 10.0.26100 + standalone LLVM 22.1.8 all installed). Verdict:
technically feasible and genuinely stronger on two axes, but it breaks two
design pillars (hermetic toolchain, Linux-host release builds) — decision
left to the owner; see `llm-wiki/build.md` "MSVC-ABI toolchain assessment"
for the full matrix. Nothing in the build was switched.

**Real bug found by the trial (fixed):** `main_self_test.cpp`
`self_test_clk_domain_survey()` sized `measuredKhz[16]` / `measuredValid[16]`
while the fill and read loops range over `CLK_PROBE_MAX_DOMAINS` (=32): a
silent stack overflow on every `--self-test` run. The MinGW `-Oz` layout
absorbed it into dead frame slots; the clang-cl `/O2` layout put the overrun
into unmapped stack and the self-test crashed (access violation in the survey
loop, found via cdb). Fixed by sizing both arrays from the constant; source
gate added in `tools/security_gates.py::check_diagnostic_probe_gates`
(requires the `CLK_PROBE_MAX_DOMAINS` sizing, forbids `measuredKhz[16]`).
This is exactly the class of bug `-ftrivial-auto-var-init`/SSP do not catch.

**Second MSVC-ABI incompatibility fixed:** MSVC's `rpcndr.h`
`#define small char` collides with a local named `small` in
`tests/regression_main.cpp` (tooltip test); renamed to `smallBuf`.

Assessment highlights (details in build.md): real kernel-enforced CFG works
with the NVAPI/NVML `GetProcAddress` pattern (negative control fast-fails
`0xC0000409`); `lld-link /cetcompat` enables strict hardware shadow stacks
(verified via `GetProcessMitigationPolicy`) and loads the non-CET-COMPAT
`nvapi64.dll` fine (`BlockNonCetBinaries` defaults off); full compile is
clean under `-W4 -WX`, regression suite and `--self-test` pass; sizes ~+6%
(`/O1` 884 KB vs MinGW `-Oz`+LTO 834 KB; clang-cl rejects `-Oz`); perf a
wash (±20% per microbench, opposite directions). XFG and `/Qspectre` are
cl.exe-only (clang-cl rejects). `-fstack-protector-strong` is silently
ignored by clang-cl. ARM64 clang-cl accepts `-mbranch-protection=standard`
(PAC/BTI emitted), but end-to-end ARM64 lld-link (misaligned-ldr/str history)
was not re-tested.

## 2026-08-28: Native-Windows matrix build failed on a poisoned Zig cache — link lock + repair

The full `python build.py` matrix on the native Windows host failed all four
Zig-driven links (windows-arm64 GUI/service, linux-x64, linux-arm64) with
`ld.lld: error: cannot open .../zig-global-cache/o/<hash>/libcompiler_rt.a` /
`lld-link: error: could not open '.../compiler_rt.lib': No such file or
directory`, while both windows-x64 clang targets succeeded. A single,
non-concurrent retry reproduced the identical error instantly: the shared
Zig global cache was left with manifests whose `o/<hash>` artifact directories
were absent, and pinned Zig 0.13.0 never rebuilds a manifest hit whose file is
missing, so the state was persistent. Upstream context: a residual
Windows-specific cache race acknowledged in ziglang/zig#14815 (post-#15641),
cache-race regressions as late as 0.14.0 (#23110), and "partial pruning of the
cache is unsupported" (#18763). CI never sees this because its earlier
`--check --target all` step warms the cache before the release-build step, and
the release workflow runs on Linux.

Empirical findings (all on the pinned 0.13.0): plain `zig c++ -c` compiles
never write the global cache (only links create CRT artifacts there); links
write nothing to the local cache; `h/` manifest file names are NOT derivable
from `o/` directory names (0/10 pairs match), so a poisoned manifest cannot be
surgically located from outside Zig; deleting a whole `o/<hash>` dir while its
manifest survives self-heals on the next link (treated as a miss), while the
surviving-manifest/missing-artifact state observed here does not. The exact
upstream mechanism that first poisoned this host is unproven (stale-risk:
concurrent-link race vs. an interrupted or partially pruned cache). A curious
side effect: after the wipe-and-rebuild, the compiler_rt digest for the probe
flags changed (06230a92… orphaned, 240ef965… now used) — the digest inputs
must include something environmental that the original poisoned state had
distorted; unreproduced since.

Fix (`tools/zig_cache.py`, new one-way-dependency module):

1. **Link serialization** — every Zig link invocation holds an exclusive
   byte-range lock (`LockFileEx` on Windows, `flock` on POSIX; per-handle, so
   it excludes other threads of the same process too; blocking acquire, no
   polling) on `build-tmp/zig-link-cache.lock`, which must stay OUTSIDE every
   wipeable cache root or a repair could delete the lock out from under
   waiting acquirers. Compiles stay parallel. Wired via `build.py`'s
   `_run_zig_link` (arm64 Windows/Linux + linux-x64 links + the `--jobs 1`
   serial fallback + the Linux fixture link), the arm64 installer link in
   `installer_build.py`, and the fuzz fixture link in `security_gates.py`.
   Windows named mutexes were rejected: they are re-entrant per thread, which
   silently breaks same-process exclusion tests.
2. **Poisoned-cache repair** — when a link fails on a diagnostic naming an
   artifact inside a cache root whose file is verifiably absent, the affected
   root is removed and the link retried exactly once, loudly, under the same
   lock (the only remedy; the manifest cannot be targeted). A failure that
   does not match the signature, or whose named file still exists, propagates
   unchanged — no masking.
3. **Compile-path diagnosis** — a poisoned *local* cache (compiles cache
   their objects there) cannot be wiped safely while sibling compiles run, so
   `_run_compiler` reports it as an explicit actionable error instead.

Deterministic self-tests (parser fixtures from the real error lines, lock
exclusivity via non-blocking acquires, wipe/refusal rules, retry-once with an
injected fake runner) run in `run_build_script_regression_tests` with source
guards requiring the wrapper at every Zig link site. `BUILD_SCRIPT_SIZE_RATCHET`
raised 5321 → 5340 for the split of `_run_compiler` into
`_audit_compiler_output` + `_run_zig_link`. Verified: the wrapper healed the
real poisoned cache end-to-end (poisoned → wiped → retried → rc 0, artifact
rebuilt, direct zig probes healthy afterwards), `python build.py --test`, and
the full six-target `python build.py` with packaging.

## 2026-08-25: Windows CI signer self-test crash fixed

The 0.24.0 push CI's Windows job appeared to pass its first `--test` and fail
the later `--test --sanitizer`, but the raw log contained no output from either
test process. The first failure was hidden because PowerShell ran `--test` and
then `--check` in one workflow step and reported the successful final native
command. A rerun reproduced the standalone exit.

The root cause was `_current_user_sid()` in `tools/update_signing.py`: Windows'
first `GetTokenInformation(TokenUser)` call returned the required size of the
complete variable-length `TOKEN_USER` plus SID, but the second call passed a
fixed `TokenUser` ctypes object while claiming that larger size. That native
buffer overflow corrupted the Python process; whether it survived depended on
the interpreter/heap layout, so local Python 3.14 happened to pass while the
Actions Python 3.12 process terminated before buffered output flushed. The
query now allocates the exact returned byte count, casts only after the API
succeeds, and rejects an impossibly short result. A Windows 1,000-query stress
run passes.

The first instrumented CI run then exposed the second defect cleanly:
`icacls /inheritance:r /grant:r` removes inherited ACEs and replaces grants
for the named SID, but does not remove unrelated ACEs that are already
explicit. The Actions temp volume therefore remained broader than owner-only.
`tools/windows_key_acl.py` now builds a fresh one-ACE ACL, installs it as a
protected DACL with `SetNamedSecurityInfoW`, and verifies the owner, protection
bit, exact ACE count/type/mask/SID, and returned security descriptor through
correctly sized native calls. The locale-sensitive icacls parser and two
incomplete duplicate implementations were removed. The real owner-only key
round trip passes on Windows. The next hosted run exposed one runner-specific
detail: an elevated token can create a file whose default owner is its token
owner group rather than its user SID. The hardener therefore sets both the
owner and protected DACL to the current token user's SID explicitly instead of
assuming file creation established that owner.

CI now runs UBSan tests and the target-matrix check as separate fail-fast
steps, removes the redundant `--test --sanitizer` invocation (`--test` already
enables UBSan), and sets `PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1`. Build-script self-tests forbid
the undersized buffer spelling, the masked command sequence, and the duplicate
UBSan job.

Once those phases passed, the separately visible Windows ASan stage exposed a
race in `tests/windows_pipe_regression.cpp`: its hostile bytes were described
as a tail on the request message but were actually sent through seven separate
`WriteFile` calls. Message-mode pipes made each call a new message, so the
server could legally drain what existed, respond, and disconnect while the
client was still issuing later writes; ASan scheduling made the client return
906 and main collapse that to 702. The fixture now assembles the request and
tail in one byte buffer and submits one `WriteFile`, matching the production
condition it claims to test. Thread joins also report both wait results and
exit codes so future hosted failures identify the failing side directly.

That ASan fix passed on hosted Windows. The newly reachable Windows
clang-tidy phase then reported two pre-existing `atoi` calls in the opt-in
ClkDomains write probe. This was not merely analyzer churn: malformed text
silently selected entry zero, which is unsafe for a diagnostic that applies a
temporary offset. `source/clk_probe_entry_policy.h` now owns strict ASCII
decimal/range parsing; invalid, signed, trailing-junk, and out-of-domain input
fails closed with a diagnostic. Regression codes 4800-4808 exercise the parser
on both hosts, and a source gate forbids `atoi` from returning to the probe.
Final CI run `32790490127` passed all Windows, Linux, and independent release-
packaging jobs, including both sanitizer stages, both analyzer ratchets,
fuzzing, target matrices, and verified package builds.

The public 0.24.0 ref is a lightweight tag pointing to an unsigned commit, so
GitHub cannot show its green signature-verification badge. Artifact provenance
attestations remain valid and are a separate control. A Verified tag requires
an annotated GPG/SSH/S/MIME-signed tag whose public signing key GitHub can
associate with the maintainer. Replacing the already-public tag is deliberately
not automatic.

The public 0.23.1 tag and HEAD have byte-identical manifest parsing, version

The same validation found `gpu_core.h` at 844 lines against its 842-line
ratchet: the zero-RPM gap added three adjacent numeric macros without updating

Release-preparation validation passed as build 119: `python build.py --test`,

## 2026-08-24: independent zero-RPM fan-off hysteresis (protocol v24)

The initial native zero-RPM implementation reused `FanCurveConfig.hysteresisC`
for two different jobs: small ordinary curve target downshifts and the much
wider anti-cycle band needed to avoid annoying fan starts/stops. These are now
independent. `hysteresisC` remains 0-10C and affects only ordinary curve
downshifts; the new one-byte `zeroRpmHysteresisC` is a 2-30C fan-off gap with a
5C default. The first enabled point remains the explicit rising/ON threshold,
so a separate rising-hysteresis knob would be redundant. Example: first point
30C, zero-RPM gap 12C, curve downshift 1C means ON at 30C, OFF at 18C, with
ordinary speed reductions still reacting after 1C.

One v23 reserved byte was assigned to the new field, so fixed wire sizes remain
1424/188/836/4248/7112 but the protocol moved to v24 to prevent old peers from
misreading it. Windows/Linux profiles persist `zero_rpm_hysteresis_c`; enabled
v23 profiles without it inherit their prior shared hysteresis (minimum 2C) and
log the compatibility migration. Linux JSON and the field-masked CLI expose
the value; `--fan-zero-rpm-hysteresis` changes it without disturbing points or
ordinary hysteresis.

The Windows dialog labels the old control **Curve downshift**, adds a themed
**Zero-RPM fan-off gap** combo, and shows actual ON/OFF thresholds in two
explicit non-wrapping lines. Its minimum client height is 580dp, the readout has
48dp, and all new controls use the existing font/input/label palettes. This
replaces the clipped/overlapping three-line help. The Linux TUI independently
exposes **Curve downshift** and **Off gap** and retains its computed OFF/ON
summary. Main-window/TUI configuration summaries say **zero-RPM enabled** and
**fan stop/start**, avoiding the misleading `zero-RPM off` wording that looked
like an unchecked setting.

Build 118 passed clang-tidy with no new findings and the complete
Windows/Linux x64/ARM64 build/package matrix. The preceding build also passed
`python build.py --test`, ASan, 20k `service_request` fuzz iterations, and
packaged Linux save/load plus v23 migration and invalid-value preservation
checks. Live fan-stop behavior remains firmware-dependent and was not tested on
hardware.

## 2026-08-24: native zero-RPM fan curves on Windows and Linux (protocol v23)

A user needed stock-style idle fan stop on a GPU whose manual duty range starts
at 30%, while retaining a custom curve above a configurable temperature.
Writing manual 0% is not portable and fights `nvmlDeviceGetMinMaxFanSpeed`.
The proper cross-platform mechanism is therefore a policy handoff:
`nvmlDeviceSetDefaultFanSpeed_v2` restores NVIDIA automatic control below the
threshold, letting firmware use its native fan stop, while
`nvmlDeviceSetFanSpeed_v2` resumes the custom curve above it.

`FanCurveConfig.zeroRpmEnabled` is an opt-in profile/wire field. The first
enabled curve point is fan ON; fan OFF is ON minus hysteresis, with a minimum
2C Schmitt gap. Thus a default 30C first point/2C hysteresis starts manual at
30C, remains manual through 29C after starting, and returns to driver auto at
28C. In-band auto remains auto, avoiding repeated starts when temperature
hovers near the threshold. Manual speeds continue to clamp to the VBIOS/NVML
minimum; the user's temperature points do not.

Both Windows and Linux consume `fan_runtime_next_action()`. Windows has a
focused `main_fan_zero_rpm.cpp` shard; Linux supports automatic control in the
initial transaction and periodic worker. Both verify/reassert the policy,
retain curve intent while live policy is automatic, use the existing failure
ladder, and never issue manual 0%. Temperature-aware intent readback accepts
either policy inside the Schmitt band and reports missing temperature as
unavailable.

The same audit fixed a related Linux state bug: a new Apply woke the worker but
did not clear the previous curve's hysteresis memory, so its next poll could
overwrite the transaction's new target with an old percentage or auto/manual
sub-state. Runtime state is now keyed to `g_fanWakeGeneration` and reseeded from
the hardware policy established by each new Apply.

Windows GUI and Linux TUI expose Native zero-RPM; both explain/show the computed
ON/OFF temperatures. Windows/Linux INI profiles persist strict
`zero_rpm_enabled=0|1`; Linux adds `--fan-zero-rpm 0|1` and JSON output. Linux
CLI fan edits carry a per-field mask, so toggling zero-RPM preserves a saved
custom curve; a conflicting point override is rejected transactionally instead
of silently resetting the curve. The IPC bump to v23 pins sizes
1424/188/836/4248/7112. New compiled cases 4720-4750 and
`tools/fan_gates.py` cover state transitions, readback, TUI visibility,
canonicalization/equality, platform API paths, persistence, and the no-manual-0
invariant. `python build.py --test`, the ASan suite, and a packaged Linux profile
round-trip pass; build 116 passed all Windows/Linux x64/ARM64 binaries,
hardening/symbol checks, installers, and archives. A live board test is still
needed because actual fan stop remains firmware-dependent.

The initial Windows prose label wrapped beyond its 40px box on some font/DPI
combinations. It is now a compact explicit three-line label with no automatic
wrapping, a 60px reserved region, and an 8px design-unit gap to the buttons.
The checkbox remains on the shared owner-drawn theme path; the description uses
the dialog's shared font and `COL_LABEL`/`COL_BG` static-control palette.

## 2026-08-24: recent-commit review closes Linux Advanced/TUI and live-telemetry gaps

Review of the previous eight days found no credential material in tracked
content, but found several correctness gaps in the new advanced-clock series.
Linux carried the portable XBAR/SYS/VIDEO profile fields without implementing
their NvAPI transaction or exposing them in the TUI. Linux now uses the shared
version-keyed full-block reader/writer with exact readback, rollback snapshots,
capability publication, durable sparse-intent merging, override detection, and
a dedicated Advanced TUI tab. Reset still works on GPUs with an unknown
optional ClkDomains schema and includes the advanced domains when proven.

The review also found that the Windows public VIDEO-clock helper was defined
but never called, so the advertised live value stayed zero; telemetry now
invokes it. Generic SYS/VIDEO field access now resolves its offset from the
response schema rather than schema-table row zero, preserving future schema
extensibility. Tracked material stays free of person names and third-party
tuning-tool names.

A final failure-path pass found two more Linux edge cases. Baseline/explicit
Reset now selects optional advanced phases from `availableMutationDomains`,
not from readable snapshot validity, so a readable-but-unwritable ClkDomains
surface cannot break core Reset or rollback. The Advanced TUI no longer
fabricates `+0` readback for unsupported domains: unavailable online rows are
truthfully labelled and non-interactive, while offline portable-profile
editing remains available.

Validation on the Linux host: normal and ASan regression suites pass (including
the cryptographic signing cross-check), clang-tidy 22 reports no findings
outside the 37-entry baseline, Linux x64/ARM64 warning-as-error checks pass, and
the full Windows/Linux x64/ARM64 build, hardening/symbol/private-path checks,

remain hardware-unverified on Linux.

## 2026-08-25: VIDEO clock SOLVED by differential dump - entry 4 pinned (protocol v22)

The live test used an external actuator plus our `--clk-domain-dump` as sensor
and identified the video domain in one shot: stock vs +400 MHz dumps
differed by exactly ONE dword, 0xE48 = 00061A80 (+400000 kHz) = ClkDomains
ENTRY 4 + the standard 0x114 freq-offset field; known XBAR/SYS fields
untouched; independent telemetry confirmed the physical clock. Key discovery: the video
engine has NO CLK_MEASURE id (0..31 all checked) - which is why write-probing
never saw it move; exact readback is the only in-transaction proof.

VIDEO promoted from configurable experiment to pinned domain:
XBAR_PINNED_VIDEO_ENTRY_INDEX=4; clk_video_binding.h and the [debug]
video_clk_entry knob removed; dialog label de-experimentalized; gates
re-pinned. Protocol stays v22.

New tool: `--clk-domain-dump` (read-only) writes the whole non-zero control
block + measure table to %LOCALAPPDATA%\Green Curve\clkdomains_dump.txt.
Differential dumps against ANY external tool's writes are now the standard
identification method - far safer than blind field probing.

User observation kept as known behavior: video clock runs ~400 MHz higher
when a curve-UV flattening is active - matches another controller, driver V/F
arbitration, not our bug.

## 2026-08-24 (later): SYS clock offset shipped as protocol v21; video offset not reachable (commit 328cbd1)

`--clk-domain-probe` (opt-in, elevated, +50 MHz write/readback/measure/restore
per entry) identified the ClkDomains entry mapping on RTX 5070 / 610.88:

- entry 0 -> measure 0 = GPC/core; entry 1 -> measure 1 = XBAR owner;
  **entry 3 -> measure 2 = second aux clock, shipped as "SYS clock offset"**.
- Entries 2/4/5/6/7: no measurable clock response under render or NVDEC load.
- CLK_MEASURE ids beyond 15 exist: 20 (~2.24 GHz), 21 (~1.9-2.4 GHz, DVFS-jumpy),
  22 (~108 MHz), 31 (constant 4). None moved from any entry write.
- VIDEO offset is NOT reachable on this board/driver: no entry/measurable-id
  pair responded even with confirmed NVDEC activity (dmon dec=5-7%). Honest
  status; revisit with future drivers or GB202 reports.

SYS shipped end-to-end as protocol v21 (mutation bit 8; sizes
176/824/4224/1416/7048; capability packed mask 0x3FFFF; capability index 8
"sys-clk"; Advanced-dialog third row; portable key `sys_clk_offset_khz`;
apply/reset/telemetry/capture/profiles wired; tests 4530-4533 + gates).
Live self-test reports sys-clk available. End-to-end service apply still
needs an installed rebuilt service (pre-existing gap).

Gotchas hit: UAC-elevated children inherit neither process nor user-scope env
changes reliably -> marker file `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Green Curve\clk_probe_entry.txt`;
`python build.py --test` does not link the GUI binary (a broken TU passed --test
once) - always run a full `python build.py` after header surgery; pstates20 V1
entry layout still unpinned (survey prints raw words only).

## 2026-08-24: video/SYS clock offsets — evidence, survey, identification probe (commit 24cd558)

User asked for video-clock offset OC support (RTX 5090, driver 610.88), where
Core/Memory/XBAR/**SYS**/**Video** clock offsets plus MSVDD clock ratio
(= PropRels U16.16 ratio, current 0.8999 = 0xE660), NVVDD/MSVDD voltage
ranges and Voltage Boost are adjustable. Aux-domain ranges are ±1000 MHz,
matching our driver behavior.

Established on the local RTX 5070 (driver 610.88) via a new read-only
`--self-test` domain survey:

- `ClockClientClkDomainsGetInfo` = **0x64B43A6A**, V1 word `0x00010928`,
  response 0x928: header count=3; rows stride `0x48`, shape
  `[id, pad×7, maxKHz, minKHz, extra, pad×7]`. Rows found: id 0 → ±1000000
  (GPU), id 4 → +3000000/−1000000 (memory), third row all-zero. **GetInfo does
  NOT enumerate aux domains** even though XBAR SetControl works — its list is
  the documented subset only.
- CLK_MEASURE ids seen on this board: 0=GPC, 2=XBAR, 4=MCLK confirmed;
  1 (~1.94 GHz), 3 (~539 MHz), 6 (~1.29 GHz) unidentified candidates.
- Control-block entries 0..7 structurally identical; all zero except entry 1
  carrying the live XBAR offset. Read-only evidence cannot map entries →
  domains.
- PSTATE20 (0x6FF81213, VER_1 `0x00011C94`) reports numPstates=5,
  numClocks=2 (graphics+memory) — no video clock there either.

Conclusion: SYS/VIDEO offset support hinges solely on the ClkDomains entry
index mapping. Added opt-in `--clk-domain-probe`: writes +50 MHz into each
candidate entry's freq-offset field via the production transaction (fresh
preimage, single dword, exact readback), measures all physical domains
before/during/after, restores each original value; refusals/no-stick entries
logged harmlessly; user's applied XBAR offset (entry 1) untouched by design.
Not yet executed — needs elevated prompt and user go-ahead.

Implementation notes: public clock ids PROCESSOR(7)/VIDEO(8) kept local to
main_self_test.cpp (gpu_core.h size ratchet); self-test CLI dispatch factored
into `self_test_cli_dispatch()` (build.py gate updated to pin new wiring);
several ~78 KB `XbarControlSnapshot` locals must stay `static` (stack).

## 2026-08-23 (latest): XBAR ClkDomains reworked to version-keyed schema dispatch (commit ad9e45c)

Review feedback on the XBAR feature: `NvAPI_GPU_ClockClkDomainsSetControl`
is 10+ years old; its `.domains` sub-structure is versioned per generation;
XBAR has been independently controllable since Volta; fixed-offset
interpretations are "Blackwell only by accident". The fix must check the
driver-REPORTED domain version (GetInfo) or type (GetControl response) and
select a different implementation per version.

Implemented:

- `source/gpu_backend_xbar.h`: pinned `g_xbarSchemas[]` table keyed by the
  response version word `(version<<16)|size`; request ladder newest-first;
  absolute field offsets in the snapshot; tri-state `schemaStatus`
  (OK / UNKNOWN_VERSION / UNAVAILABLE); unknown words log decoded words + a
  bounded head dump and refuse everything. Exactly one validated row exists
  today: V2 `0x000261A4` (base 0x124, stride 0x304, fields 0x114/0x11c).
  (Superseded 2026-09-06: the µV field moved to entry 0 `+0x118`; only the
  frequency field is `0x114` at entry 1 — see the newest entry.)
- Probe family gate removed (`gpu_capability_xbar_probe.h`); stale proof
  scalars cleared per probe; UNKNOWN_VERSION → UNPROBED so pre-schema GPUs do
  not get a spurious limited-surface warning, known-schema refusals stay
  REFUSED.
- Tests 4500–4524 extended; gates (`tools/xbar_gates.py`) pin the table design,
  family-blindness, and forbid fixed-offset geometry recomputation.
- Verified: build clean, pure tests + ASan pass, live RTX 5070 read-only
  `--self-test` reports xbar available with version word 0x000261A4 logged.

Open / stale-risk:

- **No pre-Blackwell row exists yet.** Volta..Ada owners get fail-closed with
  actionable diagnostics until someone reports a real GET_CONTROL response
  (the log line is designed to be reported verbatim). The suggested
  `NvAPI_GPU_ClockClkDomainsGetInfo` path was NOT implemented: its Windows
  interface ID and response layout are not publicly established, while the
  GetControl response word already carries the same version evidence.
- End-to-end hardware apply from an installed rebuilt service still pending
  (pre-existing gap).
- Test-code trap hit: several `XbarControlSnapshot` locals (~78 KB each, the
  0x13000 buffer) overflow the regression harness stack — keep them `static`.

## 2026-08-23 (latest): code-review fixes — three silent defects in 0.23.1..73717c0

Full review of the 22 local commits since tag 0.23.1 found three real
defects that were invisible in the happy path (which is how they escaped
live validation, the pure tests, and every existing gate). All fixed and
committed as ebb54ec:

1. **Admission throttle was inert.** `service_admission_decide()` and
   `service_admission_charge()` each declared their own function-local
   `static ServiceIpcAdmissionTable` — decisions read a table no charge ever
   touched, so rate limiting could never refuse anything. Fixed with one
   shared accessor (`gc_pipe_dispatch::service_admission_table()`); a source
   gate now forbids the per-function declaration shape. Lesson: pure-policy
   tests share one table by construction, so they cannot catch glue-level
   table splits; only a source gate can.
2. **Per-connection token-handle leak.** The worker-pool refactor dropped
   the historical post-dispatch `CloseHandle(callerToken)`; capture runs
   before magic/admission checks, so EVERY served connection (refusals
   included) leaked a duplicated SYSTEM token handle. Fixed with a
   `ServiceClientIdentity` destructor; gated.
3. **Capability probe result discarded.** b544f7d deleted both
   `g_app.gpuCapability = probe;` assignments while replacing the ID
   scanner; snapshots then carried a zeroed never-probed projection forever
   (reduced-surface warning dead, memory topology stuck UNKNOWN, packed
   mask claiming all domains available). Because UNPROBED means "available",
   nothing user-visible broke — which is why it went unnoticed. Both exits
   republish; gated via require_text_count == 2.

Also: charging is now exactly-once per connection through the pure
`service_ipc_connection_cost_tokens()` outcome table (refused connections
no longer harvest a SUCCESS token from their own refusal; mismatch paid
twice before; the anon bucket's comment now says it meters rather than
enforces); the pipe fixture is event-driven (no Sleeps) and asserts
healthy-beats-stall by ordering against the stalled probe's own deadline;
xbar dialog RegisterClassExA guarded once-per-process + removed a no-op
line; duplicate listener comment merged; UTF-8 BOMs stripped from the 20
files that gained them during the XBAR series; BUILD_SCRIPT_SIZE_RATCHET
5320→5321 for the single delegation call line left in build.py.

Validation: --test/--tidy/--fuzz/--check-cet all pass on Windows host.
The throttle fix means rejection paths are NOW reachable in production for
the first time — watch service logs after deploy for unexpected
"admission refused" lines (GUI polls cost 1/20s refill and cannot trip it;
a broken client looping at high rate will).

---

## 2026-08-23 (later): debug-log size-cap rotation

Both greencurve_debug.txt copies grew unbounded (user-side 352 MB; service-side
spanning April-August). Every debug_log append now checks size against a 32 MiB
cap (`debug_log_rotation_policy.h`, tests 4700+) and truncates in place with a
marker line when reached. Truncation instead of rename is the load-bearing
choice: GUI + service hold append-only handles on the SAME user-side file
(FILE_SHARE_WRITE), so renames would sharing-violate, while FILE_APPEND_DATA
writes always land at EOF -- a cooperative truncate moves EOF for every writer
with zero handle churn. Legacy oversized files self-heal on the first write.
Source gates pin the policy include and check-before-append ordering inside
debug_log. Committed as 73717c0 (also swept in the user's one-line .gitignore
addition).

## 2026-08-23: transition-safe IPC throttling implemented (worker pool + probe-first identity)

Implemented the transition-safe plan (details omitted) with deliberate deviations.
The single serialized pipe thread is gone: a fixed pool of six worker threads
(`main_service_pipe_listener.cpp`, one instance each) serves connections while
command execution stays serialized under `g_serviceDispatchLock` inside the
extracted `service_execute_checked_request()` (switch moved verbatim to
`main_service_pipe_switch.cpp`). Response writes happen OUTSIDE that lock, so
a client that stops reading no longer stalls hardware commands -- a bonus over
both the old design and the plan's queue sketch.

Connection pipeline: 12-byte header probe (message-mode partial read =
mandatory first read) -> brief impersonation -> throttle key from
`ServiceLifecycleIdentity` (SID string + LUID + session) -> pure admission
decision (`service_ipc_throttle_policy.h`: per-identity buckets burst
80/refill 20 per s, costs success=1/bad-command=5/fault=10, independent
handoff reserve burst 8, 128-slot table with idle/LRU eviction, anonymous
budget for unidentifiable faults) -> exact body read -> dispatch. Wrong
magic/version now drains via a PeekNamedPipe-gated bounded drainer and still
answers mismatch instead of silently timing out 2s.

Deviations from the plan: bounded worker pool instead of a WaitForMultipleObjects
overlapped state machine (reuses proven synchronous deadline+CancelIoEx I/O,
far less OVERLAPPED lifetime risk); a dispatch critical section instead of a
dispatcher queue+thread; NO austerity mode (bounded contexts already cap the
damage; complexity not justified); wake/recycle listener events removed
entirely (their ACL-rebuild purpose died with the pre-read experiment).

The new native fixture (`tests/windows_pipe_regression.cpp`, run by `--test`
on win32) immediately caught a real defect: the first drain helper blocked a
full deadline on exactly-one-message clients; it is now Peek-gated. Pure tests
4600+ cover the policy; source gates were rewritten for the new shape
(ImpersonateNamedPipeClient only in runtime_identity capture, broad AU ACL
pinned, FIRST-instance claim count=1, CancelIoEx in probe/drain, dispatch-lock
ordering). Stale baseline entry for main_service_host widening deleted after
the multiplication moved into a ULL constant.

Validation: full build x6 targets, --test/--tidy/--fuzz/--check-cet pass;
installed on the reporting machine the same night. Live GUI log shows clean
~3s telemetry polling (success=1/error=none), one transient broken probe
during the install restart recovering in ~6s, and a CLI APPLY (+475 MHz
selective offset verified) through the new path at 04:40. Service-side log
reviewed elevated 2026-08-23: zero real failure-pattern hits since install
(only false-positive 1368 substring matches inside runtimeLastApply numbers).
NOT yet exercised: fast-user-switching live test, scheduled logon handoff on
a real login, service restart while GUI polls.
Side findings: BOTH greencurve_debug.txt logs (user-side 352 MB, service-side
spanning April-August) grow unbounded -- rotation/dedup candidate.

## 2026-08-22: live IPC regression correction — no identity before first read

Installing the audit build made every GUI request fail. The service log showed
`pre-auth identity rejected ... error 1368` (`ERROR_CANNOT_IMPERSONATE`) every
two seconds, while the GUI saw broken-pipe/read failures.

Root cause: `ImpersonateNamedPipeClient` was moved before the server's first
read of the named-pipe message. That API requires the pipe read to happen
first; the old post-read authorization path was correct on this point. The
active-session SDDL design also remained suspect for scheduled-logon races.

Correction: removed both pre-read identity rejection and active-session SID
ACL construction. Restore the proven
`D:(A;;GA;;;SY)(A;;GA;;;BA)(A;;GRGW;;;AU)` ACL and complete-request/post-read
session/PID/integrity authorization. Removed the corresponding pure tests and
source gate so they cannot preserve the unsafe ordering. Keep workflow,
runtime-mitigation, Linux INI/log, and auto-profile privacy fixes. Windows x64
regression/check passed after correction; install/restart and live GUI IPC still
need confirmation on the reporting machine.

## 2026-08-22: audit hardening — pipe exposure, workflow gates, runtime policy

The security-audit follow-up fixed the malformed Linux analysis-tools step that
made all of `ci.yml` unloadable, pinned CI to `contents: read`, and added a
narrow workflow-structure self-test (including a negative control for the exact
file-scope step indentation).

*(The pipe ACL/pre-auth half below was superseded immediately afterward; see the
newer correction.)*

Windows processes now prohibit dynamic code and extension-point DLLs in addition
to System32-only runtime DLL search and strict handles. Linux INI loading is
bounded by size, section count, and per-section entries; debug logs are forced
owner-only even when reopening legacy files; auto-profile diagnostics fingerprint
foreground executable/class names instead of logging them verbatim; and the
Linux setup wrapper quotes/escapes desktop executable paths. Source gates pin
each change. Regression/ASan, tidy, bounded fuzzing, CET, toolchain verification,
and Windows/Linux x64 checks pass. Live multi-user pipe switching and NVIDIA
hardware journeys remain runtime follow-ups.

## 2026-08-22: audit hardening — wire v20, XBAR proof/schema, privacy

A full audit found that the XBAR series had grown fixed-size IPC structures while
`SERVICE_PROTOCOL_VERSION` stayed at 19. Compiling origin versus HEAD showed
`ServiceRequest` grow 1392→1408 bytes, `ControlState` 152→168,
`ServiceSnapshot` 4192→4216, and `ServiceResponse` 6952→7016. Both transports
would therefore pass a mixed v19 header handshake and then disagree on body
length. The protocol is now v20; exact sizes are static asserts, regression code
4521 pins them, and the prefix suite explicitly proves the immediately previous
version is refused.

XBAR clock/MSVDD now have independent readback-valid fields instead of deriving
voltage freshness from startup probe success or folding both into one bool.
Control adoption no longer nests XBAR inside fan presence. The private
ClkDomains response no longer uses repeated-marker "discovery": the validated
base/stride/domain count are pinned and unknown/decoy layouts fail closed
(tests 4500–4509).

Also: default logs now fingerprint paths/account/SID/LUID identifiers;
duplicate-launch activation waits on an explicit window-ready edge rather than a
50 ms poll loop; Linux profile writes preserve omitted XBAR ownership; CI runs
on main/pull requests with clang-tidy 18 pinned; version moved to 0.24.0 for the
incompatible development line; updater failed-check/staged recovery has a pure
wired policy and tests. Regression/ASan-UBSan, six-target release/package build,
fuzzing, and tidy pass; the rebuilt Windows x64 binary also passed its read-only
`--self-test` on the local RTX 5070. Privileged XBAR writes, installer/uninstall,
updater delivery, and Linux hardware were not exercised.

## 2026-08-21: GUI-loaded sparse profiles must declare XBAR stock

The first fallback projected a missing XBAR key as the current hardware value.
That avoided one stale-draft display bug but broke the normal Load→Apply flow:
loading slot 2 (no XBAR) while hardware held slot 3's +400 MHz captured +400 as
"unchanged," so Apply preserved it instead of restoring stock. Tray/hotkey paths
sent the raw sparse record and cleaned up correctly, but the editor path did not.

A loaded record is now the editor declaration: omitted XBAR clock/MSVDD fields
project as 0 and are marked profile-sourced. Thus slot2 shows a pending 0 while
hardware holds +400, Apply writes stock, and transition/identity handling agrees.
Slot 3 saves verified at `xbar_offset_khz=400000` in the live INI after Apply +
Save. Source gate now pins the zero-declaration rule.

---
## 2026-08-21: closed the XBAR profile ownership gaps

The first working hardware path treated XBAR as GUI-only state in several record
paths. Audit/findings now fixed:

- **Ownership:** standalone XBAR requests were not considered owned intent, and
  XBAR-only could be misclassified as fan-only. Active/profile identity ignored
  both fields, so a slot could appear applied while its XBAR values differed.
- **Transitions:** switching from a profile owning XBAR to one omitting it left
  the old clock/voltage request live. Transition cleanup is now per-field.
- **Persistence:** Windows always emitted both INI keys as zero, even for sparse/
  unsupported cases. Supported saves now emit effective values; unsupported saves
  emit no XBAR ownership. Old profiles without the keys project live values into
  the editor instead of retaining the previous dialog draft. Legacy/global
  controls gained the same fields.
- **Portability:** shared profiles from a Blackwell board are stripped of XBAR
  ownership when applied on a GPU without that surface; other domains still
  apply.
- **Merging/projection:** Windows and Linux sparse merges carry both fields;
  build-full-live captures them; load-replace confirmation compares them (and
  lock, an adjacent omission).
- **Proof:** service telemetry tracks a separate readback-valid bit rather than
  claiming freshness merely because the probe once succeeded.

Compiled lifecycle regressions cover per-field transition cleanup/preservation.
New source gates pin parsing, fallback-free zero fabrication, ownership compare,
merges, portable stripping, and truthful readback. Tests and x64 check pass; full
six-target rebuild follows.

---
## 2026-08-21: pending XBAR reached Apply, then the old no-change gate rejected it

The pending diff knew about `GUI_PENDING_XBAR`, and capture emitted both owned
fields, but `capture_gui_apply_settings()`'s unchanged predicate still compared
only GPU/memory/power/curve/lock/fan. With an otherwise clean profile, a +100 or
-200 MHz XBAR edit therefore enabled Apply and then hit the final all-unchanged
branch: "No changes to apply". The logs confirmed desired=`100000/-200000 kHz`
while current was stock.

Manual apply now computes `xbarUnchanged` from both requested fields versus the
service control state (falling back to live service state only when control is
absent) and includes it in both the fan-only shortcut and no-change refusal.
Diagnostics log applied/desired frequency, MSVDD, and the verdict.
`tools/xbar_gates.py` pins the comparison in both shortcut predicates.

---
## 2026-08-21: Advanced dialog bottom truncation was an outer/client size mix-up

The XBAR Advanced dialog laid out buttons in client coordinates but passed the
same 320 dp value as the outer `CreateWindowExA` height. The caption and borders
therefore reduced the actual client area and clipped the bottom row. It now
converts the desired client rect through the shared DPI-aware
`adjusted_window_size_for_client()` helper, centers the resulting outer size, and
uses only `clientW/clientH` for children. Source gate `F-XBAR-DIALOG` pins the
conversion and forbids passing raw client bounds to window creation.

---
## 2026-08-21: XBAR was reading the wrong private ABI — fixed with ClkDomains V2

The same-day "proper themed dialog" entry below described wiring, but its
backend was wrong: it treated Linux RM `CLK_DOMAINS` IDs as Windows NvAPI IDs and
stored a **PropRels** response as the XBAR snapshot. PropRels owns the GPC→XBAR
propagation ratio, not ClkDomains frequency/MSVDD offsets. The result was exactly
the reported symptom: no current offset, no measured clock, and no effective
write. The capability probe also scanned ~20k unrelated interface IDs twice and
called arbitrary private entry points with guessed buffers.

The corrected Windows surface is ClkDomains V2: GET `0xF58938F5`,
SET `0xD14B69CF`, version `0x000261A4`; measure `0x527FC458`, version
`0x0001000C`. Layout discovery validates the response and finds the repeated
entries (locally `0x124/0x304`) before using offset fields `+0x114/+0x11c`.
(Superseded 2026-09-06: µV is `+0x118` at entry 0, not `+0x11c` at entry 1.)
Writes always use a fresh full-block GET, modify only the two owned fields, and
require exact readback. Explicit Reset now clears both fields too.

Also fixed review findings from the first series: removed the direct-GUI NvAPI
fallback, duplicate profile INI keys, one-sided capture that could reset the
sibling XBAR field, silent skips when XBAR was requested but unavailable, and the
duplicated scanner blocks. Service telemetry now re-reads XBAR state and physical
clock rather than retaining startup values. Regression codes 4500–4507 cover the
layout/write/readback transaction; source gates pin the ABI and forbid the old ID
confusion. Live RTX 5070 read/probe succeeded; privileged hardware SET still needs
end-to-end verification after installing the rebuilt service.

---
## 2026-08-21: Advanced XBAR clock offset (Blackwell) — proper themed dialog

The "Advanced" button next to Updates (already placed via `ui_main_layout.cpp`''s
right-anchored row: `XBAR (100dp) + Updates (84dp) + License (118dp)`) was a
rough PropRels ratio hack: unthemed popup, direct NVAPI `0xCBFF71D0` calls from
the GUI thread, bypassing the service and the pending model. Replaced with a
proper service-owned XBAR domain:

- **Data model:** `DesiredSettings.hasXbarOffsetKhz/xbarOffsetKhz` (kHz) +
  `hasXbarMsvddOffsetUv/xbarMsvddOffsetUv` (µV) already existed in
  `gpu_core.h`/`service_protocol.h` and `gpu_backend_xbar.h`'"'"'s `XbarControlSnapshot`
  (freq + voltage with readback verification). Wiring was incomplete: the GUI
  had `g_app.xbar*` live fields but no draft/pending plumbing, and
  `populate_control_state`/`populate_service_snapshot` never carried XBAR to
  `ServiceStateEnvelope` so the pending diff had nothing to compare against.

- **GUI state:** `GuiDraft.xbarOffsetText[32]/xbarMsvddOffsetText[32]` (MHz/mV
  text) + `AppData.guiXbarOffsetKhz/guiXbarMsvddOffsetUv` (+ profile-load
  provenance flags). `GuiPendingChanges` now carries `xbarValid`,
  `applied/pendingXbarOffsetKhz` and `applied/pendingXbarMsvddOffsetUv` with
  one domain bit `GUI_PENDING_XBAR (1<<9)`. An unparseable field still counts
  as pending so Apply stays reachable and surfaces the real parse error from
  `capture_gui_desired_settings`.

- **Capture/apply:** `capture_gui_desired_settings` (in `main_runtime_control.cpp`)
  now parses the two draft fields, validates `-1000..1000 MHz` and `-100..100 mV`,
  clamps to the IPC-validated `-1e6..1e6 kHz` / `-1e5..1e5 µV` and emits
  `hasXbar*` on change or when `includeCurrentGlobals`. `gpu_backend_apply.cpp`'"'"'s
  `xbar_write` already verified readback; `populate_control_state_locked` and
  `apply_control_state_to_gui` now publish/adopt `hasXbar*` + validity so
  telemetry and the dirty/pending gates agree. `config_profiles.cpp` already
  persisted `xbar_offset_khz/mvdd`; the editor population in
  `config_profiles_gui_state.cpp` now projects them.

- **Theming & placement:** `xbar_dialog.cpp` rewritten from the ad-hoc
  `CreateWindowExA` + `0xCBFF71D0` block to a properly themed modeless dialog
  (dark titlebar via `apply_system_titlebar_theme`/`allow_dark_mode_for_window`,
  `COL_BG`/`COL_INPUT`/`COL_LABEL` brushes in `WM_CTLCOLOR*`, owner-drawn
  `OK/Cancel/Reset` via `xbar_draw_button` matching `ui_theme_button.cpp`'"'"'s
  `COL_BUTTON`/`COL_BUTTON_PRESSED`/`COL_BUTTON_BORDER`, `get_ui_font()` +
  `style_input_control`, `dp()`-scaled layout, centered on work area, disabling
  the main window while open like `LicenseDialog`). The dialog edits are the
  single XBAR surface; `OK` writes the two draft fields, sets
  `guiHasUserModifiedValues` + `set_gui_state_dirty(true)` (which captures the
  clean projection) and refreshes `GUI_PENDING_XBAR` so the Advanced button gets
  the same orange `COL_PENDING` border as `FAN_CURVE_BTN_ID` (now handled in
  `ui_theme_button.cpp`'"'"'s `pendingAccent` and `ui_pending_changes.cpp`'"'"'s
  `gui_pending_invalidate_domain`). `gui_service_state.cpp`'"'"'s
  `gui_set_editor_enabled` now includes `hXbarAdvancedBtn` in `liveControls`.

- **Capability:** Still Blackwell-only (`gpu_capability_probe.cpp`'"'"'s XBAR
  domain via `GPU_DOMAIN_CAP_AVAILABLE`/`SERVICE_MUTATION_DOMAIN_XBAR`), but the
  dialog degrades gracefully: when `serviceControlState` has no XBAR and
  `xbarProbeValid` is false it shows "XBAR not available on this GPU / driver"
  and disables the edits/OK (matching the existing best-effort warning tier in
  `main_capability_warning.cpp`).

- **Ratchet:** `tools/build_state.py: SOURCE_SIZE_RATCHET` bumped for
  `config_profiles.cpp` (879→883), `entry.cpp` (861→863),
  `gpu_backend_apply.cpp` (1280→1320) and newly oversized `gpu_core.h` (820),
  `gui_service_state.cpp` (813), `main.cpp` (801), `main_state_sync.cpp` (859).
