## 2026-07-26: Greyed caption looked foreign; checkbox captions were not clickable (F-CHECKBOX-HIT)

Reported from a screenshot: the greyed "Start program to tray on log in" caption
did not look like any other dimmed text in the window. Root cause is not the
palette — it is that the caption was a separate `STATIC` and the *static* was
what got `WS_DISABLED`. user32 paints a disabled static in the system grey-text
colour and ignores whatever `WM_CTLCOLORSTATIC` returns, so the one control in
the app that ever disables a static was also the one caption the dark theme could
not reach. `hStartOnLogonLabel` and `hServiceEnableLabel` were the only two.

Same two controls carried the second complaint. The caption forwarded
`STN_CLICKED`, so box and text both toggled, but the ~8px gap between them was
dead — the two controls were siblings with nothing covering the space between.

Both are one fix: the caption moves inside the owner-draw `BUTTON`, which already
draws a disabled label in `COL_LABEL` and is a single contiguous hit rectangle.
Ids 2036/2038 are retired, `hStartOnLogonLabel`/`hServiceEnableLabel` are gone,
and the `STN_CLICKED` arms of both `WM_COMMAND` handlers with them.

### The other half: not clickable past the caption

The checkboxes that were *already* labeled (share-with-all-users, the fan-curve
`P1..Pn`, both auto-profile dialog ones) had the opposite defect — widths picked
by eye, dp(280) for a ~155px caption, so a wide stretch of empty background
silently toggled them. `ui_theme_labeled_checkbox_width()` now sizes every one to
`inset + box + gap + text + inset`, measured in the control's own font. The
renderer reads the same inset/gap metrics, so pixels and hit rectangle cannot
drift. `draw_themed_button()` also stopped keeping a per-id list of which
checkboxes are labeled: owning a caption *is* the definition, and the VF locks
carry none (they render through `draw_lock_checkbox()` anyway and were left
alone, as asked).

Two runtime captions change without a layout pass — "... (repair needed)" and
"Share slot N with all users" — so both writers re-fit explicitly, gated on an
actual text change. Without it the control would ellipsize its own label.

`ui_theme_checkbox.cpp` moved to the top of `main_shell.cpp`, ahead of every
shard that lays out or creates a checkbox, so the fit needs no forward
declaration. Two lines in `main.cpp` would have pushed it to 802 and tripped the
source-size ratchet; the include move is the better shape regardless. The new
source guards went to `tools/ui_gates.py` for the same reason (build.py ratchet).

**Not confirmed on screen.** An older instance from `C:\Program Files\greencurve`
holds the single-instance mutex on this machine, so launching the fresh build
just re-activates the old window.

## 2026-07-26: Profile row read as live with no service installed (F-ACTIONABLE)

Reported from a screenshot: with the service uninstalled the top half of the
window is correctly dead, while Load / Save / Clear / Shared profiles /
Auto-Profiles / both apply-on-* combos all present as actionable. Nothing in the
enable logic distinguished "not installed" from "READY" — every non-READY phase
funnels through `gui_set_editor_enabled(false)`, which never touched the profile
row.

Two of those were real defects, not cosmetics. **Save** builds on
`build_full_live_desired_settings()`, and `populate_control_state_locked()`
hardcodes `valid = true` / `hasPowerLimit = true` over all-zero `g_app` fields —
a Save with no snapshot writes `power_limit_pct=0`. The only thing preventing it
was `validate_configured_gpu_selection_for_client()` failing first on
`adapterCount == 0`, reporting the *wrong* reason ("configured GPU identity is
missing or ambiguous"). Its two guards are now swapped (line-neutral, which
matters because `ui_main_window.cpp` is at its ratchet). **Load** wrote an editor
that is disabled and blank, so the only evidence it did anything was a status
line.

### Two rules, after one wrong turn

First attempt kept Clear, the slot combo, share-with-all-users, start-to-tray and
both apply-on-* combos enabled on the grounds that they "still work". Rejected on
review of the actual result, correctly: a file write succeeding is not the same as
the action accomplishing anything. Publishing to the shared bank exists so the
service can apply the profile for other users; setting a logon profile does
nothing without a service. The original instinct — grey everything but the
service checkbox — was right.

What survived from the objection is narrower and load-bearing:

  1. An escape hatch is never blocked (Refresh, service checkbox, License).
  2. Everything service-dependent is greyed, **except a control that already
     holds a service-dependent setting**, which stays enabled so the setting can
     be undone.

Rule 2's exemption is not politeness. `startup_task_config_state()` returns
ENABLED for any `logon_slot`/`logon_shared_slot`, keeping a Task Scheduler entry
registered; `hLogonCombo` is the only GUI path that can clear one; and
`clear_profile_from_config()` deliberately preserves `logon_shared_slot`, so
deleting the profile is not an escape either. Greying it unconditionally would
leave a scheduled task no one can remove. Same shape for a published shared
profile and the GUI's own autostart. So `gui_service_config_control_actionable()`
greys those four at their default and keeps them live while set — which, in the
reported state where nothing is assigned, greys all of them.

Gating on *reachability* rather than readiness is also deliberate: SYNCING /
RECOVERING / DEVICE_MISSING keep profile editing available, because a profile
loaded then is preserved as a pre-READY overlay and rebased onto the first
coherent snapshot. Pinned by a test asserting exactly that asymmetry.

### Two mechanical hazards found while wiring it

The shared-profiles button and the share-with-all-users checkbox have **two
writers each**. `update_share_all_users_check_state()` owns both and runs *after*
`update_profile_action_buttons()` inside `refresh_profile_controls_from_config()`,
and standalone from the share toggle at `ui_main_window.cpp:839`. Gating only in
the projection would have greyed them and then silently re-enabled them on every
Load/Save/Clear/slot change. Worse, that owner is on no service-transition path at
all, so gating only there would never react to the service going away. Both
writers now use identical expressions, fed by `sharedProfileCountCache` and
`machineProfileSlotSavedCache`.

And `update_profile_action_buttons()` was doing I/O: `is_profile_slot_saved()`
costs up to six INI reads under the storage lock, and calling it from
`gui_set_editor_enabled()` puts it on a ~2/second cadence while not READY (the
fan-telemetry reconnect backstop plus the 3 s reconnect timer both enqueue full
syncs). Per-slot saved state and the shared count are cached in `AppData`,
invalidated alongside the tray cache on any config write. The cache covers *all*
slots because `CBN_SELCHANGE` re-gates without a refresh.

One genuine missed re-gate site: `gui_service_handle_admin_completion()` clears
`available` for all three outcomes but only the *uninstall* branch reached
`gui_render_service_phase_only()`, so a completed install/repair left everything
enabled until the queued sync landed. Re-gate now runs before the branch.

Also consolidated three GUI-only copies of the hand-written
`ready && attached && !detached` expression onto the policy.
`main_runtime_capture.cpp` and `main_fan_runtime.cpp` are deliberately left
alone: they compile into the service binary too and use a third
`isServiceProcess ? loaded : ready` form, and those two already disagree with
each other today — migrating them would change service-side behaviour that
cannot be verified from here. Recorded in the policy header as a follow-up.

Two premises worth recording as *wrong*, so they are not re-investigated: there
is no startup flicker (the probe at `entry.cpp:520` is synchronous and includes a
real ping, long before the controls exist), and a test case passing an
out-of-range enum value is itself UB — the sanitizer build rejected it, so the
`default:` branch stays as defence but is untestable.

## 2026-07-26: Re-loading the applied profile previewed a lock change forever

Fourth F-PENDING report, and the deepest: with profile 2 applied, loading
profile 2 *again* previewed the flattened tail as changing. The log said
`mask=0x002 points=51` -- LOCK only, tail marked by
`gui_pending_mark_locked_tail()`.

The cause is physical, and the apply path had already written it down:

    correction pass 1: strict tail point 77 actual 2962 MHz != target 2957 MHz
    (tol=8); keeping requested target

Profile 2 asks for 2957. The GPU's VF grid cannot represent it and settles on
2962. `appliedLockFreq` ends up holding the settled 2962 while the editor holds
the requested 2957, and **both** the pending diff and
`capture_gui_apply_settings()` compared exactly -- the log confirms the apply path
also reported `changed=1`. So this was never purely a preview bug: loading the
applied profile genuinely enabled Apply and would have performed a full
reset-and-reapply that could not change anything. A dirty state that can never
converge.

Fix: compare the lock frequency within one VF grid step, reusing the existing
`curve_point_verify_tolerance_mhz()` -- the same tolerance the post-apply verify
pass uses, derived from the actual distance to the nearest distinct neighbour
rather than a magic number. `capture_gui_apply_settings()` now reaches its
`lockChanged` verdict through `gui_pending_lock_changed()` too, so the greyed
button and the apply verdict cannot drift apart. The tolerance covers the
frequency only; a moved anchor or changed mode is always a change (pinned,
1580-1589).

Also fixed while here: `gui_pending_changes_refresh()` ran *before*
`detect_locked_tail_from_curve()` inside `populate_global_controls()`, so live
lock detection could move the lock after the mask had been computed, leaving it a
step behind. The refresh is now last in that function.

Open question worth revisiting: `appliedLockFreq` holding a readback-derived
value at all is the deeper asymmetry -- `appliedCurveMHz` is documented as
intent-only for exactly this reason, and `main_state_sync.cpp` adopts
`snapshot->lockMHz` from live detection. The tolerance makes the symptom correct
and matches the apply path's own notion of "on target", but making the lock
baseline intent-only would remove the mismatch at the source. Not attempted here
because live detection legitimately needs readback when there is no known intent
(a lock set before launch, or by another tool).

## 2026-07-26: Previewed locked tail used a stale lock target

Third F-PENDING hardware report, and this one was a genuine wrong number rather
than a rendering choice. Stock GPU, profile 2 loaded: the graph previewed the
flattened tail at **2482 MHz** while the anchor field and the profile both said
**2957**. The headline `Lock: 2482 MHz` agreed with the graph, so both were
reading the same wrong source.

2482 was not `2957 - 475` as the arithmetic tempts you to conclude -- it is the
**stock curve value at ci 76**, and it got there like this:

    populate_desired_into_gui()
      set_edit_value(anchor field, 2957)      <- control only, NOT the draft
      apply_lock(vi, mode)
        if (lockedFreq == 0)                  <- true, nothing was locked
          lockedFreq = guiDraft.curveMHz[76]  <- still the stock baseline!
      ...
    caller: gui_draft_capture_desired()       <- draft finally gets 2957

So `apply_lock()` inferred the target from a draft the projection had not written
yet. Apply itself was always correct, because
`capture_gui_desired_settings()` prefers the draft anchor over `lockedFreq` --
and by Apply time the draft holds 2957. Only the two *displays* were wrong, and
the tail preview was wrong because it read raw `lockedFreq`.

Fixed at the root: the projection states its own lock MHz (stored absolute).
Plus `gui_pending_lock_target_mhz()` (pure, tested 1552-1557) so the preview, the
headline text, and the pending lock diff resolve the target exactly the way
capture does -- draft anchor first. That last part also removed a latent
false-positive in the diff, which had been comparing a stale `lockedFreq`
against `appliedLockFreq`.

The locked-tail drift diagnostic still reads `lockedFreq` directly on purpose: it
compares live readback against the *applied* lock, not the pending one. The
source guard is written to allow that and forbid only `return g_app.lockedFreq;`
from the preview path -- a first attempt used a multi-line needle that could
never have failed, which is worse than no guard.

## 2026-07-26: Tray tooltip named the loaded profile, not the applied one; 0.21

`ensure_tray_profile_cache()` read `[profiles] selected_slot` -- the combo's
*editing* selection. Loading a profile deliberately does not touch hardware, so
with slot 2 applied and slot 1 loaded the tray claimed "Profile 1". The user's
own config showed exactly that: `selected_slot=1, applied_slot=2`.

The right authority already existed and was already documented, one file over:
`auto_profile_active_slot()` reads `applied_slot` for the tray menu checkmark,
with a comment calling it "the consistent source of truth for which profile is
actually active on the GPU". The tooltip and the tick are the same UI surface
and were disagreeing.

Now `gui_tray_format_active_profile()` (pure, tested 1560-1570) decides the
label: `applied_slot` first, then the service's own view for the cases where no
personal slot is active. Shared and machine banks are named explicitly rather
than folded into the same-numbered personal slot -- the same rule
`applied_user_slot_from_service_profile()` already enforces, since those are
separate banks with separate contents. A hand-typed Apply, and a user slot whose
saved intent has since been edited away from what is running (exactly when
`applied_slot` is cleared to 0), both read "Manual settings". The
`(saved)`/`(empty)` suffix is gone: an applied slot is saved by definition.

Cache invalidation needed a second look. Any config write already drops the tray
cache, but the service view can move *without* one -- switching between two
shared slots leaves `applied_slot` at 0 -- so
`sync_applied_profile_from_service_metadata()` now invalidates whenever its sync
inputs change.

`main_gpu_front.cpp` was sitting on its 845-line ratchet, so the tray
presentation group (mode label, profile cache, live-hardware gate, tooltip
assembly) moved to `tray_presentation.cpp`, included from the exact position it
occupied so amalgamated ordering is unchanged. That also brought the file under
the 800-line guideline. Two `build.py` guards that named `main_gpu_front.cpp`
were switched to `require_text_in_surface` over both shards -- which is what that
helper exists for, and is more durable than retargeting a path.

Version bumped to **0.21** (`VERSION` is the single source; archives, PE
resources, and installer metadata follow automatically).

## 2026-07-26: F-PENDING missed the global GPU offset entirely

Second hardware report on the same feature: with profile 2 applied and profile 1
loaded, points 70-75 were about to drop and nothing previewed it.

Diagnosis came from the user's own config and debug log rather than from
guessing at the scenario, and the guess would have been wrong. **Neither profile
owns a single explicit curve point.** They differ only in
`gpu_offset_mhz` (475 with `exclude_low_count=70` vs 0) plus a 5 MHz lock
difference. The log confirmed the mask was already `0x10D`
(CURVE|GPU_OFFSET|GPU_EXCLUDE|FAN_CURVE, 52 points): the offset *domain* was
flagged, and the 52 points were the lock tail moving 2957 -> 2962 -- a 5 MHz
change that is sub-pixel on a 500-3400 MHz axis, which is why the graph looked
green.

The real gap: nothing modelled what a global offset does to the curve.
`gpu_backend_apply.cpp:290` is explicit about it --

    explicit point -> desired->curvePointMHz[ci]              (absolute)
    otherwise      -> base + offset component, base = live - freqOffsets[ci]

-- so an unowned point currently *displays* stock + the APPLIED component while
Apply will re-place it at stock + the PENDING one. `curve_base_khz_for_point()`
already computes that stock value; the preview simply never used it.

Fix: `gui_pending_offset_shift_changed()` marks every point whose offset
component moves (comparing components, not frequencies, so it stays drift-free),
with the two carve-outs the apply path has -- owned points are absolute, the
locked tail is pinned. The graph projects those points through the same
resolved offset numbers, cached alongside the mask so the dashed line and the
orange markers cannot disagree.

Method note worth keeping: two rounds of reasoning about which points each
profile "probably" owns were both wrong. Reading `config.ini` and
`greencurve_debug.txt` settled it in one step. The `GUI pending changes:
mask=0x%03X points=%d` line added with the feature is what made the log useful --
worth preserving.

---

## 2026-07-26: F-PENDING graph painted the whole curve orange (hardware fix)

First hardware report. Apply profile 2, then load profile 1: the entire VF curve
went orange even though only two points differed, and the edit fields correctly
showed only those two as pending. Reported as direction-dependent -- loading
profile 2 after applying profile 1 looked right.

The per-domain diff was fine; the graph was not. The pending polyline was drawn
across its **whole** length whenever anything was pending, so the orange dashed
line sat on top of the green applied line everywhere and hid it. The on-curve
MHz labels had the same shape of bug (`showPending ? COL_PENDING : COL_TEXT`,
a per-graph decision where a per-point one was needed).

The direction dependence was perception, not a second bug: when the newly loaded
profile owns MORE points than the applied one, the unowned points have
`appliedCurveMHz == 0` and are genuinely pending, so a mostly-orange curve *is*
correct there. The reverse direction is what exposed it.

Fix: `gui_pending_next_changed_run()` in the policy header walks runs of changed
points, expanded by one neighbour on each side so the dashed line departs from
and rejoins the applied curve where the two are equal; the graph draws one
dashed sub-polyline per run. Pure, so it is pinned by tests (1520-1537) incl.
edge runs, split runs, all-changed, and degenerate inputs.

Not touched: after applying a profile that owns points 49-126 and loading one
that owns only 70-126, points 49-69 are reported unchanged even though Apply
would revert them to stock (reset-before-apply writes only owned points). That
matches `capture_gui_apply_settings()`'s own curve diff exactly -- it iterates
`hasCurvePoint` too -- and the editor already displays live readback for unowned
points. Fixing it means modelling "what stock would be", which neither side
knows without a reset. Left as a known limitation rather than diverging the two
diffs.

---

## 2026-07-26: Unapplied changes are visible, and Apply greys out (F-PENDING)

The window could not distinguish what the GPU is running from what is merely
typed. Worse than neutral: the graph *merged* draft values over live readback
into one series, so an unapplied edit looked like reality. The only dirty cue
was a tray-tooltip suffix that appears solely while the GPU is **not** live.

### One predicate, two consumers

`gui_pending_changes_policy.h` owns the per-domain decisions; a cached summary
in `ui_pending_changes.cpp` feeds both the orange field colours and the Apply
enable, so the button and the colours cannot contradict each other.

Because it now gates a button, the predicate had to be conservative in exactly
one direction. Unparseable draft text always counts as pending -- otherwise a
half-typed value would grey out Apply and strand the user before the real
validation error. `capture_gui_apply_settings()` stays the authority and keeps
its "No changes to apply" path; what changed is that the common case no longer
requires clicking a button to discover nothing happened.

The other load-bearing rule was already written down elsewhere: compare against
drift-free applied intent, never live readback. Comparing `g_app.curve` would
have lit fields orange on its own as the VF curve drifts under boost, and would
have repainted the window on every telemetry tick. Both rules are pinned by
tests (1480-1519) and by source guards.

### Graph

Green solid = applied, orange dashed = pending, orange markers on exactly the
changed points. Dashed rather than colour-only so the two series stay
distinguishable. `GdipSetPenDashStyle` is resolved as an **optional** import --
`gdiplus_ensure()` fails closed on a missing symbol, and losing antialiasing
everywhere because one entry point is absent would be a bad trade -- so a dashed
line falls back to a GDI geometric pen (`PS_DASH` is cosmetic-only, so a wide
dashed line cannot use `CreatePen`). No legend: an always-visible caption was
already rejected once as clutter.

### Where the refresh hooks in

Every genuine edit funnels through `gui_draft_capture_curve_value()` /
`gui_draft_capture_text()`, so the six `EN_CHANGE` handlers needed no changes at
all. The fan curve is not in `GuiDraft` and the lock lives in `AppData`, so
those paths call the refresh explicitly. `gui_apply_ready_envelope` calls it at
the single point after **all three** projection branches -- the pre-READY sparse
overlay branch was initially missed and would have left Apply greyed with a
profile loaded.

### Splits forced by the size ratchet

`ui_main_window.cpp` sat exactly on its 1315-line ceiling and `main.cpp` exactly
on 800, so: the graph moved to `ui_main_graph.cpp`, the `WM_CTLCOLOR*` bodies to
`ui_main_ctlcolor.cpp` (ratchet lowered to 1279), the forward declarations to
`gui_render_forward.h`, and the lock-checkbox render guards from `build.py` into
`tools/ui_gates.py`. Two existing guards pointing at `ui_main.cpp` were
retargeted to `ui_main_graph.cpp`.

Open: the orange rendering, the dashed line, and the Apply greying have not been
seen on hardware yet. Worth checking specifically whether the closed fan-mode
combo honours `WM_CTLCOLORSTATIC` for its text -- it should, since the manifest
declares no comctl32 v6 dependency, but if not, colour the "Fan Control:" label
instead.

## 2026-07-26: Tooltip and dialog theming; the range caption came back out

GPU-tested feedback on yesterday's overclock work, all three points valid.

### The tooltip was system yellow

Expected, in hindsight: the app manifest declares no comctl32 v6 dependency, so
every common control runs unthemed and the tooltip fell back to the classic
`COLOR_INFOBK` yellow. That same absence is what makes the fix trivial -- with
no visual styles applied, `TTM_SETTIPBKCOLOR` / `TTM_SETTIPTEXTCOLOR` are
honored directly, so `apply_tooltip_theme()` sets the application palette plus
the UI font. `SetWindowTheme(L"", L"")` is called first so this stays correct if
a v6 dependency is ever added. The lock-checkbox tooltip shares the window and
was fixed by the same call.

### The caption was redundant

With the tooltips carrying the same numbers, the always-visible gray caption
under the overclock row was clutter in an already dense row. Removed: the
static, its `MainLayoutStaticRole`, the layout placement, `AppData` handle, the
`oc_range_format_caption()` formatter, and the guards and tests that pinned it.
Recorded in [windows-ui-layout.md](../windows-ui-layout.md) as a decision rather
than an omission, so it does not get "restored" later.

### Every prompt was light-mode

The stock message box is painted by user32 from system colors, with no hook to
change them -- `SetPreferredAppMode` does not reach it. So a confirmation showed
up as a bright light window in front of the dark main window even with Windows
set to dark. Replaced with `gc_message_box()`: own window class, owner-draw
buttons, application palette when the OS is dark and a standard light palette
when it is not, matching what `apply_system_titlebar_theme()` already did for
chrome. 49 call sites across 10 shards converted; a `forbid_text` gate keeps
them converted.

Two things worth remembering about it:

- **Escape resolves to the safest answer, not to nothing.** The stock
  `MB_YESNO` refuses to close at all; ours maps Escape and the close button to
  `No`. An undismissable modal is worse, and resolving a dismissal to `Yes`
  would be dangerous on exactly the prompt this all started with.
- **`entry.cpp` keeps two real `MessageBoxA` calls on purpose.** They report
  that the window class or the main window could not be created -- precisely
  when a custom window cannot be trusted. `ui_message_box.cpp` keeps the same
  fallback internally for class-registration or creation failure.

`gc_message_box` was added to the F-PRESENTATION-SILENT forbidden-token list;
without that, renaming the call would have silently opened a hole in the gate
that keeps auto-profile applies from showing UI.

Full build green on all six targets; regression exit codes 1440-1471 added.
Runtime confirmation of the light-mode palette is still pending -- it was
verified dark only.

## 2026-07-25: Overclock ranges are visible, and a high OC asks once

Two GUI gaps closed in the Windows overclock row: the driver-reported limits
were queried, shipped over IPC, and then used only to enable/disable the edit
controls, so a user learned a value was out of range only from a failed apply;
and a typo of `2000` for `200` went straight to the hardware with no
confirmation, even though Reset has confirmed for ages.

### Cue banners were the obvious answer and do not work

The first instinct — `EM_SETCUEBANNER` gray placeholder text in the fields — is
dead on arrival. Windows paints a cue banner only while the edit is **empty**,
and all three fields always hold a value (`"0"`/`"0"`/`"100"` at creation, then
repopulated from every service snapshot). The one existing use in
`auto_profile_dialog.cpp` works only because that hotkey edit starts empty.
Recorded as an invariant plus a `forbid_text` gate so it is not retried.

The ranges went into a gray caption on the free part of the exclude sub-row
(`x = dp(220)`, past the `"VF points"` suffix) plus a hover tooltip per field.
The overclock row is packed solid at the 1180dp base width, so that was the only
spot that did not require growing the window.

### Advertise the intersection, not the driver range

The advertised bounds are the driver window **intersected** with what the apply
path actually accepts: `+/-1000` MHz GPU and `+/-5000` MHz memory from
`validate_desired_settings_for_ipc()`, `50..150 %` power from
`gpu_backend_apply.cpp`. Advertising the raw driver range would promise values
the service then rejects. Memory stays explicitly marked advisory — F-DOM-1
applies past the reported range on purpose. There is no driver *percent* power
range at all; it is derived from the milliwatt constraints (ceil the minimum,
floor the maximum) and falls back to the enforced gate when unreported.

### Warning once, not nagging

The confirmation needs three independent conditions, all load-bearing:
hand-typed (profile-loaded values are the user's own reviewed intent, tracked
per field), at or past the threshold (200 MHz / 2000 MHz, configurable under
`[ui]`, `0` disables), and **higher than what is already applied** — lowering an
existing high clock reduces risk and must stay silent. Automation never reaches
the dialog because auto-profile/hotkey/tray/logon applies queue their mutations
directly; source guards forbid those files from even naming the policy.

### Two splits fell out of it

`ui_main_window.cpp` sat *exactly* on its 1397-line ratchet, so a one-line call
failed the build. Apply/Refresh/Reset moved to `ui_main_apply.cpp` (ratchet
lowered to 1315). `config_profiles_ui.cpp` was at 798 and my two provenance
assignments pushed it over 800, so the profile→GUI projection half moved to
`config_profiles_gui_state.cpp` (484 + 333). Guards anchored in both moved
regions were repointed. New source gates live in `tools/ui_gates.py` rather than
`build.py`, which is itself at its ratchet.

Full build green on all six targets; regression exit codes 1400–1435 added.
Runtime verification on real hardware (caption values, tooltips, the dialog
sequence, and silence on an auto-profile fire) is still pending.

## 2026-07-25: Fuzzing on the pinned toolchain, and the CET claim was wrong

Started from two questions: would using clang inside MSYS2 let us fuzz, and
would it improve the CFG/CET story. Both answers turned out to be "no, and the
premise was off" — and testing the second one found a false claim in the wiki.

### Fuzzing works today; MSYS2 would not have helped

llvm-mingw's clang driver rejects `-fsanitize=fuzzer` for
`x86_64-w64-windows-gnu`, which had been read as "no fuzzing here". The gate is
only on that convenience flag. `-fsanitize-coverage=...` and
`libclang_rt.fuzzer-x86_64.a` both ship and both work on this target, including
combined with ASan+UBSan; passing them directly gives a working libFuzzer.

Decisive check: the *same* clang binary accepts `-fsanitize=fuzzer` for
`x86_64-pc-windows-msvc` and `x86_64-linux-gnu`. So the gate is **per-target,
not per-distribution** — MSYS2's clang64 targets the identical triple and would
hit the identical gate. Rejected; recorded in [testing.md](../testing.md) so it
is not revisited.

Landed `python build.py --fuzz` with five targets in `tests/fuzz_main.cpp`
(`service_request`, `vf_snapshot`, `task_xml`, `config_strings`,
`wire_prefix`), committed seed corpora, ASan+UBSan, and a bounded 20000-run
default measured in iterations rather than seconds (no timing assumption).
5.5M total iterations found no defects.

The targets assert post-conditions, not just absence of crashes — e.g. an
accepted IPC request must satisfy every documented clamp *and* re-validate
idempotently. **Negative control**: tightening `lockCi >= -1` to `>= 0` made the
target fail with exit 77 and emit a reproducer, so the harness is not passing
vacuously.

### The CET finding: our code was fine, the wiki was not

`build.md` claimed `-fcf-protection=full` was "compiled but never materializes
in the binary". Measuring build 439's `greencurve.exe`: 312 Guard CF targets,
only 18 starting with `endbr64`. That looked like confirmation, and an initial
probe suggested LTO was stripping the instrumentation (counts 5 vs 1).

Both readings were wrong, and re-testing is what caught it:

- The LTO probe was an artifact. Isolating opt-level from `-flto` gives 4 vs 3,
  and disassembling shows the missing one is a function that was **inlined**.
  With address-taken statics, LTO keeps `endbr64` on all of them and drops it
  only from a directly-called function whose address provably never escapes.
  LTO makes placement *more precise*, not broken.
- Attributing all 294 uninstrumented targets by symbol: **zero come from Green
  Curve source.** They are prebuilt llvm-mingw runtime — 251 libc++/libc++abi
  (mostly the bundled `itanium_demangle` vtable methods pulled in by `-static`),
  23 libunwind, 20 MinGW CRT. `llvm-objdump -d` over `libmingwex.a`,
  `libmingw32.a`, `libmsvcrt.a`, `libucrt.a` finds **zero** `endbr64`: the
  vendor compiles them without `-fcf-protection`, and their address-taken
  functions still land in the linker-generated Guard CF table.
- The 18 instrumented targets are exactly our address-taken set (`WndProc`, the
  dialog/subclass procs, thread procs, PnP/service callbacks,
  `__stack_chk_fail`, `__guard_check_icall_fptr`).

Also corrected a smaller error made along the way: `DllCharacteristicsEx` is
**not** in `IMAGE_LOAD_CONFIG_DIRECTORY64`. It lives in an
`IMAGE_DEBUG_TYPE_EX_DLLCHARACTERISTICS` (type 20) debug-directory entry. A
probe reading it out of the load config was actually reading
`GuardLongJumpTargetCount` and printing nonsense — which is what made the GUI
and service binaries disagree and exposed the mistake.

And a correction to the risk framing: CET_COMPAT gates the **backward edge**
(shadow stack) only and never consults `endbr64`, which is the **forward edge**
(IBT, not enforced in Windows user mode today). So enabling that bit would not
fault on the uninstrumented vendor functions, contrary to what the earlier
concern assumed.

What is genuinely absent stays absent: the CET_COMPAT opt-in bit and
`GuardRFFailureRoutine` (both zero), because MinGW-mode `ld.lld` has no
`/cetcompat`. Fixing that needs an MSVC-ABI toolchain, which needs the
non-redistributable MSVC CRT and Windows SDK and so breaks `build.py`'s
hermetic self-downloading model. Unchanged conclusion, correct reasons.

`python build.py --check-cet` now pins this. Because release binaries are
linked `-s` and carry no symbols, it compiles its own unstripped probe with the
real shipped flags, so attribution is always possible and the check does not
depend on `dist/` state. **Negative control**: removing `-fcf-protection=full`
makes it exit 1 and name the 18 functions that lost instrumentation.

### Note on the size ratchet

`BUILD_SCRIPT_SIZE_RATCHET` failed at 5979 lines with "Move test harness /
source guards out rather than raising it." Took that instruction rather than
bumping the number: both gates moved to `tools/security_gates.py`, and the CLI
flag definitions moved with them (`add_arguments(parser)`), which also puts the
flags next to the code implementing them. `build.py` ended at 5645, under the
unchanged 5650.

Verified: `python build.py --test`, `--test --asan`, `--fuzz`, `--check-cet`,
and a Windows x64 build at build 439.

## 2026-07-24: Linux daemon fan failsafe, stop handling, and accept classification

A whole-codebase audit found the material risk concentrated in the native Linux
daemon: it runs as root and owns fan writes, but had none of the Windows
failsafe or diagnostic scaffolding. Four independent defects could each leave a
GPU fan pinned at a stale manual duty with no journal line and no recovery.

1. The fan poll deadline used `clock_gettime(CLOCK_REALTIME)` against a
   `PTHREAD_COND_INITIALIZER` condition variable (default realtime clock), so a
   backwards wall-clock step — NTP correcting a machine without a
   battery-backed RTC, a manual date change, a VM resume — stalled
   re-assertion for the size of the step. Every other timer in the tree already
   used `CLOCK_MONOTONIC`, which is what identified this as a slip rather than
   a design choice.
2. A failed `nvmlDeviceGetTemperature` skipped the worker's entire body: no
   failure counter, no lockout, no log. A persistent telemetry fault therefore
   pinned the fan indefinitely and silently — the exact opposite of the write
   path directly below it. Windows had counted temperature-poll failures all
   along (`main_fan_runtime.cpp`), confirming the omission was Linux-only.
3. When the post-lockout `linux_backend_set_fan_auto()` itself failed, Linux
   only logged `auto=0`. Windows escalates to 100%.
4. The daemon installed no signal handlers, so `systemctl stop` killed it at
   default disposition and the whole teardown block was unreachable — including
   any fan handback.

Separately, `accept()` treated every non-`EINTR` errno as fatal and then
`return 0`. Since the unit uses `Restart=on-failure`, a transient
`ECONNABORTED`/`EMFILE`/`ENOBUFS` permanently removed GPU control *and* systemd
declined to restart. The single-connection loop also let one silent peer hold
the daemon for the full 2 s I/O deadline.

Fixes: a shared pure `fan_runtime_observe_result()` ladder in
`fan_runtime_policy.h` used by both platforms (telemetry loss and refused
writes share one saturating counter; a refused handback demands
`EMERGENCY_MAX`); `pthread_condattr_setclock(CLOCK_MONOTONIC)`;
SIGTERM/SIGINT handlers driving a self-pipe so shutdown is event-driven with no
timeout; `daemon_release_fan_to_driver()` after the worker join;
`daemon_accept_disposition()` with a reserved-descriptor recovery for
`EMFILE`/`ENFILE` (no sleeps, no spinning) and a non-zero fatal exit; and a
`poll()`-driven accept stage so a stalled peer cannot wedge the loop.

`linux_daemon.cpp` grew past the 800-line ratchet, which correctly failed the
build. Rather than widening the ratchet, the new code was split into
`linux_daemon_lifecycle.h` and `linux_daemon_serve.h`; `linux_daemon.cpp` went
from 768 to 703 lines. That split broke four existing source guards that named
the file by path, so `require_text_in_surface()`/`forbid_text_in_surface()`
were added — guards now address the aggregator plus its shards, so a future
split does not punish itself.

Also fixed a signed-overflow UB in `hotkeys.cpp`: the f-key branch accumulated
an unbounded digit run before range-checking, reachable from `[hotkeys] slotN`
in user config. Reproduced directly under UBSan
(`signed integer overflow: 999999999 * 10`) and fixed by bounding inside the
loop; over-length input is now rejected instead of truncated.

Verification: the new compiled cases were proven non-vacuous by injecting an
off-by-one into the escalation threshold, which failed exactly at return 1303.
`python build.py --test`, `--test --asan`, and the full
`--check --target all --arch all` matrix (six targets) pass. The native
transport fixture gained a two-peer accept case and is now cross-compiled with
warnings-as-errors on non-Linux hosts, where it previously was not built at
all. RTX 5080 hardware acceptance is still required for the thermal paths.

## 2026-07-24: extract the regression harness and tighten the size ratchets

The compiled harness was a ~4000-line raw string literal inside `build.py`, so
it had no LSP, clang-format, clang-tidy or syntax highlighting, and adding a
regression test meant editing a Python string.

It was moved **verbatim** to `tests/regression_main.cpp`. Verification was
behavioural, not structural: the full `--test` output is byte-identical to the
pre-extraction run, and re-injecting the escalation-threshold off-by-one still
fails at exactly return 1303. `compile_commands.json` now includes the harness,
so it gets the same tooling as production code, and a `forbid_text` guard stops
it migrating back into a string literal.

`build.py` dropped from 8994 to ~5600 lines and is now covered by
`BUILD_SCRIPT_SIZE_RATCHET`, having previously been exempt from the very size
rule it enforces on `source/`.

`SOURCE_SIZE_RATCHET` had accumulated 852 lines of slack across 15 entries,
letting already-oversized modules regrow: `linux_backend.cpp` was allowed 1154
lines while sitting at 721, `ui_main.cpp` 867 while sitting at 728. Four
entries had fallen back under the guideline entirely and were dropped; the rest
are pinned to their exact current size, so slack is now zero.

**Not done, deliberately:** `run_source_regression_checks()` (~2700 lines,
~1000 assertions) has not yet moved into a `build_checks/` package, and the
four worst oversized modules (`ui_main_window.cpp` 1397, `gpu_backend_apply.cpp`
1333, `main_runtime_nvml.cpp` 1105, `gpu_backend.cpp` 972) have not been split.
Both remain open; the ratchets now prevent either from getting worse.

## 2026-07-24: repo hygiene, clang-tidy, and CI coverage

Audit follow-ups outside the runtime code.

Generated Python bytecode is no longer tracked: `.gitignore` now covers
`__pycache__/` and `*.pyc`, and a source check fails the build if any such
path is tracked. `commit_msg.txt` and
`.clangd` were both tracked *and* gitignored, which does nothing for tracked
files; `commit_msg.txt` was untracked and `.clangd` stays tracked with a
comment explaining why. A stray 213 KB file named `NUL` (a cmd-style
redirection artifact) was deleted.

Running the suite then surfaced a pre-existing flake at return 167: the DACL
fixtures named their scratch directory by PID and deleted it with a bare
`RemoveDirectoryW`, which silently failed because they had just applied a
*protected* DACL. Six directories had leaked, and a reused PID broke the run.
Fixed at the root with unique directory names and teardown through
`restore_inherited_dacl()`, with the teardown result asserted.

`build.py --tidy` now runs the bundled clang-tidy, which had shipped unused
this whole time. Two lessons worth keeping: `misc-const-correctness` alone
produced over 3000 style hits that buried everything real, and clang-tidy does
not inherit `clang++`'s libc++ search path, so two translation units reported
`clang-diagnostic-error` purely from a missing `<cstddef>`. Both are handled;
the curated backlog is 43 entries in `tidy-baseline.txt`, advisory for now.
The analyzer immediately found a genuine dead store in the new
`linux_daemon_serve.h`, which is now clean.

CI gained toolchain caching (it redownloaded ~178 MB per job) and, more
importantly, `--test` on the Linux job. `tests/linux_transport_regression.cpp`
is the only real test file in the repo and the Linux job is the only host that
can execute it, yet CI never ran it. A source guard now enforces that.

Also inlined the CET finding into `build.md`; it had cited a document that is
not part of the repository. *(Superseded 2026-07-25: the inlined finding itself was
false. `-fcf-protection=full` is effective on our own code; only the
shadow-stack CET_COMPAT opt-in is missing. See the 2026-07-25 entry.)*

## 2026-07-24: Linux crash breadcrumbs and systemd sandboxing

Second audit finding for the Linux side: it had no crash diagnostics at all.
Windows installs `SetUnhandledExceptionFilter` plus a vectored handler and
writes minidumps; Linux builds with `-fexceptions -frtti`, uses `std::string`
in the INI parser, and contains zero `try`/`catch`, no `std::set_terminate`,
and no `std::set_new_handler`. A `bad_alloc` or `length_error` therefore
aborted the root daemon silently.

`linux_crash_breadcrumb.h` adds fatal-signal and terminate breadcrumbs written
with `write(2)` only, carrying role, signal, PID, lifecycle phase, and version.
`SA_RESETHAND` plus `raise()` keeps the crash itself intact — the breadcrumb is
purely additive, so core dumps and systemd crash accounting are unchanged.
Guards forbid `snprintf`/`fprintf`/`strsignal` in that header; the hand-rolled
integer formatter was checked against `printf` across `LONG_MIN`/`LONG_MAX`
under UBSan.

The generated unit also gained sandboxing (`ProtectSystem=full`,
`ProtectHome`, `PrivateTmp`, `RestrictAddressFamilies=AF_UNIX`,
`SystemCallArchitectures=native`, and others). Four directives are pinned
*off* by `forbid_text` guards with recorded reasons, because they would break
the NVIDIA user-mode stack: `PrivateDevices`, `MemoryDenyWriteExecute`,
`ProtectSystem=strict`, and the kernel-tunable protections.

Verified: `python build.py --test` and the Linux x64 warning-as-error check
build pass, and the breadcrumb header cross-compiles clean with `-Werror`.
**Not verified:** WSL is not installed on this machine, so the breadcrumb was
never executed and the sandboxing directives were never exercised against a
live daemon. Both need the outstanding native RTX 5080 acceptance run.

## 2026-07-16: fix Unix-socket pathname authorization and Blackwell identity encoding

The first protocol-v12 reporter retest proved that daemon restart/version
verification was working (new PID, build 432, protocol 12), but exposed two
follow-up defects. The daemon logged successful socket authorization after
calling `fchown`/`fchmod`/`fstat` on the bound AF_UNIX socket descriptor. On
Linux that descriptor refers to a sockfs inode, not the filesystem entry made
by `bind()`, so `UMask=0077` left the client-visible path `root:root 0700` while
the wrong inode passed verification. Root could connect and the root-only
installer ping concealed the mismatch; a supplementary `greencurve` member
correctly received `EACCES`.

The daemon now configures and verifies `greencurve.sock` relative to the
already-proven root-owned mode-0755 runtime-directory descriptor, and aborts
before readiness on any type/owner/group/mode failure. Installation explicitly
sets `RuntimeDirectoryMode=0755` and adds real-path `root:greencurve 0660`
verification between `is-active` and the protocol/build ping, so it cannot
report success for a root-only pathname. The native Linux transport fixture
reproduces the old descriptor/path divergence under `umask(0077)` and validates
the corrected helper.

The same retest still reported one NVML GPU and one NvAPI handle as conflicting.
The matcher had assumed one fixed high-word device encoding and compared raw
subsystem values. It now locates NVIDIA vendor ID `10DE` in either word, accepts
device-only forms, compares compatible full/swapped/partial subsystem forms,
and allows either NvAPI's documented internal or external PCI device ID to
corroborate NVML. It preserves NVML's stable raw representation in published
identity. Real conflicts remain fail-closed and now report NVML device/
subsystem plus NvAPI internal/external/subsystem values and the exact conflicting
component in both health and journal logs.

The probe report now records the real socket pathname's numeric owner/group/
mode, and README/continuation guidance clarifies in-place upgrade, diagnostic
collection, and redaction of host/user/unique-GPU identifiers. Pure tests,
ASan/UBSan, the cross-compiled native fixture, and Linux x64/ARM64 warning-as-
error checks passed. Full release build 433 then verified both Windows binaries,
Linux x64/ARM64, hardening/version/private-path checks, private Windows symbols,
and all four exact-manifest 0.20 archives. RTX 5080 hardware acceptance remains
pending.

## 2026-07-16: repair Linux daemon upgrades and VF-state recovery

The reported non-root TUI failure had a deterministic upgrade root cause.
`--service-install` replaced the staged executable but ran `systemctl enable
--now`; systemd does not restart an already-active unit, so the July 9 protocol-
8 daemon and PID survived the July 16 protocol-11 client install. The daemon's
short old response was then read as a current fixed-size body and collapsed to
a generic I/O/offline message. Running the client as root appeared to help only
when it reached a separately restarted/current daemon; supplementary group
membership was not disproved by `SO_PEERCRED`'s primary GID.

Two independent paths explained `family=0`: a sole NVML GPU/NvAPI handle did
not fall back when bus/slot calls succeeded with nonmatching values, and the
NvAPI architecture query ran twice so a transient second failure discarded a
successful first result. The VF stall had separate authority defects: refresh
errors were ignored, cached arrays remained effectively current, discovery was
not retried, and live-frequency monotonicity incorrectly rejected legitimate
applied curves as an ABI mismatch.

Protocol v12 keeps the fixed magic/version prefix and adds daemon identity,
typed GPU health, architecture source/statuses, freshness/recovery state, and
server-derived available mutation domains. Transport reads headers first and
reports version mismatch, EOF, timeout, and truncation with byte counts.
Installation now makes group/reload failures fatal, enables then unconditionally
restarts, uses dependency-free `Type=notify` readiness after replay/listen, and
verifies active state plus an exact version/build/protocol ping.

Linux discovery and VF parsing are extracted into reusable policies. Unique
multi-GPU matching remains strict; a compatible sole-device fallback is
explicitly logged. Architecture is queried once, can fall back to NVML's
documented Pascal through Blackwell values or same-PCI cache, and never grants
write authority by itself. Complete info/status/control reads are parsed and
structurally validated in temporary storage, including versions, masks/counts,
ranges, and ordered voltage topology; live frequency need not be monotonic.
Failure invalidates freshness and triggers one immediate read-only same-PCI
rebind/re-read. Only advertised independent NVML domains can mutate while VF is
degraded; VF/mixed requests and full Reset fail before the first write.
Successful partial Apply merges preserve unrequested durable domains, while a
requested VF domain replaces its complete canonical sparse-point set so removed
points cannot survive only in persisted intent.

The TUI now renders offline, degraded, recovering, and ready distinctly,
preserves the first detailed transport error, disables unavailable VF editing,
and scopes draft attachment by mutation domain. Regression coverage includes
the install runner, protocol/transport classifications, PCI matching and
architecture retention/fallback, atomic VF freshness/validation, mutation
authority, and TUI state. `python build.py --test`, `python build.py --test
--asan`, and warning-as-error Linux x64/ARM64 check builds passed. Final release
build 432 verified both Windows binaries, Linux x64/ARM64, hardening/version/
private-path checks, private Windows symbols, and all four exact-manifest 0.20
archives. The README now documents in-place upgrade without uninstall, group-
session verification, unprivileged use, and degraded-state diagnostics. RTX
5080 hardware acceptance remains pending.

## 2026-07-15: keep tray profile completion presentation-silent

Applying a profile from the tray could flash a ghost of the main window when
the asynchronous service mutation completed. The private trace showed the
hidden intent remained set, but the accepted apply-result render transaction
made the main HWND visible immediately before `WM_PAINT`; the general tray
postcondition then hid it again. The specific Win32 cause is
`DefWindowProc`'s top-level `WM_SETREDRAW(TRUE)` behavior: reenabling redraw
adds `WS_VISIBLE`, so the old suppression pair was not visibility-neutral.

**Superseded 2026-07-31** (see the newest entry): keeping the pair for visible
windows was half a fix — the FALSE half clears `WS_VISIBLE` too, which dropped
an open window out of the taskbar list. The transaction no longer sends either
half to a top-level window in any state.

Top-level coherent rendering now uses a visibility-preserving transaction.
Visible windows retain the existing `WM_SETREDRAW(FALSE/TRUE)` suppression and
one settled synchronous redraw. Hidden windows never send either top-level
redraw-toggle message, never paint synchronously, and receive only deferred
parent/child invalidation for the next explicit show. A pre-paint invariant
also corrects any unexpected nested visibility change before redraw begins.
The same helper covers accepted service envelopes and standalone dynamic VF
control rebuilds; child-control redraw suppression remains unchanged.
General main-window invalidation now also defers `WM_PAINT` while the owner is
hidden or has durable tray-hidden intent.

Tray/hotkey/automatic profile apply and completion are explicitly treated as
presentation-silent operations. The automatic completion branch is isolated in
a guarded handler, and source regressions reject dialog/window creation or
showing, activation/focus/flash calls, console allocation, and helper-process
launches in the apply/completion path. This prevents rule-driven auto-profile
changes from creating a taskbar surface or stealing foreground focus as well as
preventing the observed ghost owner.

The native hidden-HWND fixture proves the old raw redraw pair really
resurrects a hidden top-level window, then proves the new policy updates child
state and leaves the owner hidden. Pure policy and source guards forbid raw
top-level redraw toggles from returning to the service projection or control-
rebuild paths. Focused and ASan+UBSan regressions and Windows x64/ARM64
warning-as-error check builds pass. Full release build 430 verified Windows
GUI/service and Linux x64/ARM64 binaries, private Windows symbols, and all four
exact-manifest 0.20 archives. The private diagnostic log remains local and
uncommitted.

## 2026-07-15: enforce tray-hidden residency beyond SWP_SHOWWINDOW

A repeated selected-GPU reconnect exposed a second Windows visibility path.
The resident GUI kept its durable tray-hidden intent and never entered Green
Curve's tray-reopen function, but its main HWND still ended up visible. The
private trace contained none of the existing `SWP_SHOWWINDOW` rejection or
explicit reopen diagnostics, proving that the pre-show `WM_WINDOWPOSCHANGING`
filter was necessary but not sufficient.

Tray residency is now both a precondition and a postcondition. The existing
filter still removes unsolicited `SWP_SHOWWINDOW`; additionally, the first
main-window message which observes `WS_VISIBLE` while hidden intent remains set
synchronously restores `SW_HIDE`. The corrective path has an explicit
reentrancy guard because `ShowWindow` generates nested window messages. Real
tray/menu or foreground second-launch activation remains unchanged: it clears
hidden intent before showing. Accepted tray activation and corrective message/
style details are now logged so future reports distinguish user activation
from Windows visibility reconstruction.

Pure policy coverage now includes the postcondition and recursion guard. The
native hidden-HWND fixture also force-adds `WS_VISIBLE` without
`SWP_SHOWWINDOW`, sends a real window message, and proves the window is hidden
again before explicit activation. The full regression suite and warning-as-
error Windows x64/ARM64 check build pass, as does the ASan+UBSan suite. Full
release build 429 verified Windows GUI/service and Linux x64/ARM64 binaries,
private Windows symbols, and all four exact-manifest 0.20 archives. The private
diagnostic log remains local and uncommitted.

## 2026-07-15: broad pre-ship review hardens diagnostics and process helpers

A whole-source review, rather than a recent-commit diff, covered the shared
model and policies, Windows GUI/service/runtime, Linux client/daemon/TUI,
persistence, subprocess/platform shims, build tooling, and tests. Static
analysis plus manual source-flow review found a Windows API contract bug:
`StringCchPrintfA` returns an HRESULT, but crash-breadcrumb and GPU apply-summary
cursors treated it as a character count. Successful appends therefore kept a
zero cursor and overwrote earlier diagnostic fields. The new bounded
`gc_appendf` cursor preserves prefixes and saturates safely; compiled tests
cover chaining and truncation, while source guards prevent the old pattern.

The cross-platform capture helper now drains output even after its caller's
buffer fills, reaps/terminates children on every path, and returns success only
for a normal zero exit. A self-spawn fixture covers excess output and an exit-7
child. Controlled-restart PID/handle parsing now rejects signs, whitespace, and
numeric overflow instead of accepting saturated maximum values. Initial DPI
fallback also validates positive results and a non-null screen DC so a rare
resource failure cannot produce zero scaling. No large rewrite or refactor was
introduced; analyzer-only padding/performance warnings without a demonstrated
correctness failure were left unchanged.

Pure/source regressions, ASan+UBSan, and the complete warning-as-error check
matrix passed. Full release build 427 verified Windows GUI/service and Linux
x64/ARM64 binaries, private Windows symbols, and all four exact-manifest 0.20
archives. Physical GPU mutation, service-restart, and display-transition tests
remain release-machine integration checks.

## 2026-07-15: release review closes Linux state and persistence gaps

A targeted pre-ship review found that the new Linux daemon could switch its
single backend while active desired/fan intent still belonged to another GPU.
GET_STATE now rejects that cross-GPU switch until the active owner is Reset,
and the fan worker independently refuses reassertion unless active and selected
identities match. The NVML adapter-0 telemetry fallback no longer becomes a
published selected write identity; the TUI makes the first multi-GPU choice
explicit and queries existing active ownership before honoring a saved target.

Linux profiles now save/load and merge `lock_mode` plus
`lock_tracks_anchor`. Legacy missing modes and contradictory enabled-`NONE`
records migrate to `FLATTEN`; direct enabled-lock/no-mode requests fail at the
daemon boundary with a diagnostic. Clearing a profile also removes app-launch,
logon, selected, and applied references to the slot. Finally, the cell renderer
does not step beyond NUL for truncated UTF-8. Pure regression tests and Linux
x64/ARM64 warning-as-error check builds pass, as does the ASan+UBSan suite. The
full release matrix passed at build 425 with verified Windows GUI/service and
Linux binaries for x64/ARM64, private Windows symbols, and all four exact-
manifest 0.20 archives.

## 2026-07-15: preserve tray residency and fail closed on no-intent recovery

A real controlled GPU recovery proved that the GUI/service process continuity
fix works, but Windows could make the hidden top-level window visible while
reconstructing the display stack without calling Green Curve's tray-reopen
path. The GUI now owns a durable tray-hidden presentation intent. While set,
`WM_WINDOWPOSCHANGING` blocks unsolicited `SWP_SHOWWINDOW`, and display/device/
exact-selected-GPU PnP handling defensively reapplies `SW_HIDE`. Explicit tray
or second foreground-process activation clears the intent before showing.

The same private trace showed that the fresh service correctly published
`activeDesiredValid=0` and zero live GPU offset after the 10-minute proof
lockout withheld automatic reapply. To prevent any pre-invalidation frame or
synthetic native-control notification from looking active, the full READY
projection now runs under one programmatic-edit transaction. A clean editor on
new authority with no active intent is rebased from authoritative live controls
and receives explicit no-active-settings status text. Pure tray-policy tests,
source/order guards, the full regression suite, and the Windows x64 check build
pass. A native hidden-HWND regression additionally proves that an actual
`ShowWindow(SW_SHOW)` is rejected until explicit activation clears hidden
intent. The final build 424 Windows/Linux x64/ARM64 matrix and four 0.20 package
archives also pass. The private diagnostic log remains local and uncommitted.

## 2026-07-15: release 0.20 Linux pseudo-GUI and Windows reconnect completion

Linux now has a dependency-free responsive pseudo-GUI TUI rather than the old
long form. One exact styled cell grid renders and hit-tests fixed header/tabs/
footer plus VF Curve, Fan Curve, and Profiles & Tools pages at compact, medium,
and wide breakpoints (minimum 72x24). It provides a Braille VF graph with
nearest-point selection, absolute target-MHz fields and full base/live/delta/
rule tables, Windows-compatible flatten/hard-pin tri-state semantics, GPU and
memory offsets, low-point exclusion, power limit, custom fan graph/points,
profiles, probe/assets, and live text/JSON exports. Mouse wheel, Page Up/Down,
Ctrl+Page Up/Down, spatial arrows, Tab traversal, numeric typing/stepping, and
hotkeys all operate without a second coordinate model. Changed-row rendering
and synchronized terminal updates avoid whole-screen flicker. Startup adopts
live daemon state; profile load is explicit; Apply/Reset carry accepted service
instance/GPU-generation/topology preconditions. The Linux daemon now publishes
coherent READY envelopes with real multi-adapter identity and controls instead
of fabricating one adapter and permanently reporting DEGRADED. `--dump-live`
and `--json-live` expose every populated point's absolute/base/live/offset/
target/rule values. The layout/rule matrix, pure tests, and x64/ARM64 Linux check
builds pass.
A full release build then passed as build 422 and produced all four verified
0.20 Windows/Linux x64/ARM64 archives with private Windows symbols retained.

A local lifecycle trace established the Windows post-reconnect failure exactly:
the GUI accepted the fresh service instance in RECOVERING at generation 1, then
received a later `DEVICEINSTANCESTARTED` presentation cue and incorrectly armed
a same-instance minimum-generation fence of 2. The recovered service correctly
published READY generation 1, so every envelope was rejected as an old GPU
generation until the GUI restarted. Local PnP now arms that fence only when it
actually retires accepted READY authority. A compiled reducer reproduces the
restart-then-late-start ordering and proves the later READY generation 1 is
accepted. Stable fan controls use idempotent Win32 text/selection/enablement
projection, and tray reopen preserves a coherent cached first frame while an
asynchronous refresh detects real authority changes, eliminating two remaining
sources of faint control redraw. The private diagnostic log was inspected only
locally and was not copied or committed.

## 2026-07-15: make stable disconnected retries flicker-free

The reconnect timer previously called the interactive full-sync entry point on
every probe. With no service installed, each three-second attempt therefore
published `DISCONNECTED -> SYNCING -> DISCONNECTED`, invalidated the main
window, and force-updated owner-drawn service and sharing controls even though
their visible values had not changed. The telemetry backstop could add more
failed probes while the window was visible.

Background reconnect probes now enqueue a full state request without changing
the current presentation. A pure failure-change gate renders only when the
phase, live authority, visible service state, or displayed error actually
changes; changing hidden transport detail while the service is not installed
is presentation-inert. Service/share control text, check state, enablement, and
visibility are projected with change-gated Win32 setters, omit erase and
`UpdateWindow`, and service status refresh no longer redraws unrelated sharing
controls. Explicit tray reopen and user-requested synchronization still enter
the neutral `SYNCING` presentation immediately. Pure tests and source guards
pin the silent-retry and idempotent-projection invariants.

## 2026-07-15: stop reconnect GUI flashing and tray reopen bounce

The initial protocol-v11 GUI integration adopted every READY telemetry envelope
through the same whole-window path as a structural full sync. Once per second it
suppressed redraw, projected unchanged active intent back into edit HWNDs, then
invalidated and erased the parent, children, and non-client frame. `WM_PAINT`
also cleared the physical paint DC before the completed system-memory DIB blit.
This made otherwise coherent state updates visibly flash.

Stable telemetry is now data-only: it updates the accepted live model plus fan
and tray projection without touching the VF editor or whole-window redraw.
Authority, active-intent, topology, forced-refresh, or missing-control changes
still promote the envelope to one redraw-suppressed full transaction. Physical
paint no longer clears before `BitBlt`, and coherent invalidations omit erase
and non-client-frame flags.

Tray activation now honors whether shell notification version 4 was actually
negotiated, rather than accepting both its `NIN_SELECT` events and legacy mouse
events. Repeated activation while visible is idempotent. Duplicate tray-
autostart processes no longer reopen a resident hidden window, while an
explicit second foreground launch routes activation through the resident GUI
thread. Pure callback/render-policy tests and source guards cover these
invariants without sleeps.

## 2026-07-15: make GUI state and GDI reconnect-safe

The service already recovered hardware after selected-GPU disable/enable, but
the GUI could combine snapshot, active-intent, telemetry, and cached `loaded`
values from different device lifetimes. Runtime pipe calls could also complete
out of order or update presentation outside one main-thread transaction. Hiding
and reopening the tray window merely repainted retained claims, while the
compatible GDI bitmap could retain driver-backed state across adapter teardown.

Protocol v11 now puts one complete `ServiceStateEnvelope` in every response:
nonzero per-process service instance, monotonic state revision and GPU
generation, explicit GPU phase, validity mask, topology signature, and explicit
active-intent presence. The service publishes one immutable state atomically.
Exact removal, missed-removal arrival, READY-refresh failure, selected identity
loss/change, and response-time authority loss advance the GPU generation;
`READY` is published only after reacquisition/reapply and a complete successful
refresh. Interactive mutations carry expected instance/generation/topology and
stable GPU identity and are rejected as stale before or after the service
runtime lock. State clients no longer chain snapshot and active-desired reads.

The GUI now reduces immutable completions on the window thread through one
`GuiServiceModel`. Its sole runtime I/O coordinator owns pipe/SCM verification,
full-sync/telemetry scheduling, mutation ordering, and cancellable admin waits.
Connection, presentation, and GPU epochs reject late work; full sync coalesces,
telemetry drops behind higher-priority work, Reset retains precedence, and the
latest valid Apply wins. Tray reopen and exact selected-device PnP immediately
show a neutral syncing/reconnect overlay and enqueue a full sync. `GuiDraft`
owns unsaved edits independently of HWNDs and reattaches only when stable GPU
identity and the complete topology signature match; otherwise it remains
detached and Apply is blocked. Accepted READY state is rendered in one
redraw-suppressed transaction, with every `visibleMap` entry participating in
topology identity. Sparse desired/profile data loaded before the first READY
state remains an unkeyed overlay until it can be rebased onto a coherent live
baseline, preventing omitted fields from becoming empty or default values.

GDI remains the UI renderer but retained surfaces are disposable. The
full-client backbuffer is now a top-down 32-bit system-memory DIB created with a
null reference DC, never a device-compatible bitmap. Selected-device PnP, tray
reopen, display/composition/devnode changes advance a GDI generation and retire
the old surface. Creation/selection/blit failures are checked; paint falls back
to direct drawing and a failed final blit retires the buffer without a repaint
loop.

Regression coverage includes strict v11 layout/validation, reducer generation
and late-instance cases, default-value replacement, topology/draft/queue policy,
hidden Win32 envelope projection/redraw, memory-DIB select/blit, coordinator
shutdown cancellation, and source boundaries preventing worker/transport GUI
mutation or synchronous runtime window calls. Repeated physical Device Manager
cycles and multi-GPU reorder remain explicit release-machine checks because the
automated suite does not disrupt the active display adapter.

## 2026-07-13: prevent false service-unavailable state during GUI mutation

User runtime logs showed a successful 3.75-second protocol-v10 Apply temporarily
rendering "Background service not responding." At +1 second, the GUI fan timer
tried telemetry while the single service pipe was intentionally occupied by the
Apply. The telemetry failure called `sync_fan_ui_from_cached_state()`, whose tray
refresh issued another 500 ms ping; `refresh_background_service_state()` treated
that expected busy timeout as service failure and cleared authoritative state.
The same redundant probe raced the successful completion response.

Fix: `service_health_probe_policy.h` classifies a running installed service plus
this GUI's known active mutation as deferred health, not failure. Fan telemetry
is skipped during that state, tray/other health refreshes preserve the last
proven availability, and a valid mutation response explicitly re-proves service
availability without another ping. SCM stop/removal still transitions to
unavailable, and transport failure is probed after the mutation ends. The
telemetry routine moved to `main_fan_telemetry.cpp`, reducing the pre-existing
oversized runtime module instead of raising its ratchet. Added deterministic
policy tests and source guards; sanitizer regressions and all six x64/ARM64
check targets pass at build 414, followed by the complete four-archive release
rebuild at build 415.

## 2026-07-13: harden mutation workflows and platform compatibility

Implemented the validated release-remediation set without creating an audit
report:

- Windows paths remain UTF-8 internally and cross strict UTF-16 wrappers for
  filesystem, canonical/final path, module/system path, INI/profile,
  enumeration, and CLI/config/diagnostic operations. Existing path-length
  limits remain unchanged.
- Named-pipe identity now comes from a duplicated exact-client impersonation
  token, with PID correlation, medium-integrity gating, and RAII reversion.
  Privileged report capture is separated from caller-impersonated destination
  validation and writing.
- IPC v10 adds 64-bit mutation IDs, bounded deduplication, explicit outcome
  states, timeout result query, and backward-readable persisted correlation.
  Windows GUI Apply/Reset uses one background request plus one latest pending
  request, with Reset precedence and GUI-thread-only state adoption.
- Linux curve composition now handles selective offsets, populated-point low
  exclusion, explicit overrides, flatten/hard-lock precedence, and global-vs-
  curve routing transactionally. Unsupported-family writes remain enabled by
  default and failures roll back.
- Linux fan control uses a pure hysteresis reducer, temperature-derived initial
  target, configured interruptible interval, wake events, and repeated-failure
  return to driver automatic control.
- Linux socket ownership/mode/type was intended to be verified on the bound
  descriptor with a root-only fallback when `greencurve` is absent; the
  2026-07-16 follow-up above proved that this checked the wrong sockfs inode and
  replaced it with filesystem-path verification. The TUI uses a parent
  supervisor for normal terminal restoration after child fatal signals.
- Split GUI capture/data-path/Linux mutation implementation into focused shards
  and removed only the two satisfied source-size ratchets.

Regression coverage was expanded in `build.py` for Unicode paths, operation and
GUI queue state machines, Linux curve/fan/state policies, privileged-boundary
source ordering, socket verification, and terminal supervision. Native live-GPU
and pseudo-terminal/token race validation remains a release-machine activity;
deterministic logic and compile/source boundaries are covered locally.

## 2026-07-13: bump version to 0.19.2

Build 412. Version bumped from 0.19.1 to 0.19.2.

## 2026-07-13: fix OC proof stamp reset preventing 2nd GPU driver recovery; GUI stale state on reconnect

Build 411. Bug: second GPU device disable/enable (Device Manager suspend/resume)
did not restore OC/UV/fan settings. First disable/enable worked. GUI showed wrong
active profile state and visual corruption after reconnect.

Root cause: `service_lifecycle_attempt_driver_restore()` in
`source/main_service_lifecycle_apply.cpp:732` unconditionally called
`service_record_oc_apply_stamp()` on success, resetting the 10-minute awake-time
stabilization clock to zero. A second driver recovery within 10 minutes failed
the check in `service_auto_restore_allowed_after_driver_event()`.

Additionally, three GUI state issues compounded the problem:
1. `populate_global_controls()` (`main_runtime_gpu.cpp:322`) unconditionally set
   `serviceSnapshotAuthoritative = false`, undoing the authoritative flag even
   after a valid snapshot was just applied — preventing `sync_applied_profile_
   from_service_metadata()` from recognizing the active profile.
2. `clear_service_authoritative_state()` (`main_state_sync.cpp`) did not zero
   `appliedCurveMHz[128]`, leaving stale baseline data that corrupted fan-only
   apply detection after service reconnect.
3. `refresh_curve()` (`ui_main_window.cpp`) never called `sync_applied_profile_
   from_service_metadata()` after applying the service snapshot, so `applied_slot`
   in config.ini was never updated from the fresh service state.

Fixes:
- Preserve mature OC proof stamp on driver recovery (mirror standby proof path):
  capture proof before write via `service_capture_mature_oc_apply_proof()`,
  restore it on success via `service_restore_mature_oc_apply_proof()`, fall back
  to fresh stamp only when no mature proof exists.
- Clear `appliedCurveMHz` in `clear_service_authoritative_state()`.
- Only clear `serviceSnapshotAuthoritative` in `populate_global_controls()` when
  service is unavailable or no snapshot is loaded.
- Call `sync_applied_profile_from_service_metadata()` after snapshot apply in
  `refresh_curve()`.
- Enhanced debug logging: `proof=%s priorAgeMs=%llu` in driver recovery line.

Files: `source/main_service_lifecycle_apply.cpp`, `source/main_state_sync.cpp`,
`source/main_runtime_gpu.cpp`, `source/ui_main_window.cpp`

## 2026-07-13: fix scheduled-task logon handoff silently failing on boot (auth LUID race)

Build 409. Bug: on some Windows configurations, the scheduled-task logon handoff
(`--logon-start`) produced `identity superseded before config resolve: session
identity changed (session=1 expectedAuth=572473 currentAuth=572531)` and the
profile was never applied automatically on boot. Opening the GUI manually
applied the profile immediately.

Root cause: `service_verify_active_session_identity()` compared `authenticationId`
(LUID from `TOKEN_STATISTICS.AuthenticationId`) extracted from two different
token sources — `OpenProcessToken(taskPid)` at handoff time vs
`WTSQueryUserToken(sessionId)` at apply time. On certain Windows configurations
(Fast Startup, specific session-manager timing), these return different auth
LUIDs even though `sessionId` and user `sid` are identical. The strict equality
check in `service_lifecycle_identity_equal()` then returned `SUPERSEDED`
(terminal), clearing the pending logon state.

Fix:
- `service_lifecycle_policy.h`: added
  `service_lifecycle_identity_equal_session_and_user()` helper that compares
  only `sessionId` + `sid` (ignoring the volatile `authenticationId`).
- `main_service_sessions.cpp::service_verify_active_session_identity()`: when
  `sessionId` + `sid` match but `authenticationId` differs, accept the current
  identity as `MATCH` (log the refresh at debug level) instead of returning
  `SUPERSEDED`.
- `main_service_lifecycle_apply.cpp::service_lifecycle_attempt_logon()`: after
  identity check passes, detect auth LUID drift and update
  `g_serviceLifecycleState.pendingLogonIdentity.authenticationId` + local
  `identity` to the live-session value. This ensures all subsequent checks
  (`logon_still_current`, `authorize_logon_write`, context-identity comparison)
  use the correct auth LUID.

Safety (no security reduction): the `authenticationId` LUID comparison was
redundant defense-in-depth.  Three independent mechanisms already prevent
applying a profile to the wrong session/user:
1. `WTS_SESSION_LOGOFF` cancels pending logon state on any logoff.
2. The lifecycle reducer replaces old pending state when a new LOGON arrives.
3. `sessionId` + `SID` are still strictly enforced — cross-user (Fast User
   Switch) and cross-session misapplication are impossible.
The auth LUID relaxed check is annotated with this rationale in both
`service_lifecycle_policy.h` and `main_service_sessions.cpp` so future
auditors see why it is safe.

## 2026-07-13: auto-save [gpu] section for single-GPU systems to fix share with all users (revised)

Build 404 (build 405). Bug: clicking "share slot N with all users" on a
single-GPU system produced exit code 1 with "Cannot publish slot N without its
selected GPU".

Root cause: `save_configured_gpu_selection_atomic()` was never called because
the GPU selector combo is disabled for single adapters (the user cannot
interact with it). The `[gpu]` section was never written to config.ini.

When the share/unshare CLI helper loaded the config, `config_section_has_keys(srcPath,
"gpu")` returned false and the share was rejected.

First attempted fix (build 404): placed auto-save in `entry.cpp:WinMain()` after
`refresh_global_state()`. **Failed** because the background service is NOT
available at that point (`backgroundServiceAvailable=0`), so `adapterCount=0`
and the condition never fired.

Corrected fix (build 405): moved auto-save to `apply_service_snapshot_to_app()`
in `main_state_sync.cpp`, right after `LeaveCriticalSection(&g_appLock)`.
This location runs when the service snapshot is applied (with valid adapter
info), runs outside `g_appLock`, and is idempotent via
`config_section_has_keys` guard. Bumped `main_state_sync.cpp` ratchet from 1215
to 1229, restored `entry.cpp` ratchet to 960.

**Third bug (build 406):** The `[gpu]` section was created, but
`save_profile_to_config` in `config_profiles.cpp:471` called
`refresh_service_snapshot_and_active_desired` which did a second
`service_client_ping` from the CLI helper. This second ping timed out
("Timed out during reading service response"), so the machine config
publication failed with "Background service is not available".

Fix: changed the `refresh_service_snapshot_and_active_desired` call from
fatal to a logged warning (`debug_log`). The profile data is already fully
loaded into the `desired` parameter, so the service refresh is unnecessary.
`python build.py --test` passes.

**Fourth bug (build 407-408):** The shared profile was published successfully
and the `[gpu]` section existed, but auto-apply at logon still didn't work.
The service loaded the machine config profile but skipped all curve points
as "hidden" -> "Profile is missing VF curve points".

Root cause: `save_profile_to_config` wrote `pointN_visible` via
`is_curve_point_visible_in_gui(i)` which reads `g_app.curve[i].volt_uV`.
In the CLI helper (where machine config publication runs), `g_app.curve` is
zero-initialized, so ALL points got `pointN_visible=0`. The service's
`load_curve_points_explicit_from_section` then skipped every point with
`visible=0`.

Fix: when `g_app.curve[i].volt_uV == 0` (no live GPU data), fall back to
`desired->hasCurvePoint[i]` for visibility. Applied at both the
`profileN_curve` section (line 630) and the legacy `[curve]` section (line
719) in `config_profiles.cpp`.

Also clarified the logon dropdown labels: "Admin default: Shared profile N"
-> "Use admin's default (Shared profile N)", "Shared profile N (admin)" ->
"Always use: Shared profile N". Bumped version to 0.19.1.
`python build.py --test` passes.
