## 2026-07-12: retain recovery until DXGI reports the user-mode adapter set

Build 402 completed the controlled restart after a Device Manager GPU restart:
the new service validated the nonce, parent, snapshot, proof, and SCM start, but
its initial NVAPI initialization returned `NVAPI_NVIDIA_DEVICE_NOT_FOUND`.
Configuration Manager reported the selected device `STARTED` eight seconds
later while NVAPI was still unavailable, and no later PnP edge woke the pending
restore. A read-only `--dump` request several minutes later initialized NVAPI,
posted the existing serialized readiness cue, and the same service immediately
restored the full intent (GPU and memory offsets, power limit, clock lock, and
fan curve). This isolated the defect to a missing late user-mode readiness edge;
the authorization, persisted intent, proof, GPU selection, and write path were
all valid.

The service now registers `IDXGIFactory7::RegisterAdaptersChangedEvent` before
the lifecycle worker reports ready and waits on that event in the same
long-lived worker. DXGI adapter-set changes wake the still-pending recovery at
the user-mode display layer even if Device Manager re-enable happens minutes or
hours later. There is no deadline, polling loop, sleep, timer, per-event thread,
or hardware access in the notification registration. Exact selected-device PnP
and authenticated client readiness signals remain as complementary paths, and
failure to obtain DXGI notifications is logged explicitly.
`python build.py --test --asan`, Windows x64/ARM64 warnings-as-errors checks,
and the complete six-binary/four-archive release build pass at build 403.
Native delayed re-enable retesting remains required on the reporting machine.

## 2026-07-12: SCM owns final controlled STOPPED; retain actionable symbols

Build 400 reached the nonce/helper commit after a Device Manager GPU restart,
but the service then remained stopped. The old service had published
`SERVICE_STOPPED` immediately before `ExitProcess`. The helper correctly waited
for the pinned process exit, but that self-published status could already be
visible while SCM was still tearing down the old service dispatcher, allowing
the sole `StartServiceW` attempt to race SCM generation cleanup.

Both serialized and poisoned-runtime controlled exits now publish
`SERVICE_STOP_PENDING` and exit with the dedicated code. SCM alone publishes
the authoritative `SERVICE_STOPPED` transition after dispatcher teardown; the
existing notification-driven helper waits for exactly that transition before
revalidating authorization and making its one start attempt. Source-order
regressions pin both exit paths.

The earlier helper access violation produced only a Windows Error Reporting
event because helper dispatch preceded crash-filter installation. Helper mode
now installs the unhandled filter first. Bounded minidumps include thread info,
unloaded modules, handles, referenced memory, process/thread records, and the
virtual-memory map. Windows x64 builds emit structurally verified matching PDBs
under `dist/symbols/windows-x64`; Windows ARM64 builds emit verified private
DWARF `.debug` images because Zig's ARM64 PE linker has no PDB support. Symbol
artifacts and private source paths are excluded from release payloads.
`python build.py --test`, the ASan suite, Windows x64/ARM64 warnings-as-errors
checks, and the complete six-binary/four-archive release build pass at build
402. Native Device Manager recovery retesting remains required on the reporting
machine after repairing the stopped installed service with build 402.

## 2026-07-12: keep controlled-recovery helper out of service user-path startup

A fresh Device Manager disable/re-enable trace showed valid stale-driver
evidence, a persisted nonce authorization, and then helper handshake failure.
The Windows Application log identified an access violation in the short-lived
`greencurve-service.exe --recovery-restart-helper` process at that exact time.
The previous diagnostics change had routed this deliberately minimal helper
through `service_resolve_active_user_paths_for_startup()`, adding WTS token,
profile, Known Folder/Shell, directory, config, and global path-cache startup
before the helper could validate the authorization and signal its ready event.

The helper no longer enters that normal `service_main` initialization surface.
Its parent now records the exact helper exit code when the process wins the
handshake wait, so future pre-handshake exits remain diagnosable without making
the helper perform user/config logging setup. A source regression guard forbids
the full path resolver in helper dispatch and pins exit-code capture.
`python build.py --test`, the ASan suite, and Windows x64/ARM64 warnings-as-errors
check builds pass. The complete six-binary/four-archive release rebuild passes
at build 400.

## 2026-07-12: controlled GPU recovery accepts PID-ambiguous SCM stop-pending

A Device Manager GPU disable/re-enable produced a corroborated stale-driver
fault, a durable recovery snapshot, and a validated restart helper, but the old
service exited and remained stopped. The helper queried SCM after the dedicated
parent exit and rejected the transitional status because `dwProcessId` did not
equal the old PID. This was an invalid assumption: `QueryServiceStatusEx`
explicitly does not guarantee a valid process ID while `SERVICE_STOP_PENDING`.

`service_classify_controlled_recovery_scm_stop_state()` now treats
`STOP_PENDING` as the sole PID-ambiguous waitable transition after the helper
has verified the pinned parent's dedicated exit. `STOPPED` proceeds immediately;
other non-stopped states still have to name the exact old generation, so an
intervening process remains fail-closed. The helper still uses one alertable
`NotifyServiceStatusChangeW` wait, revalidates the nonce/snapshot immediately
before its only `StartServiceW` call, and never polls or sleeps.

The helper now initializes the existing serialized debug logger before internal
dispatch and resolves the active user's debug configuration, so parent-exit,
SCM-transition, authorization, and start-attempt failures survive in the same
diagnostic log. Pure tests cover `STOPPED`, `STOP_PENDING` with zero/stale PID,
matching old-generation state, wrong generation, and missing expected identity.
`python build.py --test` and Windows x64/ARM64 warnings-as-errors check builds
pass, as do the ASan suite and full six-binary/four-archive build 399 rebuild.

## 2026-07-12: make auto-profile owner-draw checkbox state explicit

The dark-theme conversion changed the auto-profile controls from native
`BS_AUTOCHECKBOX` to `BS_OWNERDRAW` but continued to use
`BM_GETCHECK`/`BM_SETCHECK`. Button type bits are mutually exclusive, so an
owner-draw button is not a native checkbox and Windows did not reliably retain
that state. A click could repeatedly derive the same next value, making the
visual and eventual Save result appear unchanged or indeterminate.

The dialog now owns `UiCheckboxState` for Enable, Pause, and every rule-focus
control. Populate, draw, click, and capture all use this one model; clicks log
the previous/new state and synchronously redraw the control. Pure get/set/toggle
tests (returns 735–737) and source guards forbid returning to native BM state on
owner-draw controls. `python build.py --test` and Windows x64/ARM64 check builds
pass, as do the ASan suite and full six-binary/four-archive build 398 rebuild.

## 2026-07-12: restore/center Windows UI, unify dark controls, and harden shared profiles

The main window used `CW_USEDEFAULT`, so Windows chose a slightly top-left-
shifted position. It now centers in the cursor monitor's work area on first
launch and persists/restores a validated per-user normal rectangle thereafter.
Late VF-population growth preserves the existing center. The placement uses a
version-last commit marker and detailed startup/save geometry diagnostics.

Ordinary checkboxes had two different sizing formulas: the unlabeled service
control resolved smaller than the labeled share control. One 14-logical-pixel
metric and shared owner-draw renderer now cover both. The auto-profile dialog
uses that renderer for all checkboxes, explicitly toggles owner-draw state,
themes opened combo listboxes with the dark input palette, and centers/clamps
over its owner.

The multi-user audit found a real reliability bug: logon resolution required a
per-user config and `[gpu]` section before it chose any source, so a fresh or
restricted account could never receive an otherwise valid machine default.
Published machine slots now include `[profileN_gpu]` with the administrator's
stable PCI identity. Publication holds the cross-session lock, invalidates the
settings/curve/fan/GPU data under a durable `publishing` marker first, verifies
identity readback, and commits visibility last. Crashes/partial failures leave
the slot unavailable. Logon resolves profile source before GPU config:
personal content uses per-user `[gpu]`; shared/default content uses the slot
binding. Restricted manual Apply likewise ignores both client settings and
client target. Legacy missing bindings remain compatible only on a proven
single-GPU system; malformed or multi-GPU-ambiguous cases fail closed.

Pure regressions cover centering, clamping, center-preserving growth, checkbox
metrics, and stable shared-GPU INI round-trip. Source guards pin dialog theming,
placement commits, fail-closed slot coupling, and both restricted-user paths.
`python build.py --test`, `python build.py --test --asan`, and Windows x64/ARM64
check builds pass. The full six-binary/four-archive release rebuild passed at
build 397; native visual and real alternate-account testing remain recommended
on the reporting machine.

## 2026-07-12: selected saved profile no longer masquerades as live GPU state

Captured second-launch logs showed a correct stock service snapshot followed by
`startup selected profile restore`, which loaded slot 2's saved 475 MHz GPU,
3000 MHz memory, custom fan, and locked VF intent into the fields without
applying it. The graph remained live, creating the reported contradiction. The
root cause was the GUI-only selected-slot restore policy, not NVAPI/NVML
readback.

With app-start apply disabled, startup now repaints the complete editor from the
service snapshot and leaves `selected_slot` as storage/UI selection only. Saved
intent is loaded only by explicit **Load**; an enabled app-start slot and the
service-owned logon path keep their existing semantics. Diagnostics record the
snapshot result, selected/saved slot, active service source/slot, all global
controls, and lock state while explicitly noting that the saved slot was not
loaded. A pure startup-source policy plus source/order guards covers disabled,
invalid, enabled, and logon cases and prevents the old restore path returning.
Verification passed `python build.py --test`, `python build.py --test --asan`,
the Windows x64/ARM64 check build, wiki link/source-anchor consistency checks,
and the full four-target release rebuild at build 395.

## 2026-07-12: grow after late VF population and repaint scrolling atomically

Real 4K/150%-DPI logs showed that the empty startup state selected a
1770x1086 client. Installing/connecting the service then populated 78 visible
VF points, but only rebuilt the child controls. The unchanged outer window fell
to a 1744x1060 viewport against 1770x1236 content: the vertical scrollbar took
26 physical pixels from the width and unnecessarily induced the horizontal
scrollbar. Manually enlarging the window removed both, confirming the populated
layout itself was sound and that top-level size reconciliation was missing.

Dynamic VF rebuild now performs one-way content growth toward the populated
preferred client size, clamps it to the current monitor work area, and never
shrinks a user-enlarged window. Minimized/maximized growth is deferred until a
normal restore. Diagnostics record the reason, point count, DPI, current,
preferred and work-area geometry, target, and whether it changed.

Thumb scrolling previously reran hundreds of child `SetWindowPos` calls and
invalidated only the parent without erasing, allowing DWM to present transient
stale pixels mixed with moved native controls. Scrolling now moves pixels and
children atomically, erases exposed regions, and synchronously redraws the full
settled parent/child frame. The compiled regression reproduces the captured
144-DPI/78-point geometry and verifies six columns, 13 rows, preferred graph
height, no overflow, no shrink, and work-area clamping.

Verification passed `python build.py --test`, `python build.py --test --asan`,
the Windows x64/ARM64 check build, wiki link/source-anchor consistency checks,
and the full four-target release rebuild at build 394. Native populated-control
visual confirmation remains pending because the local service was removed.

## 2026-07-12: make the main window responsive and lossless at custom DPI

A 3440x1440 user at custom 140% scaling showed the fixed-height main window
behind the taskbar. With 87 visible VF points, the old six-column grid plus a
fixed 420-logical-pixel graph required about 1012 logical client pixels; scaling
that entire minimum made it physically impossible to fit the monitor work area.
`WM_GETMINMAXINFO` nevertheless enforced the oversized minimum, and `WM_SIZE`
only repositioned the bottom panel.

The main window now has one pure physical-pixel layout plan. VF columns adapt to
width, graph height flexes from 260 to 520 logical pixels, and the initial/active
window is clamped to the selected monitor's work area. If minimum readable
geometry cannot fit, the complete content canvas scrolls horizontally and/or
vertically rather than squeezing, hiding, or clipping a feature group. Standard
scrollbars converge iteratively, mouse-wheel input works in both axes, and Tab
focus scrolls off-screen controls into view. Display/work-area changes and
Per-Monitor-V2 `WM_DPICHANGED` rebuild fonts and relayout the full window.

Default diagnostics record plan dimensions, columns/rows, graph height,
overflow, work-area clamps, DPI transitions, scrolling, and deferred-placement
fallbacks. Control creation and rebuild lifecycle were split into dedicated
shards so the existing oversized UI files did not grow.

Compiled regression coverage includes the reported custom-140% geometry,
wide-column reflow, impossible 300%-DPI overflow, loading/empty state, and 800
DPI/viewport combinations, including every one of the 87 populated point-cell
bounds. A fractional-DPI regression caught and fixed a real four-pixel drift
between combined-offset scaling and individually positioned controls.

## 2026-07-12: make VF lock clicks one-shot and state-coherent

The earlier lock-checkbox fix correctly restricted the ID-only command branch,
but its documented `BN_SETFOCUS` explanation was not supported: these
`BS_OWNERDRAW` controls do not set `BS_NOTIFY`. Owner-draw buttons do emit a
distinct `BN_DBLCLK`, and the old handler accepted every notification. More
importantly, source-string guards could not prove that one physical gesture
caused one transition or that startup synchronization did not change NONE to
FLATTEN between press and release.

Lock input now uses a pure activation policy plus a dedicated GUI shard. Mouse
and Space presses capture the exact lock model; release-time `BN_CLICKED` can
consume that gesture once. Duplicate/wrong-control commands and state changes
before release fail safe. `WM_LBUTTONDBLCLK` and its trailing release are both
suppressed, while unarmed accessibility/`BM_CLICK` activation remains valid.
Every notification, decision, press/current stamp, gesture boundary, and
subclass-install failure is logged. The tri-state mutation has one entry point.

Compiled regressions cover all transitions and decisions. A real hidden Win32
`BS_OWNERDRAW | WS_TABSTOP` button fixture proves distinct `BN_CLICKED` and
`BN_DBLCLK` delivery. Source guards pin routing, diagnostics, centralized
mutation, and double-click-pair handling. UI code was split into
`ui_lock_checkbox.cpp` to keep existing oversized shards below their ratchets.
The README/wiki debug-log filename was also corrected to
`greencurve_debug.txt`.

Verification passed `python build.py --test`, `python build.py --test --asan`,
the Windows x64/ARM64 check build, wiki link/stale-claim checks, and the full
four-target release rebuild at build 391.

## 2026-07-12: preserve mature proof across successful standby restore

A normal standby restore still bypasses the driver-recovery proof gate and
reapplies complete in-memory intent once. It now snapshots an already mature,
same-boot proof before the hardware boundary, retains mandatory pre-write proof
invalidation, and republishes that exact stamp only after the complete restore
succeeds. This prevents ordinary standby from unnecessarily resetting settings
that had already survived 10 awake minutes. Immature, missing, invalid, and
cross-boot proofs start fresh after successful restore; failed writes retain
the existing lockout behavior. Pure boundary tests cover 9:59 versus 10:00,
and source-order guards pin capture-before-write and restore-after-success.
Verification passed `python build.py --test`, the Windows x64/ARM64 check, and
the full four-target release rebuild at build 389.

## 2026-07-11: stable same-boot proof for Device Manager/driver recovery

Live Device Manager suspend/resume logs showed the selected GPU did enter the
validated nonce-bound fresh-process recovery path, but the new service rejected
a four-hour-old successful Apply proof as belonging to another boot and latched
automatic restoration off. The proof used
`SystemTimeOfDayInformation.BootTime`, a wall-clock-derived value that can move
after clock correction within one actual Windows boot.

The stability proof, controlled-restart authorization, and recovery-spam ledger
now carry both halves of the per-boot
`SystemBootEnvironmentInformation.BootIdentifier`. Their formats are versioned
forward, proof mismatch diagnostics include both boot identities and awake
clocks, and source guards prevent reintroduction of BootTime authority.
Non-empty obsolete tick history cannot be safely rebound to the new identity
and therefore remains fail-closed until explicit Apply; empty history is
discarded. The power handler also recognizes legacy critical resume and the
lifecycle worker logs exact suspend/resume event values. Pure tests cover both
BootIdentifier halves. Verification passed `python build.py --test`, the
Windows x64/ARM64 check build, and the full four-target `python build.py`
release rebuild at build 388.

## 2026-07-11: Linux transaction safety and 0.19 release hardening

Linux Apply, Reset, and startup replay now use a fail-closed transaction:
capability preflight and a live rollback snapshot precede a durable `prepared`
record, writes are ordered and read back, the first failure rolls back touched
domains, and only complete success publishes a checksummed `active` record.
Rollback that cannot prove the prior locked-clock policy moves to `uncertain`
and blocks unattended replay. State persistence uses a root-owned same-directory
temp, file fsync, atomic rename, and directory fsync; corrupt, legacy, prepared,
uncertain, and GPU-mismatched records never write hardware at startup.

Linux enumerates all NVML adapters and maps NvAPI by PCI location plus
device/subsystem identifiers. Multi-GPU writes require a unique persisted BDF
from `--gpu` or the TUI selector; ambiguous/stale/cross-API identities retain
telemetry but cannot write. Unknown-family best-effort support remains enabled
after identity resolution.

Release 0.19 makes `VERSION` mandatory and injected, makes `--check --arch all`
build both architectures, compiles ARM64 translation units separately with LTO
disabled, and gates final binaries on PE/ELF hardening plus BTI/PAC/AUT. Release
archives use an exact allowlist and are read back after 7-Zip creation, so Zig's
`main.lib` cannot ship. The known x64 CFG shim collision is the only accepted
duplicate-symbol diagnostic; x64 CET absence remains a documented llvm-mingw
limitation. *(Refined 2026-07-25: what is absent is the shadow-stack CET_COMPAT
PE opt-in, not the `endbr64` instrumentation, which is present on our code.)* Pure tests cover state corruption, PCI reordering/missing/duplicate
identity, architecture selection, and unexpected package payload rejection.
Final verification passed `python build.py --test`, `python build.py --test
--asan`, the full OS/architecture check matrix, x64 UBSan checks, clang analyzer,
Flawfinder level 4/5, Gitleaks, OSV, and `python build.py` at build 387. All four
0.19 archive manifests and SHA-256 files were read back successfully.

## 2026-07-11: persistence and automatic-profile reliability hardening

Profile loads now hold the cross-session config lock across every related INI
section, scalar writes flush and validate readback, and explicit-format unlocked
VF curves no longer lose legitimate custom points to a legacy cleanup heuristic.
Auto-profile rules, stale-rule removal, and hotkeys commit in one atomic rewrite.

GPU selection now persists and revalidates a stable PCI identity rather than
trusting an enumeration ordinal; missing, ambiguous, or legacy multi-GPU
selection fails closed until explicit reselection. Eligible explicit logon
profile choices likewise stay pending when unavailable instead of unexpectedly
falling back to the machine default.

Lifecycle readiness now moves ancestor directory watches inward and recovers a
failed watch rearm, WTS enumeration failure cannot substitute a console session,
and the sticky auto-restore lockout has a protected HKLM fallback. GUI, CLI,
app-start, and foreground apply paths validate the configured GPU immediately
before mutation and surface persistence failures rather than continuing with
uncommitted state.

---

## 2026-07-11: eliminate repeated GUI profile INI reloads

The first foreground-I/O reduction removed redundant service hardware/SCM
work, but the GUI still measured about 5.17 MB/s and 229 reads per second while
visible. `GetProcessIoCounters` confirmed these were real GUI reads. The root
cause was `apply_service_snapshot_to_app()` invoking
`sync_applied_profile_from_service_metadata()` on every one-second telemetry
snapshot; ownership validation reloaded the full saved INI profile through many
small Win32 profile reads even when nothing changed.

Ownership synchronization now caches its completed decision using service
authority/source/slot, the complete active intent, config path, and cheap exact
file presence/size/last-write metadata. Unchanged telemetry is an immediate
cache hit with no INI reads. Real in-process or external config changes alter
the stamp and force a fresh comparison. Failed persistence is never cached.
The cache helpers live in `config_profile_sync_cache.cpp` to keep the profile UI
shard within the project's size band.

---

## 2026-07-11: visible-GUI device/IPC I/O reduction

System Informer showed about 5 MB/s attributed I/O only while the GUI window
was visible. Logs confirmed each one-second telemetry request repeated the full
`hardware_initialize()` adapter/NVAPI probe and then posted a lifecycle
prerequisite wake, even though the service fan worker already maintained the
temperature/fan cache.

Routine telemetry now serves the existing cache and initializes hardware only
when bootstrapping the first unavailable snapshot. It no longer wakes lifecycle
restoration. Named-pipe authentication remains fail-closed, but successful SCM
and binary-path verification is cached for the exact service process generation
using both PID and process creation time; a restart cannot reuse it. This
removes repeated SCM/registry/image queries without weakening reconnect or
server-identity handling.

---

## 2026-07-11: exact-file filtering for logon config readiness

Live logs exposed a service CPU/I/O loop after logon: the pending profile
resolver watched the config's parent directory, while the debug log lived in
that same directory. Every diagnostic write signaled the broad directory
watch; the worker interpreted it as a config edit, resolved and logged again,
and thereby retriggered itself roughly 50 times per second.

Directory notifications remain necessary to observe atomic config replacement,
but are now filtered using the exact config file's presence, volume/file ID,
size, and last-write time. Sibling-file activity merely rearms the notification
and does not run lifecycle attempts. Source regression guards pin both halves of
the fix. The live suspended service was not modified during diagnosis.

---

## 2026-07-11: final startup/config and poisoned-runtime race closure

The authenticated task handoff may now arrive before WTS marks the session
active: the pipe captures `{session, SID, authentication LUID}` and the worker
retains it until final active-session revalidation. Config watches are armed
before one interruptible, cross-process transaction reads selected GPU, account
and shared selectors, protected policy, and profile data. Atomic replacement or
profile-mount gaps remain pending; GUI reconciliation preserves saved selectors,
the handoff task, and tray Run value on indeterminate reads.

Exact selected-device CM callbacks publish a monotonic event generation and
removed flag immediately. Every Apply/Reset/lifecycle write rechecks it plus
pending logoff/removal state at the final boundary, so driver recovery dominates
coincident standby/logon work. Runtime-mutex abandonment after a corroborated
NVIDIA VEH fault and fan-call wedges use only the already-durable snapshot and
nonce helper; they never wait on or reuse the poisoned hardware mutex. Missing
proof/helper/ledger state exits ordinarily with sticky lockout and zero replay.

---

## 2026-07-11: final fail-closed write boundary, ownership transitions, and service sharding

Automatic restore now crosses one explicit hardware-write boundary. Zero-write
identity/config/GPU validation completes first; immediately before the first
real mutation the backend durably invalidates the previous stability proof. If
that invalidation fails, no GPU write occurs. Once mutation begins, failure is
terminal and latches automatic restoration off. Lifecycle worker creation is a
startup prerequisite, and unexpected later worker death also latches lockout
before an ordinary, non-replaying SCM availability restart.

Named personal/shared profiles now replace active ownership instead of merging
omitted fields from another account or profile. Their transition request resets
only omitted controls that Green Curve previously owned, while the published
active intent remains the exact new profile. Ad-hoc partial Apply requests still
merge, and standby/controlled recovery reconstruct a sparse full restore from
the resulting exact in-memory intent.

The large service implementation surfaces are thin aggregators over focused
shards: host/pipe/request policy; runtime identity/fan/apply; client connection,
typed commands, admin helpers, and machine config; plus separate lifecycle
events/apply/worker, controlled restart, proof clock, ledger, and selected-GPU
PnP units. Protocol v9 declarations live in `service_protocol.h`.

Durable documentation was reconciled with that layout and policy. In particular,
the GPU-backend page no longer presents SCM failure-action startup replay as the
current recovery design, startup memory-offset uncertainty is documented as
diagnostic-only, user-facing registry disclosure includes the opt-in tray Run
value, and test documentation distinguishes executable pure-policy coverage
from SCM/helper/token/snapshot behavior currently pinned by source/order guards.

---

## 2026-07-11: bounded logon handoff separated from resident tray startup

The canonical handoff task has a `PT3M` execution limit because its client may
wait up to 120 seconds for SCM `RUNNING`. Letting that same scheduled process
remain as the tray GUI meant Task Scheduler could terminate the resident GUI at
the limit. `--logon-start` now always sends the settings-free authenticated
handoff and exits; a per-user HKCU Run value independently launches
`--tray-start --config ...` when `[startup] start_program_on_logon=1`. The
startup checkbox is therefore truly tray-only, while a selected logon profile
independently controls the handoff task.

Task XML classification now preserves only known functional legacy variants:
valid delay, `HighestAvailable`, old `PT0S`, and omitted safe schema defaults.
Extra triggers/actions, repetition/restart, battery/idle/network gating,
unsupported/too-short execution limits, and non-`IgnoreNew` instance policies
are broken and require repair. New executable fixtures cover those cases.

Asynchronous startup repair carries a monotonic generation. Its completion
re-reads `logon_slot`/`logon_shared_slot` on the UI thread; if a user changed
config while the worker was active, the stale generation schedules a fresh
reconciliation and cannot move the combo/task back to the older choice.

---

## 2026-07-11: Controlled-recovery SCM stop synchronization

The restart helper no longer assumes that termination of the old service
process means SCM has already published `SERVICE_STOPPED`. After validating the
dedicated parent exit code, it subscribes with
`NotifyServiceStatusChangeW`, waits alertably within the nonce's remaining
freshness window without polling or timing sleeps, and keeps the terminated
process object open so the expected PID cannot be recycled during SCM-state
verification. It rejects another process generation, deletion/error/timeout,
revalidates the protected authorization, and makes exactly one `StartServiceW`
call. Failure clears the authorization and snapshot without a hardware write.

Explicit recovery-history acknowledgement also fails closed when either the
current ledger path or the legacy migration path cannot be resolved; a path
failure can no longer be mistaken for a successful history clear.

---

## 2026-07-11: Cross-session atomic INI locking

The prior `Local\\GreenCurveConfigMutex` created unrelated locks in the
interactive GUI session and the service's session 0, so its documented
cross-process serialization did not actually cover GUI/service concurrency.
The versioned `Global\\GreenCurveConfigMutex-v2` uses an explicit protected DACL
that grants authenticated callers only mutex wait/release rights, plus a medium
integrity label so an unelevated GUI can open a SYSTEM-created object. Security-
descriptor creation, mutex creation/open, timeout, and wait failures all fail
closed.

Whole-file profile save and clear now retain that storage lock for the complete
read/modify/write, atomic rename, and Win32 INI-cache flush. This prevents an
earlier snapshot of `logon_slot`/`logon_shared_slot` from overwriting a newer
atomic logon selection. Atomic section replacement also compares INI section
names case-insensitively; otherwise a hand-edited `[Profiles]` survived beside a
new `[profiles]` and Win32 kept reading the stale first section. Deterministic
fixtures and source guards cover the cross-session mutex access contract,
mixed-case section matching, cache flush, and whole-RMW guards.

---

## 2026-07-10: Reliable fail-closed Windows settings restoration

The reported profile-2 logon failure was reproduced from the user's task and
log: the correct least-privilege task ran while the service pipe was ready, but
`--logon-start` only logged a handoff and exited. If Windows omitted the WTS
notification, the service received no event and applied nothing. Elevation was
not the fix; the user's 30-second delay merely changed notification timing.

Protocol v9 adds settings-free `SERVICE_CMD_LOGON_HANDOFF`, typed
`ServiceApplyOrigin`, profile source/slot ownership, lifecycle result, and
lockout reason. Every `--logon-start` path waits for service `RUNNING` through
SCM status notification (maximum 120 seconds), sends the authenticated handoff,
and exits nonzero on failure. WTS and task events coalesce by `{WTS session ID,
SID, TokenStatistics.AuthenticationId}`; logoff clears matching pending/debounce
state so a reused numeric session and account with a new authentication LUID is
a new login.

`service_lifecycle_policy.h` is a pure reducer driven by one long-lived service
worker. Control handlers only post/coalesce events. Pending logon intent has no
arbitrary deadline, waits only on real identity/config/PnP signals, and is
revalidated with selected GPU under the runtime lock before one hardware write.
Explicit Apply/Reset, logoff/supersession, lockout, stop, or a terminal write
ends the generation; a real hardware write is never retried. Ordinary startup,
install/repair, Task Manager kill, and snapshot-only SCM restart are
non-mutating; the former boot-reconcile/start markers were removed. Fast Startup
and autologon use the real task event.

Power/recovery policy is now explicit:

- suspend arms a generation; duplicate resumes coalesce and restore the complete
  in-memory curve/offset/power/lock/fan intent once with no 10-minute gate;
- confirmed selected-GPU recovery dominates coincident standby and waits for a
  current-boot proof measured by `QueryUnbiasedInterruptTime`, so sleep does not
  advance the 10-minute window;
- recovery evidence is deduplicated in one protected ledger, automatic success
  never clears history, and sticky lockout can be cleared only by a successful
  explicit GUI/CLI/hotkey/tray Apply;
- null/global `DBT_DEVNODES_CHANGED` is read-only re-enumeration and cannot
  authorize a write; expected temperature/boost/VF drift remains diagnostic.

Task definitions are classified as canonical, compatible legacy, or broken.
Correct-user/action delayed or `HighestAvailable` tasks continue to work and
are normalized best-effort; disabled/wrong-user/stale-action tasks require
repair. The canonical task is immediate, least privilege, and `PT3M`, with no
repeat-on-failure. Saving `logon_slot`/`logon_shared_slot` is one locked atomic
transaction with readback and is independent of task repair, so repair failure
does not erase the user's choice. Combo synchronization uses item data, and
`applied_slot` follows service profile ownership metadata instead of comparing
temperature-sensitive live VF MHz.

The pure harness now covers task-only profile-2 logon, WTS/task coalescing,
authentication-LUID reuse, logoff cancellation, exactly-one fake write,
prerequisite failpoints, one-shot standby and driver dominance, 9:59/10:00
awake proof, sleep/cross-boot rejection, persistent/deduplicated recovery
evidence, null devnodes, typed origins, executable task-XML fixtures, atomic
config failure, shared combo tags, and drift-safe applied ownership.

The earlier same-day "service-owned automatic apply/restore policy" entry below
documents the superseded intermediate boot-inference/ticket design.

---

## 2026-07-10: Linux daemon group-access onboarding

`--service-install` leaves the Unix socket model unchanged (`0660
root:greencurve`) but now clearly tells users that the installer creates the
admin group without enrolling anyone. Its success output, `--help`, generated
Linux-assets README, and root README instruct the account owner to run
`sudo usermod -aG greencurve "$USER"`, then sign out/in or use `newgrp
greencurve`.

The client preserves the connection `errno`; `EACCES`/`EPERM` now produce that
same remedy, and the TUI/CLI no longer overwrite it with the inaccurate
"daemon not running" message. Regression/source checks and the Linux x64 check
passed (`python build.py --test`, `python build.py --target linux --check`).

The full Windows-inclusive `python build.py` remains blocked by pre-existing,
unrelated dirty-worktree errors in `main_service_sessions.cpp`: missing
`service_has_pending_logon_apply` and `service_cancel_pending_logon_apply`.

---

## 2026-07-10: service-owned automatic apply/restore policy (superseded intermediate design)

The July startup report was traced to a task/service readiness race and to an
unsafe split of automatic-apply ownership. The scheduled `--logon-start` task
now only launches/observes the tray handoff; it cannot load a profile, reset the
GPU, or send an apply request. A service-side deferred coordinator coalesces
session events and retries only WTS identity, profile materialization, and GPU
readiness prerequisites. It makes at most one actual logon GPU apply; a failed
automatic apply is terminal.

Automatic restoration now has a documented safety policy:

- configured per-user/shared logon profile → service applies at real logon (or
  boot-only Fast Startup/autologon reconcile) unless a persistent safety lockout
  is active;
- standby resume → restores the active intended settings immediately unless
  locked out; driver/TDR recovery → requires a prior successful settings apply
  to have survived 10 minutes in the current boot;
- service install/repair/manual/no-snapshot start → non-mutating;
- unstable apply, TDR/restart spam, or a failed automatic reapply → persistent
  `service_auto_restore_lockout.bin`, cleared only by explicit successful Apply;
- a recovery snapshot alone cannot restore after a Task Manager/process kill:
  `service_controlled_recovery.bin` is a short-lived, consumed-once ticket from
  Green Curve's own deliberate driver-recovery exit; without it startup discards
  the snapshot and remains non-mutating;
- expected boost/temperature/VF drift → diagnostic only, never a reapply
  trigger.

Task registration now validates existing task XML and repairs stale delay,
elevation, user/action/path, and instance-policy settings. The desired task is
immediate and `LeastPrivilege`; no Task Scheduler retry is used. See
[automatic restore policy](../auto-restore-policy.md).

Regression coverage in `build.py --test` now exercises the exact 10-minute
boundary and lockout decision, plus source guards for no direct scheduled-logon
apply, no-snapshot GPU reset, and controlled-recovery-ticket gating. `README.md`
now describes the user-visible persistence contract, including Task Manager as
an emergency non-restore stop.

Follow-up clarifications:

- Standby/resume is not TDR evidence and restores current active intent without
  the 10-minute driver-recovery proving gate (the persistent safety lockout
  still dominates).
- Task Scheduler canonicalizes our valid XML by omitting default
  `Enabled=true` and `RunLevel=LeastPrivilege` elements. The verifier now
  accepts those omissions, but still repairs explicit disablement, elevation,
  delay, identity, action, or working-directory drift in tasks created by any
  earlier version.

---

## 2026-07-05 (0.18.1, build 368): tray-pick queue + reliable apply + manual log tag

**Problems fixed:**
1. **Silent drop on re-click:** if a hotkey/tray-pick arrived while `g_apApplyInFlight`
   was true (e.g. auto-switch apply started during `TrackPopupMenu`'s modal loop),
   `ap_do_apply_slot` returned silently at the in-flight guard — no apply, no log.
   **Fix:** `auto_profile_on_hotkey` / `auto_profile_pick_slot` now set
   `g_apPendingPickSlot` when in-flight. `ap_execute` drains the deferred pick
   (full retry through `auto_profile_pick_slot`, which re-enters the controller
   state machine and applies). Latest-wins: only the most recent pending slot is
   kept.

2. **Log always `(auto)`:** `ap_execute` hardcoded `manual=false` for
   `AP_ACTION_APPLY_SLOT`, so tray/hotkey picks were indistinguishable from
   auto-switch in the log. **Fix:** `ap_execute` now takes a `bool manual`
   parameter. `auto_profile_on_hotkey` / `auto_profile_pick_slot` pass `true`.

3. **Silent in-flight guard:** no log when `g_apApplyInFlight` caused a skip.
   **Fix:** added `debug_log` at the guard.

4. **Version bump:** `0.18` → `0.18.1` (VERSION, app_shared.h, linux_port.h fallbacks).

**Changed files:**
- `source/auto_profile_win32.cpp` — `g_apPendingPickSlot`, queue+drain in
  entry points and `ap_execute`, `manual` param, debug logs
- `source/app_shared.h`, `source/linux_port.h` — version fallbacks
- `VERSION` — `0.18.1`

## 2026-07-05 (0.18, build 366): tray checkmark uses `[profiles] applied_slot`, not `selected_slot`

The tray icon context menu checkmark (via `auto_profile_active_slot()`) now reads
`[profiles] applied_slot` instead of `[profiles] selected_slot`. The combo-selection
handler writes `selected_slot` (for editing/saving) but the checkmark must only appear
when a profile is actually applied to the GPU. All apply paths (auto-switch, hotkey,
manual Apply button, app-start apply, logon apply) write `applied_slot`. The slot
deletion path (`clear_profile_from_config`) resets `applied_slot` to 0 if the deleted
slot was the applied slot.

**Changed files:**
- `auto_profile_win32.cpp` — write `applied_slot` in `ap_do_apply_slot()`, read it in
  `auto_profile_active_slot()`
- `config_profiles_ui.cpp` — write `applied_slot` on app-launch + logon apply
- `ui_main_window.cpp` — write `applied_slot` after successful Apply button
- `config_profiles.cpp` — preserve `applied_slot` in save + slot deletion
- `linux_port_profiles.cpp` — preserve `applied_slot` for config parity

## 2026-07-04 (0.18, build 363): removed the continuous VF-drift monitor + auto-reapply (TDR cause)

**A real driver TDR** (VEH stale-handle crash → service restart, 18:56:10) was traced
to the **continuous VF-drift monitor**. `service_check_active_vf_drift_monitor()` polled
every 5s and, on >3 MHz off-target for 2 samples, queued a **full OC reapply** with
`resetOcBeforeApply=true` — reset-to-stock (momentary tail spike to ~3442 MHz, the
`max=ci76/480MHz` transients) then re-write the aggressive +475/flatten curve, **under
game load**. Worse, it fought an **unreachable target**: flatten 2957 vs the driver's
−1000000 kHz floor which bottoms out at **2962** → a permanent 5 MHz "drift" it could
never close, so it reapplied every window forever (**41 reapplies** in the crashed
session; NVML fan writes began failing `NVML_ERROR_OTHER` right before the crash).

**Verdict:** actively "correcting" NVIDIA's expected temperature/boost drift is the wrong
idea (user's instinct confirmed) and materially raised TDR risk.

**Fix (0.18):** deleted `main_service_vf_drift.cpp` and everything that drove it — the
main-loop call (`main_service_server.cpp`), the forward decl + `g_serviceVfDrift*` globals
+ `SERVICE_VF_DRIFT_*` constants (`main.cpp`), the `#include` + GUI stub (`main_shell.cpp`).
Tail-drift telemetry (`main_tail_diagnostics.cpp`) is now **diagnostic only** ("expected
NVIDIA boost/temperature drift — NO reapply"). **Kept** the event-driven reapply worker
(`service_reapply_thread_proc`) used by resume-from-standby / driver-TDR recovery / session
logon — those fire on real OC-wiping events, not a periodic on-target poll. Guarded by
`F-NO-DRIFT-FIGHT` (forbid the monitor call, constants, and shard include) + the updated
tail-diagnostic text check.

**Version → 0.18** (`VERSION` + both `APP_VERSION` header fallbacks in `app_shared.h` /
`linux_port.h`, guarded by `require_app_version_fallback_in_sync`). `python build.py` (all
4 targets) + `--test` green (build 363).

**Open (not blocking):** the 2957-vs-2962 floor gap is now harmless (nothing acts on it),
but a future nicety is to recognize the driver floor at apply time so a below-floor flatten
target is accepted rather than perpetually "off".

## 2026-07-04 (build 357+): auto-profiles + hotkeys + tray/main-window profile picker

New feature (see [auto-profiles](../auto-profiles.md)): automatically switch the
active tuning profile based on the foreground process/window, plus per-slot
global hotkeys and a Profiles picker in the tray submenu and a main-window
"Auto-Profiles..." button.

**Design anchors (from builds 349–356 groundwork):** the ~2.5–3s single-apply
floor + the single-instance serialized service pipe mean the real lever is
**client-side coalescing**, not faster applies. So the whole thing lives in the
resident GUI process on the message thread: an OUTOFCONTEXT
`EVENT_SYSTEM_FOREGROUND` WinEvent hook (event-driven, no polling, no injection)
+ a ~3s backstop timer feed a **pure** resolver + a **pure** debounce/cooldown
controller; applies reuse the existing TDR-safe path with the
`desired_settings_match_active_service_intent()` idempotency skip ("don't
needlessly re-apply"). Manual pin: a hotkey/tray pick pins+applies a slot;
pressing the **same** slot again resumes auto (per user request).

**Anti-cheat:** strictly read-only observation
(`OpenProcess(PROCESS_QUERY_LIMITED_INFORMATION)` for the image name, a toolhelp
presence scan, the OUTOFCONTEXT hook). `F-NO-INJECT` build guard forbids
`WriteProcessMemory`/`CreateRemoteThread`/`VirtualAllocEx` in the detect/driver
files and requires the least-privilege + OUTOFCONTEXT APIs.

**New files:** `auto_profile_rules.{h,cpp}` (pure resolver + INI I/O),
`auto_profile_controller.{h,cpp}` (pure state machine),
`auto_profile_detect.cpp` (Win32 probing), `auto_profile_win32.cpp` (driver),
`hotkeys.{h,cpp}`, `auto_profile_dialog.cpp` (rule editor). Config helpers
`get/set_config_string` added to `config_utils.cpp`.

**Tests:** `F-AUTO-PROFILE` returns 220–267 (resolver matching/order/
require_focus/default, controller coalescing/cooldown/manual-pin, config
round-trip, hotkey parse/format) + `F-NO-INJECT`/`F-AUTO-PROFILE` source guards.
`python build.py` (all 4) + `--test` green. **Live GPU/foreground switching
under game load is not verified here** — needs the user's hardware.

**Deferred:** service-assisted PID→image resolution for elevated games
(unelevated GUI can get access-denied → title/class fallback for now); worker-
thread apply to avoid the ~3s message-pump stall; regex title/class matching.

## 2026-07-04 (build 356): reverted broken `skip_reset_curve_write`; reset-write is load-bearing

**GPU test of build 355 found a real correctness bug** in `skip_reset_curve_write=1`:
switching from a +475 profile to a milder/stock one left **+475 MHz stranded on the
excluded-low points 70-75** (log: `selective offset: point 70 excluded from selective,
hardware applied residual offset 475000 kHz, accepting actual 2797 MHz` … up to point
75 @ 2932). Exactly the "stuck in an undesired state" auto-switching must never do.

**Root cause (corrects the build-355 assumption):** the selective/boost apply is
deliberately **delta-based** for temperature independence (`gpu_backend_apply.cpp:417`
warns absolute boost targets "blow out to 700+ MHz when the cold base curve is
elevated"). Each point is `originalOffset - currentGpuComponent + desiredGpuComponent`;
for **excluded/stock** points both GPU components are 0, so the target collapses to
`originalCurveOffsets[ci]` = *whatever is currently on the point*. The reset-to-zero is
what makes that "current" a clean stock baseline. So the reset is **load-bearing**, not
redundant — my earlier "offset-independent math, reset removable" was wrong for the
selective/boost path (it only holds for explicit curve points).

**Fix (build 356):** removed the `skip_reset_curve_write` gate — the reset-curve-write
always runs when the previous profile had curve offsets. Guarded by `F-APPLY-SPEED`
(`forbid` the skip gate + `require` the unconditional reset + the TDR GPU-offset reset).

**Kept:** `[apply] reset_settle_ms` (default 1000) — the settle reduction is the one
safe single-apply win (~750ms at 250ms; no stale-offset/TDR issue seen in the test).

**Single-apply floor:** ~reset-write 1s (needed) + 2 driver `setControl` ~1s each
(boost + Blackwell correction, immovable) + settle. So a selective+flatten switch can't
go much below ~2.5-3s safely. The real lever for **rapid auto-switching** is therefore
**Tier A coalescing** (skip intermediate profiles, converge to the latest) — NOT further
single-apply surgery. `python build.py` (all 4) + `--test` green (build 356).

---
## 2026-07-04 (build 355): Tier B profile-switch speed levers (gated, default = current behaviour)

Per-phase timings (build 354 instrumentation) confirmed a ~3.8–4.7s switch is four
~1s blocks: **reset+`Sleep(1000)` settle**, an optional **~1s reset-curve-write** (when
the previous profile had curve offsets), the **boost VF batch write ~1s**, and the
**Blackwell tail correction ~1s**. Two are driver `setControl` latency (can't shrink);
two are ours (the settle + the reset-curve-write).

Added two **opt-in** levers in `gpu_backend_apply.cpp`, both defaulting to the exact
current TDR-safe behaviour (zero regression), for the user to A/B on the GPU:
- **`[apply] reset_settle_ms`** (default **1000**, clamped 0–5000): the post-reset TDR
  settle is now tunable. Lowering it trades TDR margin for speed → validate under game
  load. `Sleep()` skipped entirely at 0.
- **`[apply] skip_reset_curve_write`** (default **0**): when 1, skip the ~1s per-point
  VF-curve reset-to-zero (offset math is `base = liveFreq - liveOffset`, offset-
  independent, so it's not needed for correctness). The **TDR-critical GPU-offset reset
  still always runs**; only the per-point curve reset is skipped. EXPERIMENTAL — watch
  for TDR + flatten/selective correctness on rapid A↔B switches while gaming.

Guarded by `F-APPLY-SPEED` (defaults preserved; GPU-offset reset unconditional). The
driver's ~1s/`setControl` (boost + correction) is unchanged. Still pending: **Tier A**
coalescing (the real rapid-auto-switch fix) + possibly collapsing the boost+correction
double-write. `python build.py` (all 4) + `--test` green (build 355).

**Suggested GPU test:** set `reset_settle_ms=250` and/or `skip_reset_curve_write=1` in
`%LOCALAPPDATA%\Green Curve\config.ini` `[apply]`, restart the service, rapid-switch
A↔B under load, confirm no TDR + correct final clocks; compare the new per-phase
`+Nms` timings.

---
## 2026-07-04 (build 354): profile-apply speed — analysis + safe groundwork (Tier A/B pending)

**Goal:** make profile applies faster + robust for future **auto-switching** (rapid
successive switches between hard OC/UV/fan/flatten/pin profiles — must not queue up,
time out, or get stuck). User approved "Tier A + Tier B (with GPU testing)".

**Timing breakdown (from success-but-slow switch logs, ~3.5–4.7s each):** almost all
of it is driver ~1s/`setControl` × 2–3 writes + a deliberate 1s TDR settle:
- reset-before-apply: fast GPU-offset reset + **~1s VF-curve-reset `setControl`** +
  **`Sleep(1000)` TDR settle** (VRM/mem stabilize at stock before aggressive clocks,
  commit 4b225e1) = ~2.1s
- new-curve batch write **~1s** · Blackwell tail correction **~1s** (selective+flatten
  only, conditional) · fan+overhead ~0.5s.

**Concurrency finding:** the service pipe is **single-instance, fully serialized**
(`FILE_FLAG_FIRST_PIPE_INSTANCE`, one request at a time under `g_serviceRuntimeLock`).
No coalescing → rapid auto-switches would queue (~4s each) + client-timeout. This,
not single-apply speed, is the real auto-switching blocker.

**Shipped this build (both safe, no behaviour/TDR change):**
1. **Per-phase apply-duration logging** in `set_last_apply_phase` ("previous phase took
   +Nms") so Tier B experiments are precisely measurable.
2. **Client apply/reset timeout 5000ms → `SERVICE_APPLY_CLIENT_TIMEOUT_MS`=20000ms**
   (`main.cpp`). Reliability, not speed: the 5s timeout was < worst-case apply, so a
   legit heavy apply falsely reported "Timed out during reading service response" AND
   the client disconnected mid-apply → service response write failed (error 232) even
   though the apply SUCCEEDED — a double-apply / inconsistent-view risk under rapid
   switching. A genuinely wedged apply is still caught by the fan-pulse wedge watchdog.

**NOT changed (deferred — most correctness-critical + TDR code, can't GPU-test here):**
- **Tier B** — dropping the ~1s VF-curve-reset (offset math at `gpu_backend_apply.cpp:243`
  is `base = freq - offset`, already offset-independent, so the reset-write isn't needed
  for the math — its role is the TDR settle-at-stock) and/or shortening the `Sleep(1000)`.
  Each ~1s lives in the entangled flatten/selective/HARD/mem apply path; needs a
  carefully-scoped change verified on the user's GPU under game load.
- **Tier A** — latest-wins coalescing + interruptible/superseding applies. Race-sensitive
  service concurrency change; must be designed + unit-tested carefully, staged.

## 2026-07-04 (build 352): slow apply ("Timed out during reading service response") — diagnosis phase

**Report:** applying a selective **+475 MHz** offset (exclude-low 70) + flatten lock
76 popped "Timed out during reading service response". The apply **succeeded**
(`service response APPLY: ok=1`) but the client had already given up: it took ~10 s
and `service_client_apply_desired` uses a **5000 ms** timeout
(`main_service_ipc.cpp:574`); the late response write then failed with error 232
(client gone). **Not** the drift/reset work — `gpu_backend_apply.cpp` is untouched
by builds 350/351.

**Where the ~10 s goes (from the log):** each NVAPI `setControl` VF-curve write is
**~1.0 s** on this Blackwell (the raw `setFunc` call in
`apply_curve_offsets_verified`; verify-reads are ~50 ms). The apply issues ~8 of
them: reset-before-apply curve reset + a fixed **`Sleep(1000)`**
(`gpu_backend_apply.cpp:62`), then a "VF curve batch write" (3 passes + point-127
per-point fallback) **and** a separate "VF curve correction write" (3 passes +
point-127 fallback). Point 127 (the +475 boost tip) never converges in one write, so
it forces the fallback + full correction phase every time. `#writes × ~1 s` = the
cost.

**Decision (user):** the fix is the apply speed, not the timeout (do NOT bump the
5 s). Chosen approach: **diagnose point-127 non-convergence first** before touching
the flatten-correctness convergence code (can't verify flatten from here — needs the
user's GPU).

**This build (352) = instrumentation only, no behaviour change:**
`apply_curve_offsets_verified` (`main_runtime_gpu.cpp`) now logs, per pass and in a
final summary, the **offset-space** gap for any point the driver won't honor exactly:
`wroteOffset` vs `readbackOffset` vs `driverDelta` + `liveFreq`. This reveals whether
the driver snaps/rounds/caps the +475 kHz offset at point 127 (suspected: the VF
point's resulting frequency hits a driver ceiling, so the effective offset is
reported smaller → never == the written value → endless retries). Guarded by
`F-DIAG-OFFSET` in `build.py`.

**Diagnosis confirmed (reproduced log):** `curve batch pass 1 unconverged: ci=127
wroteOffset=475000kHz readbackOffset=0kHz driverDelta=-475000kHz liveFreq=405000kHz`.
Point 127 is a **populated-but-placeholder** VF entry (405 MHz — below the 500 MHz
visible threshold; the real peak is 3157 MHz @ 1240 mV elsewhere). It's marked
editable in `vfMask`, but the driver **pins its offset at exactly 0** no matter what
we write. `apply_curve_offsets_verified` demanded exact convergence, so it retried
point 127 across a 2nd batch pass **and** a per-point fallback — **~2s of a 5.8s
apply** (which is what tipped it over the 5s client timeout). The flatten tail
converged in one pass; the correction loop was NOT the culprit for this config.

**Fix (build 353):** `apply_curve_offsets_verified` now detects driver-refusal (asked
for a non-zero offset, write returned success, readback still exactly 0) and accepts
the point as a **non-offsettable placeholder** — no 2nd pass, no fallback, not counted
unconverged. Accepting is a hardware no-op (the driver was never going to move it),
and it mirrors the pre-existing **verify-time** acceptance already in
`gpu_backend_apply.cpp:686` ("point N hardware refused offset … accepting actual") +
the `actualMHz >= 500` user-relevance gate at line 674. Expected effect: the boost
write drops from 3 passes+fallback to a single pass, cutting ~2s → apply back under
5s for this config. Guarded by `F-OFFSET-REFUSAL` + the existing `F-DIAG-OFFSET`
checks. Does NOT touch the flatten convergence. `python build.py` (all 4) + `--test`
green (build 353).

**Still open (larger configs):** when the flatten tail itself needs the 25-pass
correction loop (the earlier ~10s build-351 log), each correction pass is still a
~1s driver write. If those configs still exceed 5s after this fix, the next lever is
collapsing the batch+correction double-write — a separate, flatten-touching change.

---

## 2026-07-04 (build 351): Reset showed "Custom Curve" fan + re-adopted lock (reset-ordering bug)

**Report:** After Reset, the GUI still showed the fan as "Custom Curve" instead of
Default/Auto. Attributed to the build-350 drift work.

**Verified NOT caused by build 350:** `git show HEAD` for the drift-isolation commit
touched no fan/reset/snapshot logic (only a comment in `main_state_sync.cpp`
contained the word "fan"). Latent bug, exposed by the fan-intent-ownership change
(build 321).

**Root cause (`service_reset_all` in `main_service_runtime.cpp`):** the active-desired
intent was cleared (`g_serviceHasActiveDesired=false` + memset) only AFTER
`refresh_global_state()` + `initialize_gui_fan_settings_from_live_state()` +
`populate_control_state()`. So:
- `initialize_gui_fan_settings_from_live_state()` line 40 set
  `g_app.activeFanMode = current_green_curve_fan_intent_mode()`, which — with the
  stale active-desired still present — returned `FAN_MODE_CURVE`, overwriting the
  `FAN_MODE_AUTO` set by the fan-auto reset step. `populate_control_state()` /
  `populate_service_snapshot()` then reported `fanMode=2`, and the GUI's
  `initialize_gui_fan_settings_from_live_state`/snapshot adoption showed Custom Curve.
- `detect_locked_tail_from_curve()` (via `should_auto_detect_locked_tail_from_live_curve()`)
  saw the stale active-desired lock and *preserved* `g_app.lockedCi=76` instead of
  clearing it, so the RESET snapshot reported `hasLock` and the GUI re-adopted the
  lock (log: "preserving intent ci=76" + "adopted service lock ci=76").

Log evidence: `service response RESET: ok=1 ... fanMode=2`; "detect_locked_tail_from_curve:
live lock detection suppressed; preserving intent ci=76 mhz=2962".

**Fix:** move the active-desired clear to BEFORE `refresh_global_state()` (marker
`F-RESET-INTENT`). Reset = "stop applying anything", so lock/fan intent derivation
must see no intent → fan derives Auto and the lock live-detects stock (none). Fixes
both the fan display and the lock re-adoption.

**Tests:** `require_text` + two `require_order` `F-RESET-INTENT` checks in `build.py`
(clear precedes `refresh_global_state`/fan derivation). Full build (all 4 targets) +
`--test` green (build 351).

**Files:** `main_service_runtime.cpp`, `build.py`, `llm-wiki/fan-control.md`.

---

## 2026-07-04 (build 350): VF boost/temperature drift isolated from intent (display + fan-only apply + save)

**Report:** User profile had explicit pre-tail points 74=2902 / 75=2932 and a
flatten lock at 76=2962. After a game had been running (so the live NVIDIA VF
curve had boost/temperature-drifted), opening the fan-curve dialog, changing one
fan point, and hitting Apply made the GUI show VF point **75 = 2962** (same as the
locked point 76). User's requirement: do **not** fight the boost shift (it's
expected); just make sure it never produces unexpected applied/displayed/saved
values.

**Root cause (two coupled defects, one root):** the GUI used live `g_app.curve[]`
readback (which legitimately drifts) as the source of truth for *owned* VF points.
1. `capture_gui_apply_settings` computed `curveUnchanged` by comparing the editor
   intent against `displayed_curve_mhz(g_app.curve[i])` (live drift). Drift on a
   pre-tail point → `curveUnchanged=false` → the fan-only shortcut was skipped and
   a genuine fan-only change ran a full OC reset-and-reapply.
2. That non-fan-only apply then ran `populate_edits()`, which reseeded every
   pre-tail edit field from live drift and cleared `guiCurvePointExplicit`, so the
   graph/edit (via `displayed_curve_mhz_for_gui_point`) — and any later Save —
   showed/persisted the drifted value. `populate_edits` is also reachable from the
   Refresh button and `g_guiForceFullRefresh`.

**Fix — one drift-free baseline, `g_app.appliedCurveMHz[VF_NUM_POINTS]`:**
populated EXCLUSIVELY from intent (the applied / service-active `DesiredSettings`),
never from live readback (`capture_applied_curve_baseline()` in
`main_runtime_gpu.cpp`). Used as the single source for owned points in three places:
- **Fan-only detection** (`capture_gui_apply_settings`): compares editor vs
  `appliedCurveMHz`, not live readback. Baseline `0` (newly loaded/typed point)
  still forces a full apply.
- **Display/repaint** (`populate_edits`): owned points shown from the baseline (and
  marked `guiCurvePointExplicit`), so `displayed_curve_mhz_for_gui_point` (which
  reads the edit field for explicit points) shows intent. Stock/unowned points
  still show live readback. Voltage (mV) is immutable so still read live.
- **Save** is drift-free by consequence: the sparse profile save already writes
  `desired->curvePointMHz` for owned points, and the editor now holds intent — so
  editing only point 76 leaves 74/75 at their saved values (no drift persisted).

Baseline set at: client apply success (`gpu_backend.cpp`, only when `!fanOnlyApply`
so fan-only never drops the curve the service holds), service active-desired
adoption (`apply_service_desired_to_gui`), and startup (`entry.cpp` →
`apply_service_desired_to_gui`). Cleared on Reset (`reset_curve`).
`refresh_service_snapshot_and_active_desired` still (RC7) does NOT push
active-desired into the GUI, so the baseline persists across the Apply-time refresh.

**Deliberately NOT changed:** no GPU writes to counteract drift. Excessive real
drift stays owned by the service monitor (`service_check_active_vf_drift_monitor`).
This is intent-isolation, not drift-suppression.

**Tests:** 9 `require_text` source invariants under `F-DRIFT-1` in `build.py`
(baseline field exists; baseline fed from `desired->curvePointMHz`; fan-only compare
uses the baseline; fan-only apply preserves it; active-desired adoption + populate
+ reset wired). `python build.py` (all 4 targets) + `--test` green (build 350).

**Files:** `app_shared.h`, `main.cpp` (fwd decl), `main_runtime_gpu.cpp`,
`gpu_backend.cpp`, `main_state_sync.cpp`, `ui_main.cpp`, `ui_main_window.cpp`,
`build.py`.

## 2026-07-03 (0.17.1, build 349): Linux TUI mouse-offset fix + keyboard nav + live apply; title-bar version drift

**Report (Linux TUI mouse offset):** a Wayland-session user reported TUI button clicks
land increasingly *below* the visible button the further down the screen — first
row fine, growing drift downward. Identical in gnome-terminal and Alacritty, and
independent of display scaling.

**Root cause:** `render_ui` hand-maintained a `y` row counter passed to
`push_button`, incremented separately from the actual `printf("\n")` count. A
stray `y++` on the profile row, a `y += 2` after "Fan fixed pct", and extra
`y++`s at the VF and bottom sections each added a row of drift that accumulated
downward — so registered hitboxes sat 1..4 rows below the drawn buttons. A
secondary X bug: `push_button` used `line->size()` (bytes), so the `°C` glyph
(2 bytes / 1 column) shifted the fan-point `Pct` buttons +1 column.

**Fix:** extracted a pure, POSIX-free `linux_tui_layout.{h,cpp}` that builds the
printed line list **and** the hitboxes in one pass — each hitbox row is
`lines.size()+1` and its X is the UTF-8 display column, so drift is impossible by
construction. Also: left-button-only SGR mouse filter (wheel `button & 64` and
non-left `button & 3` rejected — no phantom clicks from scrolling), full keyboard
navigation (arrows/Tab focus ring + reverse-video highlight, Enter/Space
activate, `g` = apply), live `Apply`/`Reset GPU` buttons calling
`linux_daemon_apply`/`linux_daemon_reset`, dynamic min-size from content, and
info-line clipping so a long path can't wrap and re-introduce drift.

**Tests:** new F-LNX-TUI compiled test (harness `#include`s
`linux_tui_layout.cpp`; asserts each hitbox lands on the drawn bracket at the
right display column) + F-LNX source guards.

**Also (title-bar version):** VERSION was bumped to 0.17.1 but the Windows title
bar still showed v0.17. Cause: the build does **not** inject `-DAPP_VERSION`; the
compiled string comes from the `#ifndef APP_VERSION` fallback in `app_shared.h` /
`linux_port.h`, which were still `"0.17"` (build.py's own `APP_VERSION` only
feeds the PE resource / archive names / NSIS). Bumped both header fallbacks +
build.py default to 0.17.1 and added `require_app_version_fallback_in_sync()` so a
future VERSION bump that forgets the headers fails the build. Verified the
rebuilt `greencurve.exe` embeds `Green Curve v0.17.1`.

**README:** added a prominent note that Windows arm64 / Linux x64 / Linux arm64
are 100% untested/experimental.

Verified: `python build.py --test`, `python build.py --target linux --check`,
full `python build.py` (6 targets, build 349), all pass.

---

## 2026-07-03 (0.17.1, build 348): fix curve not applied after boot until the GUI is opened once (Fast Startup / autologon)

**Report:** User's service was running after boot but the VF curve stayed at
driver defaults in-game; opening the GUI once (no interaction) and closing it
made it apply, reproducibly. Config had `start_program_on_logon=1`,
`apply_on_launch=1`, per-user `logon_slot=1` + a published shared logon default.

**Evidence (from the user's `%LocalAppData%` GUI log):** the manual GUI open at
22:07 logged `adopted service lock ci=107 mhz=1965` (GPU at a default state, not
the configured 1890) and `maybe_load_app_launch_profile_to_gui: ... service
active intent ... profile=1 active=0` — the **service had no active desired
settings**. The GUI's app-launch apply is what programmed the GPU. No
`session change` / `session apply` / logon-start lines existed anywhere in the
log after boot.

**Root cause:** On a no-snapshot boot the startup coordinator was deliberately
non-mutating and applied **only** on catching a *live* `WTS_SESSION_LOGON`. When
the interactive session is already active by the time the service's control
handler registers — **Windows Fast Startup** (hybrid boot restarts the service
fresh; console session resumes already-active), autologon, or a fast SSD — that
event is never delivered, so the service stayed idle forever (no retry). The
per-user startup task (the intended tray/app-launch backstop) also didn't fire.
Diagnosis was hampered because the service starts with debug logging OFF (no user
config readable pre-login), so the logon/boot decision was written nowhere.
Notably a stale comment in `main_service_persist.cpp` already *claimed* the
startup worker reconciles the active user's profile — it didn't (now it does).

**Fix (root-cause, gated so manual starts stay non-mutating):**

1. **Boot reconcile safety net** — no-snapshot **boot auto-start** with an
   already-active session applies that session's logon profile once via the
   existing `service_apply_profile_for_session()` (identical resolve + `{session,
   SID}` debounce). `service_maybe_reconcile_active_session_at_boot()`
   (`main_service_sessions.cpp`), called from the no-snapshot coordinator branch.
2. **Gating** via pure, unit-tested `should_reconcile_active_session_at_boot()`
   (`app_shared.cpp`): a **`--manual`** start (GUI/CLI install/repair/restart
   threads `--manual` from `StartServiceW` → `service_main` → `g_serviceManualStart`)
   stays non-mutating; a persisted **per-boot marker** (`service_boot_reconcile.bin`,
   boot identity = system boot wall-time, `main_service_persist.cpp`) makes it
   at-most-once per boot (SCM crash-restart can't re-drive an unstable apply); the
   restart-loop breaker also suppresses it.
3. **Diagnostics (Fix B)** — `service_session_logon_resolve_and_load_profile()`
   and the boot reconcile now `refresh_service_debug_logging_from_config()` right
   after resolving the user's paths, so the decision + apply land in the user's
   log (honoring the `[debug] enabled` opt-out). Boot decision logged as
   `boot reconcile decision (...)`.

**Tests:** unit cases 160–167 (`should_reconcile_active_session_at_boot`) + source
regression checks (reconcile wiring, `--manual` threading, per-boot marker).
`python build.py --test` and full `python build.py` (all 6 targets) green.

**Corrected misconception:** service logs are NOT in `%ProgramData%` (that holds
only `shared-profiles.ini`); pre-login early logs go to the SYSTEM profile's
`AppData\Local\Green Curve`, and post-impersonation logs go to the active user's
`%LocalAppData%\Green Curve` (same file the GUI writes).

**Follow-up (not done):** the per-user startup task (`--logon-start` → tray → app
apply) also failed to fire in this report; worth a separate look, but the
service-side reconcile now makes auto-apply robust regardless.
